===Original===
> There’s a dark lantern of the spirit,

 

 Which none see by but those who bear it,

 

 That makes them in the dark see visions

 

 And hag themselves with apparitions,

 

 Find racks for their own minds, and vaunt

 

 Of their own misery and want.
> — Samuel Butler

===Chinese===
> 心灵里有一盏遮光的暗灯，
> 唯有提灯的人才借它看清；
> 它教人在黑暗中看见重重异象，
> 又教人拿幢幢鬼影把自己纠缠，
> 为自己的头脑备下刑架，还要
> 把自身的悲惨与匮乏夸个不完。
> ——塞缪尔·巴特勒（Samuel Butler）

===Original===
> **Matthew.** Oh! it’s your only fine humour, sir. Your true melancholy breeds your perfect fine wit, sir. I am melancholy myself, divers times, sir; and then do I no more but take pen and paper presently, and overflow you half a score or a dozen of sonnets at a sitting.
> **Stephen.** Truly, sir, and I love such things out of measure.
> **Matthew.** Why, I pray you, sir, make use of my study: it’s at your service.
> **Stephen.** I thank you, sir, I shall be bold, I warrant you. Have you a stool there, to be melancholy upon?
> — Ben Johnson, *Every Man in His Humour*, Act 3, Scene I

===Chinese===
> **马修.** 哎哟！这可是您独一份的妙气质了，先生。货真价实的忧郁，才养得出十全十美的妙才情呢，先生。我自己也有好几回忧郁来着，先生；每到那时，我便什么也不做，只消即刻抓起纸笔，一次坐定，就给您倾泻出十来首、一打十四行诗。
> **斯蒂芬.** 说真的，先生，我对这类东西爱得没边儿了。
> **马修.** 嗐，我说先生，您何必客气，尽管用我的书房：随时为您效劳。
> **斯蒂芬.** 多谢您了，先生，那我就不客气了，准定叨光。您那儿可有一张凳子，好坐在上头发忧郁？
> ——本·琼森（Ben Johnson），《人人扫兴》（*Every Man in His Humour*），第3幕，第I场

===Original===
> Ay esleu gazouiller et siffler oye, comme dit le commun proverbe, entre les cygnes, plutoust que d’estre entre tant de gentils poëtes et faconds orateurs mut du tout estimé.
> — Rabelais, *Prol.* L. 5

===Chinese===
> Ay esleu gazouiller et siffler oye, comme dit le commun proverbe, entre les cygnes, plutoust que d’estre entre tant de gentils poëtes et faconds orateurs mut du tout estimé.（我宁愿像那句俗话说的，在天鹅群里做一只嘎嘎乱叫、嘶嘶乱鸣的呆鹅，也不愿在这么多高雅的诗人和雄辩的演说家中间落得个全然无人看重。）
> ——拉伯雷（Rabelais），《第五卷序言》（*Prol.* L. 5）

## I / 第一章

===Original===
Nightmare Abbey, a venerable family-mansion, in a highly picturesque state of semi-dilapidation, pleasantly situated on a strip of dry land between the sea and the fens, at the verge of the county of Lincoln, had the honour to be the seat of Christopher Glowry, Esquire. This gentleman was naturally of an atrabilarious temperament, and much troubled with those phantoms of indigestion which are commonly called “blue devils.” He had been deceived in an early friendship: he had been crossed in love; and had offered his hand, from pique, to a lady, who accepted it from interest, and who, in so doing, violently tore asunder the bonds of a tried and youthful attachment. Her vanity was gratified by being the mistress of a very extensive, if not very lively, establishment; but all the springs of her sympathies were frozen. Riches she possessed, but that which enriches them, the participation of affection, was wanting. All that they could purchase for her became indifferent to her, because that which they could not purchase, and which was more valuable than themselves, she had, for their sake, thrown away. She discovered, when it was too late, that she had mistaken the means for the end—that riches, rightly used, are instruments of happiness, but are not in themselves happiness. In this wilful blight of her affections, she found them valueless as means: they had been the end to which she had immolated all her affections, and were now the only end that remained to her. She did not confess this to herself as a principle of action, but it operated through the medium of unconscious self-deception, and terminated in inveterate avarice. She laid on external things the blame of her mind’s internal disorder, and thus became by degrees an accomplished scold. She often went her daily rounds through a series of deserted apartments, every creature in the house vanishing at the creak of her shoe, much more at the sound of her voice, to which the nature of things affords no simile; for, as far as the voice of woman, when attuned by gentleness and love, transcends all other sounds in harmony, so far does it surpass all others in discord, when stretched into unnatural shrillness by anger and impatience.

===Chinese===
噩梦隐修院（Nightmare Abbey），一座年高德劭的世家宅邸，正处于半圮未圮、极尽如画的状态，怡然坐落在林肯郡（Lincoln）边缘一条夹在大海与沼泽地之间的干地上，有幸充作克里斯托弗·格洛里乡绅（Christopher Glowry, Esquire）的府邸。这位绅士生来一副忧郁质（atrabilarious）的脾性，又被那些消化不良招来的鬼影——通称「蓝魔」的忧郁症（blue devils）——苦苦纠缠。他早年交友受了骗；恋爱又遭了挫；于是一赌气，向一位小姐递上手去；那小姐出于利害应允了他，并在应允之际，生生扯断了一段久经考验的少年深情。做一所宏大——虽说毫不热闹——的宅门的主妇，她的虚荣心得了满足；可她一切同情的泉眼都冻住了。财富她有的是，唯独缺那让财富生辉的东西——恩爱的分享。财富替她买来的一切，她都无所谓了；因为那件买不来的、比财富本身更贵重的东西，她恰恰为了财富给扔掉了。待她发觉——发觉时已经太迟——自己把手段错当成了目的：财富用得其所，是幸福的工具，本身却不是幸福。在这场任性作践出来的感情枯萎里，她发现财富连当手段也不值了：她原是把全部感情统统祭献给财富这个目的的，如今这目的成了她仅剩的唯一目的。这一层，她从不曾向自己招认、奉为行事准则，它却借着无意识的自我欺骗暗暗运作，终至养成根深蒂固的吝啬。她把自己内心失序的罪责推到外物头上，于是渐渐修炼成一位炉火纯青的骂婆。她常常每日例行巡行，穿过一连串人去屋空的房间：宅里一切生灵，听得她鞋跟一响便无影无踪，听得她的嗓音——对此，万物本性中找不出任何比方——更是逃之夭夭；因为女人的嗓音，经温柔与爱调准之后，其和谐胜过世间一切声响多少，那么经愤怒与烦躁绷成反常尖利之后，其刺耳也就盖过世间一切声响多少。

===Original===
Mr. Glowry used to say that his house was no better than a spacious kennel, for everyone in it led the life of a dog. Disappointed both in love and in friendship, and looking upon human learning as vanity, he had come to a conclusion that there was but one good thing in the world, *videlicet*, a good dinner; and this his parsimonious lady seldom suffered him to enjoy: but, one morning, like Sir Leoline in Christabel, “he woke and found his lady dead,” and remained a very consolate widower, with one small child.

This only son and heir Mr. Glowry had christened Scythrop, from the name of a maternal ancestor, who had hanged himself one rainy day in a fit of *toedium vitae*, and had been eulogised by a coroner’s jury in the comprehensive phrase of *felo de se*; on which account, Mr. Glowry held his memory in high honour, and made a punchbowl of his skull.

===Chinese===
格洛里先生常说，他这宅子不过是间宽敞的狗窝，因为宅里人人过的都是狗日子。爱情与友谊双双失意，又把人间学问一概看作虚妄，他终于得出一个结论：世上好东西只有一样，即（videlicet），一顿好饭；而这顿好饭，他那吝啬的太太难得让他享用。可是，一天早晨，就像《克丽斯特贝尔》（*Christabel*）里的利奥莱恩爵士（Sir Leoline），「他一觉醒来，发现太太死了」，遂做了个万分宽慰的鳏夫，身边留下一个年幼的孩子。

格洛里给这独子兼继承人施洗命名，唤作斯克罗普（Scythrop），用的是一位母系先祖的大名；那位先祖某个雨天里一阵 *toedium vitae*（厌倦人生）发作，自缢了，验尸陪审团用一条概括万方的短语表彰了他：*felo de se*（自戕罪）。格洛里先生因此对这位先祖追念弥笃，还拿他的脑壳做了一只潘趣酒碗。

===Original===
When Scythrop grew up, he was sent, as usual, to a public school, where a little learning was painfully beaten into him, and from thence to the university, where it was carefully taken out of him; and he was sent home like a well-threshed ear of corn, with nothing in his head: having finished his education to the high satisfaction of the master and fellows of his college, who had, in testimony of their approbation, presented him with a silver fish-slice, on which his name figured at the head of a laudatory inscription in some semi-barbarous dialect of Anglo-Saxonised Latin.

His fellow-students, however, who drove tandem and random in great perfection, and were connoisseurs in good inns, had taught him to drink deep ere he departed. He had passed much of his time with these choice spirits, and had seen the rays of the midnight lamp tremble on many a lengthening file of empty bottles. He passed his vacations sometimes at Nightmare Abbey, sometimes in London, at the house of his uncle, Mr. Hilary, a very cheerful and elastic gentleman, who had married the sister of the melancholy Mr. Glowry. The company that frequented his house was the gayest of the gay. Scythrop danced with the ladies and drank with the gentlemen, and was pronounced by both a very accomplished charming fellow, and an honour to the university.

===Chinese===
斯克罗普长大后，照例被送进公学，在那里，一小点学问被痛打进他的脑袋；随后进了大学，又在那里被小心翼翼地取了出来；于是他被遣送回家，像一穗脱粒净尽的玉米，脑袋里空空如也：他的学业至此圆满完成，深惬学院院长与评议员诸公之意；诸公为表嘉许，还赠他一柄银质分鱼刀，刀上刻着颂词，他的名字冠于其首，所用文字乃某种半蛮化的、盎格鲁-撒克逊味的拉丁方言。

不过，他那一班同窗——个个能把一犋串联马赶得横冲直撞、技进乎道，又都是品鉴上好客栈的行家——早在他离校之前，就教会了他痛饮。他同这班精选的酒中豪杰厮混了大把时光，亲眼看着午夜孤灯的光晕，颤颤地映在一列列越排越长的空酒瓶上。他的假期有时在噩梦隐修院度过，有时在伦敦他姨父希拉里先生（Mr. Hilary）府上；希拉里先生是位极快活、极有弹性的绅士，娶的正是忧郁的格洛里先生的妹妹。他府上往来的宾客，是人尖里的人尖。斯克罗普同女士们跳舞，同先生们喝酒，两边一致评定：他是位多才多艺的可爱人物，为大学增光。

===Original===
At the house of Mr. Hilary, Scythrop first saw the beautiful Miss Emily Girouette. He fell in love; which is nothing new. He was favourably received; which is nothing strange. Mr. Glowry and Mr. Girouette had a meeting on the occasion, and quarrelled about the terms of the bargain; which is neither new nor strange. The lovers were torn asunder, weeping and vowing everlasting constancy; and, in three weeks after this tragical event, the lady was led a smiling bride to the altar, by the Honourable Mr. Lackwit; which is neither strange nor new.

Scythrop received this intelligence at Nightmare Abbey, and was half distracted on the occasion. It was his first disappointment, and preyed deeply on his sensitive spirit. His father, to comfort him, read him a Commentary on Ecclesiastes, which he had himself composed, and which demonstrated incontrovertibly that all is vanity. He insisted particularly on the text, “One man among a thousand have I found, but a woman amongst all those have I not found.”

===Chinese===
在希拉里先生府上，斯克罗普初见美丽的埃米莉·吉鲁埃特小姐（Emily Girouette）。他坠入了情网；这不算新鲜。他蒙受了青眼；这不算离奇。格洛里先生与吉鲁埃特先生为此事会了一面，为交易的条件吵了一架；这既不新鲜也不离奇。一对恋人被生生拆散，洒泪盟誓永守忠贞；这场惨剧过后三个星期，小姐便笑吟吟地被牵上圣坛，做了尊贵的拉克维特先生（the Honourable Mr. Lackwit）的新娘；这既不离奇也不新鲜。

斯克罗普在噩梦隐修院得到这消息，登时半疯了似的。这是他头一回落空，狠狠啃噬着他那颗敏感的心灵。他父亲为安慰他，给他诵读了一部《传道书》注释——他父亲亲笔所著——其中无可辩驳地证明：凡事都是虚空。他尤其着力发挥这一节经文：「千人之中我寻得一个，众女子之中却一个也未寻得。」

===Original===
“How could he expect it,” said Scythrop, “when the whole thousand were locked up in his seraglio? His experience is no precedent for a free state of society like that in which we live.”

“Locked up or at large,” said Mr. Glowry, “the result is the same: their minds are always locked up, and vanity and interest keep the key. I speak feelingly, Scythrop.”

“I am sorry for it, sir,” said Scythrop. “But how is it that their minds are locked up? The fault is in their artificial education, which studiously models them into mere musical dolls, to be set out for sale in the great toyshop of society.”

===Chinese===
「他还能指望什么呢，」斯克罗普说，「那一千个女人全锁在他的后宫里？他的经验，在咱们这种自由社会里，可算不得先例。」

「锁着也好，放出来也好，」格洛里先生说，「结果一个样：她们的心智永远是锁着的，钥匙攥在虚荣和利害手里。我是有切身体会的，斯克罗普。」

「这一点我很遗憾，先生，」斯克罗普说。「可她们的心智怎么会是锁着的？错就错在那套矫揉造作的教育，刻意把她们塑造成一只只只会奏乐的玩偶，摆进社会这爿大玩具铺里标价出售。」

===Original===
“To be sure,” said Mr. Glowry, “their education is not so well finished as yours has been; and your idea of a musical doll is good. I bought one myself, but it was confoundedly out of tune; but, whatever be the cause, Scythrop, the effect is certainly this, that one is pretty nearly as good as another, as far as any judgment can be formed of them before marriage. It is only after marriage that they show their true qualities, as I know by bitter experience. Marriage is, therefore, a lottery, and the less choice and selection a man bestows on his ticket the better; for, if he has incurred considerable pains and expense to obtain a lucky number, and his lucky number proves a blank, he experiences not a simple, but a complicated disappointment; the loss of labour and money being superadded to the disappointment of drawing a blank, which, constituting simply and entirely the grievance of him who has chosen his ticket at random, is, from its simplicity, the more endurable.” This very excellent reasoning was thrown away upon Scythrop, who retired to his tower as dismal and disconsolate as before.

===Chinese===
「可不是嘛，」格洛里先生说，「她们受的教育，收尾的功夫不如你那套来得到家；你这个音乐玩偶的比方，出得好。我自己就买过一只，可惜该死地走调。不过呢，斯克罗普，不管病根在哪里，病征准是这样：婚前凭任何眼力去判断，这一个同那一个简直半斤八两。只有到了婚后，她们才显出真成色，这一点我是拿血泪经验换来的。所以婚姻是张彩票，买彩票时挑得越少、拣得越少越好；因为，倘若一个人费了老大的心思和钱财去弄一个吉利号码，而这吉利号码到头开出来是张白票，那他尝到的就不是一重、而是叠床架屋的好几重失望：劳力与钱财的损失，压在抽中白票的失望之上；至于随手乱抓一张的人，他的委屈单单纯纯、通通统统就只是那张白票本身，正因其单纯，反倒好受些。」这番绝顶高明的推理，在斯克罗普身上全然白费；他退回自己的塔楼，同先前一样凄凄惶惶。

===Original===
The tower which Scythrop inhabited stood at the southeastern angle of the Abbey; and, on the southern side, the foot of the tower opened on a terrace, which was called the garden, though nothing grew on it but ivy, and a few amphibious weeds. The southwestern tower, which was ruinous and full of owls, might, with equal propriety, have been called the aviary. This terrace or garden, or terrace-garden, or garden-terrace (the reader may name it *ad libitum*), took in an oblique view of the open sea, and fronted a long tract of level seacoast, and a fine monotony of fens and windmills.

The reader will judge, from what we have said, that this building was a sort of castellated abbey; and it will, probably, occur to him to inquire if it had been one of the strongholds of the ancient church militant. Whether this was the case, or how far it had been indebted to the taste of Mr. Glowry’s ancestors for any transmutations from its original state, are, unfortunately, circumstances not within the pale of our knowledge.

===Chinese===
斯克罗普住的那座塔，踞于宅邸的东南角；塔的南脚下敞着一片台地，人称作花园，虽然上面除了常春藤和几茎水陆两栖的野草，什么也不长。西南那座塔倾颓破败，满塔猫头鹰，称之为鸟舍，亦属名正言顺。这片台地也好，花园也好，台地花园、花园台地也好（名称悉听尊便，*ad libitum*），斜斜望去可纳入一片开阔的海，正对着一带平直的海岸，以及一派精美的、千篇一律的沼泽和风车。

读者自会据上文判定：这座建筑乃是某种带城堡气派的隐修院；他多半还会起意追问：它可曾是古代战斗教会的一座堡垒？事实是否如此，或者它从原初格局到眼前模样，亏欠格洛里先生历代先祖的趣味几多，可惜都属我等知识范围之外的情由。

===Original===
The northwestern tower contained the apartments of Mr. Glowry. The moat at its base, and the fens beyond, comprised the whole of his prospect. This moat surrounded the Abbey, and was in immediate contact with the walls on every side but the south.

The northeastern tower was appropriated to the domestics, whom Mr. Glowry always chose by one of two criterions—a long face, or a dismal name. His butler was Raven; his steward was Crow; his valet was Skellet. Mr. Glowry maintained that the valet was of French extraction, and that his name was Squelette. His grooms were Mattocks and Graves. On one occasion, being in want of a footman, he received a letter from a person signing himself Diggory Deathshead, and lost no time in securing this acquisition; but on Diggory’s arrival, Mr. Glowry was horror-struck by the sight of a round ruddy face, and a pair of laughing eyes. Deathshead was always grinning—not a ghastly smile, but the grin of a comic mask; and disturbed the echoes of the hall with so much unhallowed laughter, that Mr. Glowry gave him his discharge. Diggory, however, had stayed long enough to make conquests of all the old gentleman’s maids, and left him a flourishing colony of young Deathsheads to join chorus with the owls, that had before been the exclusive choristers of Nightmare Abbey.

===Chinese===
西北塔里是格洛里先生的居所。塔脚下的护宅河，连同河外的沼泽，构成他凭窗眺望的全部景观。这条护宅河环绕整座宅邸，除南面外，处处紧贴着院墙。

东北塔拨给仆役居住；格洛里先生挑仆人，永远只用两条标准之一——一张长脸，或一个丧气的名字。他的酒膳总管是雷文（Raven）；他的田庄管事是克罗（Crow）；他的贴身男仆是斯凯雷特（Skellet）——格洛里先生一口咬定，此仆系法国血统，名字本作斯克雷特（Squelette，法语「骷髅」）。他的马夫是马托克斯（Mattocks）和格雷夫斯（Graves，「坟墓」）。有一回缺一名听差，他收到一封信，署名迪戈里·死人头（Diggory Deathshead），当即火速把这份奇货锁定了下来；不料迪戈里一到，格洛里先生骇然望见一张红扑扑的圆脸、一双笑吟吟的眼睛。死人头先生脸上永远挂着咧开的笑——不是阴森森的浅笑，而是喜剧面具那种咧法；他那些不成体统的狂笑，搅得门厅里的回声不得安生，格洛里先生只得打发他走人。不过迪戈里待的日子也够长久了，把老先生的使女们尽数掳获了一遍，给他留下一窝生机蓬勃的小死人头，加入猫头鹰的合唱——在那之前，猫头鹰可是噩梦隐修院独家承包的唱诗班。

===Original===
The main body of the building was divided into rooms of state, spacious apartments for feasting, and numerous bedrooms for visitors, who, however, were few and far between.

Family interests compelled Mr. Glowry to receive occasional visits from Mr. and Mrs. Hilary, who paid them from the same motive; and, as the lively gentleman on these occasions found few conductors for his exuberant gaiety, he became like a double-charged electric jar, which often exploded in some burst of outrageous merriment to the signal discomposure of Mr. Glowry’s nerves.

===Chinese===
宅邸的主体建筑，隔成了气派的礼仪厅堂、宽敞的宴客厅室，还有许许多多客房；只是访客稀稀落落，少得可怜。

家族利害逼得格洛里先生不得不偶尔接待希拉里先生伉俪的来访——他们来访也是出于同一动机。每逢这种场合，这位快活绅士的过剩欢乐总是寻不到几条导线可走，于是便活像一只充了双倍电的莱顿瓶，动不动就炸出一声骇人的哄笑，把格洛里先生的神经震得七零八落。

===Original===
Another occasional visitor, much more to Mr. Glowry’s taste, was Mr. Flosky,1 a very lachrymose and morbid gentleman, of some note in the literary world, but in his own estimation of much more merit than name. The part of his character which recommended him to Mr. Glowry was his very fine sense of the grim and the tearful. No one could relate a dismal story with so many minutiae of supererogatory wretchedness. No one could call up a “raw-head and bloody-bones” with so many adjuncts and circumstances of ghastliness. Mystery was his mental element. He lived in the midst of that visionary world in which nothing is but what is not. He dreamed with his eyes open, and saw ghosts dancing round him at noontide. He had been in his youth an enthusiast for liberty, and had hailed the dawn of the French Revolution as the promise of a day that was to banish war and slavery, and every form of vice and misery, from the face of the earth. Because all this was not done, he deduced that nothing was done; and from this deduction, according to his system of logic, he drew a conclusion that worse than nothing was done; that the overthrow of the feudal fortresses of tyranny and superstition was the greatest calamity that had ever befallen mankind; and that their only hope now was to rake the rubbish together, and rebuild it without any of those loopholes by which the light had originally crept in. To qualify himself for a coadjutor in this laudable task, he plunged into the central opacity of Kantian metaphysics, and lay perdu several years in transcendental darkness, till the common daylight of common sense became intolerable to his eyes. He called the sun an *ignis fatuus*; and exhorted all who would listen to his friendly voice, which were about as many as called “God save King Richard,” to shelter themselves from its delusive radiance in the obscure haunt of Old Philosophy. This word “old” had great charms for him. The good old times were always on his lips; meaning the days when polemic theology was in its prime, and rival prelates beat the drum ecclesiastic with Herculean vigour, till the one wound up his series of syllogisms with the very orthodox conclusion of roasting the other.

===Chinese===
另一位偶一来访的客人，就称合格洛里先生的口味多了：弗洛斯基先生（Mr. Flosky）1，一位泪汪汪、病恹恹的绅士，在文坛小有名气，而据他自己的估价，是才远大于名。他性格中最讨格洛里先生欢心的一点，是对阴惨与垂泪之事的绝妙敏感。没有人能把一个惨淡的故事，讲出那么多节外生枝的苦难细节。没有人能唤出一只「青头血骨怪」（raw-head and bloody-bones），还给它配上那么多阴气森森的机关布景。神秘是他心智的本元素。他住在那个幻视的世界正中央——在那世界里，凡是存在的，皆是不存在的。他睁着眼睛做梦，正午时分也看得见群鬼绕身起舞。他青年时代曾是自由的狂热信徒，曾把法国大革命的黎明，当作一日晴光的许诺来欢呼——那一日，将把战争与奴役，连同一切形态的罪恶与苦难，从大地上驱逐净尽。因为这些没有件件办到，他推论出：什么也没办到；再循着他那套逻辑体系，从这一推论又推出一条结论：办到的比什么都没有还糟；推翻专制与迷信的封建堡垒，乃是人类空前的浩劫；人类如今唯一的指望，就是把那堆瓦砾重新搂拢到一起，再造起来，而且这一回，原先让光悄悄漏进来的那些射孔，一个也不许留。为把自己历练成这项可敬大业的助手，他一头扎进康德形而上学那不透光的中心，在先验的黑暗里潜伏经年，直到常识的寻常日光变得叫他两眼无法忍受。他把太阳唤作 *ignis fatuus*（鬼火）；还劝告一切愿意听他友好忠告的人——人数约莫同高呼「上帝保佑理查王」的一样多——快躲进旧哲学那幽暗的巢穴里，避一避日光那骗人的光辉。「旧」这个字眼于他有莫大的魅力。「美好的旧时光」永远挂在他嘴边；他所指的，是论战神学如日中天的年代——争锋的主教们各以赫拉克勒斯般的膂力擂响教会的战鼓，直到其中一位，用一条再正统不过的结论给他的三段论系列收了尾：把另一位架到火刑柱上烤了。

===Original===
But the dearest friend of Mr. Glowry, and his most welcome guest, was Mr. Toobad, the Manichaean Millenarian. The twelfth verse of the twelfth chapter of Revelations was always in his mouth: “Woe to the inhabiters of the earth and of the sea! for the devil is come among you, having great wrath, because he knoweth that he hath but a short time.” He maintained that the supreme dominion of the world was, for wise purposes, given over for a while to the Evil Principle; and that this precise period of time, commonly called the enlightened age, was the point of his plenitude of power. He used to add that by and by he would be cast down, and a high and happy order of things succeed; but he never omitted the saving clause, “Not in our time”; which last words were always echoed in doleful response by the sympathetic Mr. Glowry.

===Chinese===
而格洛里先生最亲爱的朋友、最受他欢迎的客人，是图巴德先生（Mr. Toobad），一位摩尼教派的千禧年论者。《启示录》第十二章第十二节永远挂在他嘴边：「地与海的居民有祸了！因为魔鬼已来到你们中间，怒气冲冲，因为他知道自己的时候不多了。」他坚称：世界的最高统治权，出于明智的安排，暂时交付给了恶的原则；而眼下这个时段——俗称启蒙时代——恰是那恶原则权力登峰造极之点。他还惯常补充：早晚有一天，恶的原则会被打下去，继之而起的是一种高尚而幸福的秩序；只是他从不漏掉那句但书——「只是等不到咱们那时候」；而这最后几个字，每次都由同气相求的格洛里先生哀声应和。

===Original===
Another and very frequent visitor, was the Reverend Mr. Larynx, the vicar of Claydyke, a village about ten miles distant;—a good-natured accommodating divine, who was always most obligingly ready to take a dinner and a bed at the house of any country gentleman in distress for a companion. Nothing came amiss to him—a game at billiards, at chess, at draughts, at backgammon, at piquet, or at all-fours in a tête-à-tête—or any game on the cards, round, square, or triangular, in a party of any number exceeding two. He would even dance among friends, rather than that a lady, even if she were on the wrong side of thirty, should sit still for want of a partner. For a ride, a walk, or a sail, in the morning—a song after dinner, a ghost story after supper—a bottle of port with the squire, or a cup of green tea with his lady—for all or any of these, or for anything else that was agreeable to anyone else, consistently with the dye of his coat, the Reverend Mr. Larynx was at all times equally ready. When at Nightmare Abbey, he would condole with Mr. Glowry—drink Madeira with Scythrop—crack jokes with Mr. Hilary—hand Mrs. Hilary to the piano, take charge of her fan and gloves, and turn over her music with surprising dexterity—quote Revelations with Mr. Toobad—and lament the good old times of feudal darkness with the transcendental Mr. Flosky.

===Chinese===
另一位来得极勤的客人，是拉林克斯牧师（the Reverend Mr. Larynx），克莱迪克村——一个约摸十英里外的村子——的教区牧师；这是位和蔼可亲、随方就圆的圣职人员，无论哪位乡绅老爷苦于无人作伴，他总是最殷勤地乐于在那府上领受一顿晚饭、一张床铺。天下没有他不接的招：单人对坐，可以来台球、象棋、西洋跳棋、十五子棋，或者一局皮克牌（piquet）、全四牌（all-fours）——或者人手超过两名，随便什么纸牌戏，圆的方的三角的，一概奉陪。逢着朋友圈子里，他甚至连舞都肯跳——免得哪位小姐，哪怕早过了三十岁的坎儿，落得独坐一隅没有舞伴。清晨骑一回马、散一回步、泛一回舟；饭后唱一支歌，夜宵后讲一个鬼故事；同老爷对饮一瓶波特酒，或者陪夫人喝一杯绿茶——凡此种种，或任意一项，或任何旁人觉得有趣、又不至于染坏他那身道袍颜色的勾当，拉林克斯牧师无时不刻一律随时伺候。在噩梦隐修院做客时，他向格洛里先生吊愁肠——同斯克罗普喝马德拉酒——同希拉里先生打趣逗乐——搀希拉里太太（Mrs. Hilary）到钢琴前，替她掌扇、管手套，翻乐谱的手指灵巧得惊人——同图巴德先生互引《启示录》——再同那位先验论的弗洛斯基先生一道，痛悼封建黑暗时代的美好旧时光。

## II / 第二章

===Original===
Shortly after the disastrous termination of Scythrop’s passion for Miss Emily Girouette, Mr. Glowry found himself, much against his will, involved in a lawsuit, which compelled him to dance attendance on the High Court of Chancery. Scythrop was left alone at Nightmare Abbey. He was a burnt child, and dreaded the fire of female eyes. He wandered about the ample pile, or along the garden-terrace, with “his cogitative faculties immersed in cogibundity of cogitation.” The terrace terminated at the southwestern tower, which, as we have said, was ruinous and full of owls. Here would Scythrop take his evening seat, on a fallen fragment of mossy stone, with his back resting against the ruined wall—a thick canopy of ivy, with an owl in it, over his head—and the *Sorrows of Werther* in his hand. He had some taste for romance reading before he went to the university, where, we must confess, in justice to his college, he was cured of the love of reading in all its shapes; and the cure would have been radical, if disappointment in love, and total solitude, had not conspired to bring on a relapse. He began to devour romances and German tragedies, and, by the recommendation of Mr. Flosky, to pore over ponderous tomes of transcendental philosophy, which reconciled him to the labour of studying them by their mystical jargon and necromantic imagery. In the congenial solitude of Nightmare Abbey, the distempered ideas of metaphysical romance and romantic metaphysics had ample time and space to germinate into a fertile crop of chimeras, which rapidly shot up into vigorous and abundant vegetation.

===Chinese===
斯克罗普对埃米莉·吉鲁埃特小姐的恋情惨淡收场后不久，格洛里先生发现自己极不情愿地卷进了一场官司，只得天天到衡平法院去听候发落。斯克罗普独守噩梦隐修院。他是个被火烫过的孩子，生怕女人眼睛里的火。他在巍峨的宅子里游荡，或沿着台地花园踱步，「他的思维机能，正沉浸于沉思之复沉思的沉思态中」。台地尽于西南塔——我们说过，那塔倾颓破败，满塔猫头鹰。斯克罗普傍晚惯来此处，在一块生了青苔的断石上落座，背靠残墙——头顶是一厚顶常春藤，藤里蹲着一只猫头鹰——手里捧着《维特之烦恼》（*Sorrows of Werther*）。上大学之前，他对传奇小说颇有些兴味；至于大学——为了对他学院公平起见，我们必须承认——把他对一切形态的读书之爱都治好了；这场治疗本可除根，若不是失恋与彻底的孤独合谋，促成一场旧病复发。他开始狼吞虎咽地读传奇和德国悲剧，又经弗洛斯基先生举荐，埋头苦啃先验哲学的大部头；那些书用神秘的行话与招魂摄魄的意象，哄得他甘愿领受这份研读之苦。在噩梦隐修院意气相投的孤独里，玄学传奇与传奇玄学这些失了调的观念，有的是时间与空地，发芽抽条，长成一茬丰产的妄想，转眼便拔节为一片繁茂葱茏。

===Original===
He now became troubled with the *passion for reforming the world*.2 He built many castles in the air, and peopled them with secret tribunals, and bands of illuminati, who were always the imaginary instruments of his projected regeneration of the human species. As he intended to institute a perfect republic, he invested himself with absolute sovereignty over these mystical dispensers of liberty. He slept with horrid mysteries under his pillow, and dreamed of venerable eleutherarchs and ghastly confederates holding midnight conventions in subterranean caves. He passed whole mornings in his study, immersed in gloomy reverie, stalking about the room in his nightcap, which he pulled over his eyes like a cowl, and folding his striped calico dressing-gown about him like the mantle of a conspirator.

===Chinese===
他如今为一种*改造世界的热狂*（passion for reforming the world）2所苦。他在半空里营造了许多城堡，里面住满秘密法庭与一队队光照派（illuminati）——这些人永远是他规划中人类再造大业的假想工具。他既打算缔造一个完美的共和国，便自任这些神秘的自由施与者的绝对君主。他枕着骇人的秘藏入眠，梦见令人敬畏的自由执政官（eleutherarchs）与面色灰败的同党，在地下岩洞里召开午夜大会。他整上午整上午地待在书房里，浸在阴郁的遐想中，戴一顶睡帽在屋里踱方步——睡帽直拉到眼上，活像兜帽——身上裹一条条纹印花布晨衣，恰似阴谋家的大氅。

===Original===
“Action,” thus he soliloquised, “is the result of opinion, and to new-model opinion would be to new-model society. Knowledge is power; it is in the hands of a few, who employ it to mislead the many, for their own selfish purposes of aggrandisement and appropriation. What if it were in the hands of a few who should employ it to lead the many? What if it were universal, and the multitude were enlightened? No. The many must be always in leading-strings; but let them have wise and honest conductors. A few to think, and many to act; that is the only basis of perfect society. So thought the ancient philosophers: they had their esoterical and exoterical doctrines. So thinks the sublime Kant, who delivers his oracles in language which none but the initiated can comprehend. Such were the views of those secret associations of illuminati, which were the terror of superstition and tyranny, and which, carefully selecting wisdom and genius from the great wilderness of society, as the bee selects honey from the flowers of the thorn and the nettle, bound all human excellence in a chain, which, if it had not been prematurely broken, would have commanded opinion, and regenerated the world.”

===Chinese===
「行动，」他这样独白道，「是意见的产物；重新塑造意见，便是重新塑造社会。知识就是力量；它攥在少数人手里，被用来误导多数人，以满足少数人自我膨胀、巧取豪夺的私图。倘若它换攥在另一批少数人手里，被用来引领多数人，又当如何？倘若它遍行天下，芸芸众生尽皆开明，又当如何？不。多数人永远得拴着学步带；但总得给他们配上诚实而贤明的领路人。少数人思考，多数人行动：这是完美社会唯一的基础。古代哲人便是这么想的：他们各有内传与外传两套教义。崇高的康德也是这么想的：他发布神谕所用的语言，唯入门者能解。那些光照派秘密会社的见解亦复如是——它们曾是迷信与暴政闻之丧胆的对象；它们从社会的大荒野里精心拣选智慧与天才，好比蜜蜂从荆棘与荨麻的花朵里采蜜，把人间一切的卓越锁进一条链子；那链子若非过早崩断，本可号令舆论，再造世界。」

===Original===
Scythrop proceeded to meditate on the practicability of reviving a confederation of regenerators. To get a clear view of his own ideas, and to feel the pulse of the wisdom and genius of the age, he wrote and published a treatise, in which his meanings were carefully wrapt up in the monk’s hood of transcendental technology, but filled with hints of matter deep and dangerous, which he thought would set the whole nation in a ferment; and he awaited the result in awful expectation, as a miner who has fired a train awaits the explosion of a rock. However, he listened and heard nothing; for the explosion, if any ensued, was not sufficiently loud to shake a single leaf of the ivy on the towers of Nightmare Abbey; and some months afterwards he received a letter from his bookseller, informing him that only seven copies had been sold, and concluding with a polite request for the balance.

===Chinese===
斯克罗普接着冥想：复兴一个再造者同盟，是否可行。为把自己的主张看个分明，也为了摸一摸这个时代智慧与天才的脉搏，他写了一部论著，付梓刊行；书里他的种种深意，都仔细裹进先验术语的僧兜帽里，内里却满是又深又险的题目的暗示，他以为这足以叫举国鼎沸；于是他怀着肃然的期待等候结果，像点了导火索的矿工，等候岩石炸响。然而他侧耳细听，什么也没听见；因为那爆炸——倘若当真有过——响得尚不足以撼动噩梦隐修院塔上一片常春藤叶；数月之后，他收到书商一封信，通知他统共只卖出去七本，信末还客客气气地请他结清余款。

===Original===
Scythrop did not despair. “Seven copies,” he thought, “have been sold. Seven is a mystical number, and the omen is good. Let me find the seven purchasers of my seven copies, and they shall be the seven golden candlesticks with which I will illuminate the world.”

===Chinese===
斯克罗普并不绝望。「卖掉了七本，」他想，「七是个神秘的数字，兆头是吉的。且让我寻出我那七本书的七位买主，他们就是七座金灯台，我要借他们照亮世界。」

===Original===
Scythrop had a certain portion of mechanical genius, which his romantic projects tended to develop. He constructed models of cells and recesses, sliding panels and secret passages, that would have baffled the skill of the Parisian police. He took the opportunity of his father’s absence to smuggle a dumb carpenter into the Abbey, and between them they gave reality to one of these models in Scythrop’s tower. Scythrop foresaw that a great leader of human regeneration would be involved in fearful dilemmas, and determined, for the benefit of mankind in general, to adopt all possible precautions for the preservation of himself.

===Chinese===
斯克罗普颇有几分机械天分，他那些浪漫的宏图正好把它激发出来。他造出许多暗室暗龛、滑板暗道的模型，其精巧足叫巴黎警察的本事折戟沉沙。他趁父亲不在，把一个哑巴木匠偷运进宅，两人合力，在斯克罗普塔里把其中一件模型变成了现实。斯克罗普预见，人类再造事业的伟大领袖，势必陷入种种可怕的两难；于是为着全人类的福祉，他决计采取一切可能的防范措施，务使自身无虞。

===Original===
The servants, even the women, had been tutored into silence. Profound stillness reigned throughout and around the Abbey, except when the occasional shutting of a door would peal in long reverberations through the galleries, or the heavy tread of the pensive butler would wake the hollow echoes of the hall. Scythrop stalked about like the grand inquisitor, and the servants flitted past him like familiars. In his evening meditations on the terrace, under the ivy of the ruined tower, the only sounds that came to his ear were the rustling of the wind in the ivy, the plaintive voices of the feathered choristers, the owls, the occasional striking of the Abbey clock, and the monotonous dash of the sea on its low and level shore. In the meantime, he drank Madeira, and laid deep schemes for a thorough repair of the crazy fabric of human nature.

===Chinese===
仆人们，连女仆在内，都已被调教得缄口不言。整座宅邸内外，一片深沉的寂静；只有偶尔一扇门关上，长长地回荡过画廊，或是那位愁眉苦脸的酒膳总管沉重的脚步，惊起门厅里空洞的回声。斯克罗普踱来踱去，俨然一位宗教大法官，仆役们则像一群供巫师驱遣的小魔使，从他身边悄没声地掠过。傍晚他在台地上、残塔的常春藤下冥想，入耳的声音只有：风过藤叶的窸窣、羽族唱诗班——猫头鹰们——哀婉的歌喉、宅邸钟楼偶尔的敲响，以及海水拍击低平海岸的单调涛声。这期间，他一边喝着马德拉酒，一边为人性这座摇摇欲坠的大厦，筹划一场彻底的大修。

## III / 第三章

===Original===
Mr. Glowry returned from London with the loss of his lawsuit. Justice was with him, but the law was against him. He found Scythrop in a mood most sympathetically tragic; and they vied with each other in enlivening their cups by lamenting the depravity of this degenerate age, and occasionally interspersing divers grim jokes about graves, worms, and epitaphs. Mr. Glowry’s friends, whom we have mentioned in the first chapter, availed themselves of his return to pay him a simultaneous visit. At the same time arrived Scythrop’s friend and fellow-collegian, the Honourable Mr. Listless. Mr. Glowry had discovered this fashionable young gentleman in London, “stretched on the rack of a too easy chair,” and devoured with a gloomy and misanthropical *nil curo*, and had pressed him so earnestly to take the benefit of the pure country air, at Nightmare Abbey, that Mr. Listless, finding it would give him more trouble to refuse than to comply, summoned his French valet, Fatout, and told him he was going to Lincolnshire. On this simple hint, Fatout went to work, and the imperials were packed, and the post-chariot was at the door, without the Honourable Mr. Listless having said or thought another syllable on the subject.

===Chinese===
格洛里先生从伦敦回来，官司输了个精光。正义在他这一边，法律却在对面。他见斯克罗普正处在一腔极具同病相怜之感的悲剧情绪里；父子俩便你追我赶，借着哀叹这堕落时代的堕落来给杯中之物助兴，间或还点缀几个关于坟墓、蛆虫和墓志铭的冷峭笑话。我们在第一章提过的格洛里先生那几位朋友，趁他回来，一齐登门拜访。与此同时，斯克罗普的大学同窗好友、尊贵的李斯特里斯先生（the Honourable Mr. Listless）也到了。格洛里先生在伦敦发现了这位时髦青年——「直挺挺摊在一张过于柔软的安乐椅刑架上」，被一种阴郁而厌世的 *nil curo*（我全不在乎）啃噬着——便恳切相劝，请他来噩梦隐修院受用受用纯净的乡间空气；李斯特里斯先生盘算下来，拒绝比从命还要费事，便唤来他的法国贴身男仆法图（Fatout），告诉他要动身去林肯郡。得了这简简单单一句话的由头，法图便着手操办一切：车顶行李箱收拾停当，邮车式四轮马车停在了门口，而尊贵的李斯特里斯先生在这件事上，再没说过、也没想过另一个音节。

===Original===
Mr. and Mrs. Hilary brought with them an orphan niece, a daughter of Mr. Glowry’s youngest sister, who had made a runaway love-match with an Irish officer. The lady’s fortune disappeared in the first year: love, by a natural consequence, disappeared in the second: the Irishman himself, by a still more natural consequence, disappeared in the third. Mr. Glowry had allowed his sister an annuity, and she had lived in retirement with her only daughter, whom, at her death, which had recently happened, she commended to the care of Mrs. Hilary.

Miss Marionetta Celestina O’Carroll was a very blooming and accomplished young lady. Being a compound of the *allegro vivace* of the O’Carrolls, and of the *andante doloroso* of the Glowries, she exhibited in her own character all the diversities of an April sky. Her hair was light-brown; her eyes hazel, and sparkling with a mild but fluctuating light; her features regular; her lips full, and of equal size; and her person surpassingly graceful. She was a proficient in music. Her conversation was sprightly, but always on subjects light in their nature and limited in their interest: for moral sympathies, in any general sense, had no place in her mind. She had some coquetry, and more caprice, liking and disliking almost in the same moment; pursuing an object with earnestness while it seemed unattainable, and rejecting it when in her power as not worth the trouble of possession.

===Chinese===
希拉里先生伉俪随身带来一位孤女，是格洛里先生最小的妹妹的女儿；那位妹妹当年跟一个爱尔兰军官私奔成了亲。那位太太的财产，头一年里就消失了：爱情，顺理成章，第二年里也消失了：那位爱尔兰人本人，照更加顺理成章的推论，第三年里也消失了。格洛里先生给妹妹立了一份年金，她便带着独女深居简出；前些时日她去世了，临终把女儿托付给希拉里太太照管。

玛丽奥内塔·塞莱斯蒂娜·奥卡罗尔小姐（Marionetta Celestina O’Carroll）是位容光焕发、多才多艺的少女。她是奥卡罗尔家的 *allegro vivace*（活泼的快板）与格洛里家的 *andante doloroso*（哀伤的行板）的化合物，因而在自己这一副性情里，展览出四月晴空的一切阴晴不定。她的头发是浅褐色；眼睛是榛色的，闪着柔和而游移的光；五官端正；双唇丰润，大小匀停；身段袅娜，超逸绝尘。她精于音律。她的谈吐轻快活泼，但话题永远天生轻浅、兴味有限：因为道德同情——就其任何普遍的意义而言——在她心里没有位置。她有几分卖弄风情，更有几分反复无常，喜爱与厌憎几乎发生在同一瞬；一件东西似乎得不到时，她孜孜以求；一旦到了手，又嫌占有它不值得费那个事，弃之如敝屣。

===Original===
Whether she was touched with a *penchant* for her cousin Scythrop, or was merely curious to see what effect the tender passion would have on so outré a person, she had not been three days in the Abbey before she threw out all the lures of her beauty and accomplishments to make a prize of his heart. Scythrop proved an easy conquest. The image of Miss Emily Girouette was already sufficiently dimmed by the power of philosophy and the exercise of reason: for to these influences, or to any influence but the true one, are usually ascribed the mental cures performed by the great physician Time. Scythrop’s romantic dreams had indeed given him many *pure anticipated cognitions* of combinations of beauty and intelligence, which, he had some misgivings, were not exactly realised in his cousin Marionetta; but, in spite of these misgivings, he soon became distractedly in love; which, when the young lady clearly perceived, she altered her tactics, and assumed as much coldness and reserve as she had before shown ardent and ingenuous attachment. Scythrop was confounded at the sudden change; but, instead of falling at her feet and requesting an explanation, he retreated to his tower, muffled himself in his nightcap, seated himself in the president’s chair of his imaginary secret tribunal, summoned Marionetta with all terrible formalities, frightened her out of her wits, disclosed himself, and clasped the beautiful penitent to his bosom.

===Chinese===
她究竟是对表哥斯克罗普动了 *penchant*（倾心），还是纯粹好奇，想看看温柔的情热在这么一个怪诞人物身上会烧出什么景象——总之，她进宅不满三日，便把美貌与才艺的全部钓饵一齐撒出，要把他那颗心当战利品擒获。斯克罗普果然不堪一攻。埃米莉·吉鲁埃特小姐的倩影，在哲学的威力与理性的操练之下，早已淡得够可以了：因为大医师时间手到病除的种种心疾之愈，人们照例归功于这类影响，或除了真正原因之外的任何影响。斯克罗普的浪漫迷梦，确实曾给予他许多*纯粹的先期认知*（pure anticipated cognitions），关乎美与智的种种组合；他隐约有些疑心，这些组合在表妹玛丽奥内塔身上并未严丝合缝地兑现；但尽管有这些疑心，他很快便神魂颠倒地堕入了情网；小姐把这一切看得分明，随即变了一副战术，摆出十足的高冷与矜持，恰与她先前表露的热烈而天真的依恋等量齐观。斯克罗普被这骤变惊得目瞪口呆；可他没有跪倒在她脚下讨一个解释，反倒退回自己的塔楼，把睡帽往下一裹，坐进他幻想中秘密法庭的庭长高椅，以种种骇人的仪节传唤玛丽奥内塔，把她吓得魂飞魄散，然后亮明身份，把这位美丽的悔罪者一把搂进胸怀。

===Original===
While he was acting this reverie—in the moment in which the awful president of the secret tribunal was throwing back his cowl and his mantle, and discovering himself to the lovely culprit as her adoring and magnanimous lover, the door of the study opened, and the real Marionetta appeared.

The motives which had led her to the tower were a little penitence, a little concern, a little affection, and a little fear as to what the sudden secession of Scythrop, occasioned by her sudden change of manner, might portend. She had tapped several times unheard, and of course unanswered; and at length, timidly and cautiously opening the door, she discovered him standing up before a black velvet chair, which was mounted on an old oak table, in the act of throwing open his striped calico dressing-gown, and flinging away his nightcap—which is what the French call an imposing attitude.

===Chinese===
正当他把这场白日梦演到酣处——恰在秘密法庭那位威严的庭长掀开兜帽和大氅、向可爱的罪人揭示自己原是她倾心爱慕、宽宏大量的情郎的一刻——书房的门开了，货真价实的玛丽奥内塔出现了。

引她上塔来的动机，是一点点悔意、一点点关切、一点点情意，再加上一点点担忧——斯克罗普因她态度突变而突然绝迹，这兆头究竟作何解。她敲了几回门，无人听见，自然也无人应答；末了她怯生生、轻轻地推开门，只见他正站在一把黑丝绒椅子前——那椅子架在一张老橡木桌上——恰在张开条纹印花布晨衣、甩掉睡帽的那个动作之中——此情此景，法国人称之为气势凌人的姿势。

===Original===
Each stood a few moments fixed in their respective places—the lady in astonishment, and the gentleman in confusion. Marionetta was the first to break silence. “For heaven’s sake,” said she, “my dear Scythrop, what is the matter?”

“For heaven’s sake, indeed!” said Scythrop, springing from the table; “for your sake, Marionetta, and you are my heaven—distraction is the matter. I adore you, Marionetta, and your cruelty drives me mad.” He threw himself at her knees, devoured her hand with kisses, and breathed a thousand vows in the most passionate language of romance.

===Chinese===
两人各在各的位置怔了片刻——小姐惊讶，绅士狼狈。玛丽奥内塔先开的口。「看在老天爷份上，」她说，「亲爱的斯克罗普，这是怎么了？」

「可不是看在老天爷份上！」斯克罗普喊道，一跃离桌；「是看在你的份上，玛丽奥内塔，你就是我的老天爷——我怎么的？我疯了。我爱你，玛丽奥内塔，你的残忍把我逼疯了。」他扑倒在她膝前，把她的手吻了个饱，用传奇里最热烈的语言，喘出一千个誓愿。

===Original===
Marionetta listened a long time in silence, till her lover had exhausted his eloquence and paused for a reply. She then said, with a very arch look, “I prithee deliver thyself like a man of this world.” The levity of this quotation, and of the manner in which it was delivered, jarred so discordantly on the high-wrought enthusiasm of the romantic inamorato, that he sprang upon his feet, and beat his forehead with his clenched fist. The young lady was terrified; and, deeming it expedient to soothe him, took one of his hands in hers, placed the other hand on his shoulder, looked up in his face with a winning seriousness, and said, in the tenderest possible tone, “What would you have, Scythrop?”

Scythrop was in heaven again. “What would I have? What but you, Marionetta? You, for the companion of my studies, the partner of my thoughts, the auxiliary of my great designs for the emancipation of mankind.”

===Chinese===
玛丽奥内塔默然听了半晌，直到情郎的雄辩告罄，停下来等回音。她这才摆出一副十分俏皮的神情，说道：「妾请君且像个尘世中人那样说话。」这句引语，连同说它的那副腔调，在这位高烧不退的浪漫情郎昂扬的热忱上刺耳地一撞，他一蹦而起，攥拳直捶自己的额头。小姐吓坏了，觉得还是安抚为上策，便一手握住他一只手，另一只手搭上他的肩，仰起脸望着他，带着动人的郑重，用再温柔不过的声调说：「你要什么，斯克罗普？」

斯克罗普又上了天堂。「我要什么？除你之外还要什么，玛丽奥内塔？要你，做我学问的同伴、思想的合伙人、我解放人类宏图大业的辅弼。」

===Original===
“I am afraid I should be but a poor auxiliary, Scythrop. What would you have me do?”

“Do as Rosalia does with Carlos, divine Marionetta. Let us each open a vein in the other’s arm, mix our blood in a bowl, and drink it as a sacrament of love. Then we shall see visions of transcendental illumination, and soar on the wings of ideas into the space of pure intelligence.”

===Chinese===
「我怕我当不好这个辅弼，斯克罗普。你要我做什么呢？」

「像罗莎莉娅（Rosalia）待卡洛斯（Carlos）那样去做，神圣的玛丽奥内塔。咱们各自在对方臂上割开一条血管，把血混进一只碗里，喝下去，当爱情的圣餐。然后我们便会看见先验启明的种种异象，乘着理念的翅膀，翱翔进纯粹智性的太空。」

===Original===
Marionetta could not reply; she had not so strong a stomach as Rosalia, and turned sick at the proposition. She disengaged herself suddenly from Scythrop, sprang through the door of the tower, and fled with precipitation along the corridors. Scythrop pursued her, crying, “Stop, stop, Marionetta—my life, my love!” and was gaining rapidly on her flight, when, at an ill-omened corner, where two corridors ended in an angle, at the head of a staircase, he came into sudden and violent contact with Mr. Toobad, and they both plunged together to the foot of the stairs, like two billiard-balls into one pocket. This gave the young lady time to escape, and enclose herself in her chamber; while Mr. Toobad, rising slowly, and rubbing his knees and shoulders, said, “You see, my dear Scythrop, in this little incident, one of the innumerable proofs of the temporary supremacy of the devil; for what but a systematic design and concurrent contrivance of evil could have made the angles of time and place coincide in our unfortunate persons at the head of this accursed staircase?”

===Chinese===
玛丽奥内塔答不上来；她的胃口没有罗莎莉娅那般坚强，这提议叫她恶心欲呕。她猛地从斯克罗普怀里挣脱，闪身出了塔门，顺着走廊仓皇飞奔。斯克罗普在后紧追，一路喊：「站住，站住，玛丽奥内塔——我的性命，我的爱人！」眼看看就要得手，偏偏在一个不祥的拐角——两条走廊在那里交成一个死角，正当楼梯口——他一头撞上图巴德先生，两人一齐滚落楼梯底，像两颗台球滚进同一个袋。这一撞给小姐赢得了脱身的时间，把自己关进了卧房；图巴德先生慢慢爬起来，揉着膝盖和肩膀，说道：「亲爱的斯克罗普，你在这个小小的意外里，看见了魔鬼暂时称尊的无数明证之一；若非邪恶有一套成体系的设计、彼此协同的机谋，又怎能让时间与地点的死角，恰好在咱们两个倒霉人身上、在这该死的楼梯口撞个正着？」

===Original===
“Nothing else, certainly,” said Scythrop: “you are perfectly in the right, Mr. Toobad. Evil, and mischief, and misery, and confusion, and vanity, and vexation of spirit, and death, and disease, and assassination, and war, and poverty, and pestilence, and famine, and avarice, and selfishness, and rancour, and jealousy, and spleen, and malevolence, and the disappointments of philanthropy, and the faithlessness of friendship, and the crosses of love—all prove the accuracy of your views, and the truth of your system; and it is not impossible that the infernal interruption of this fall downstairs may throw a colour of evil on the whole of my future existence.”

===Chinese===
「除了这个，再没别的了，」斯克罗普说，「您完全正确，图巴德先生。邪恶，与祸殃，与苦难，与混乱，与虚空，与心灵的愁烦，与死亡，与疾病，与暗杀，与战争，与贫困，与瘟疫，与饥馑，与贪婪，与自私，与怨毒，与嫉妒，与郁怒，与恶意，与博爱的落空，与友谊的背信，与爱情的乖舛——桩桩件件，都证明您见解的精确、您体系的真实；何况这一跤跌下楼来的地狱式打岔，也未必不会给我下半辈子整个儿抹上一层邪恶的颜色。」

===Original===
“My dear boy,” said Mr. Toobad, “you have a fine eye for consequences.”

So saying, he embraced Scythrop, who retired, with a disconsolate step, to dress for dinner; while Mr. Toobad stalked across the hall, repeating, “Woe to the inhabiters of the earth, and of the sea, for the devil is come among you, having great wrath.”

===Chinese===
「好孩子，」图巴德先生说，「你对后果有好眼力。」

说着，他拥抱了斯克罗普；斯克罗普迈着凄惶的脚步退下去更衣用饭；图巴德先生则大步穿过门厅，口中念念有词：「地与海的居民有祸了，因为魔鬼已来到你们中间，怒气冲冲。」

## IV / 第四章

===Original===
The flight of Marionetta, and the pursuit of Scythrop, had been witnessed by Mr. Glowry, who, in consequence, narrowly observed his son and his niece in the evening; and, concluding from their manner, that there was a better understanding between them than he wished to see, he determined on obtaining the next morning from Scythrop a full and satisfactory explanation. He, therefore, shortly after breakfast, entered Scythrop’s tower, with a very grave face, and said, without ceremony or preface, “So, sir, you are in love with your cousin.”

Scythrop, with as little hesitation, answered, “Yes, sir.”

“That is candid, at least; and she is in love with you.”

“I wish she were, sir.”

“You know she is, sir.”

“Indeed, sir, I do not.”

“But you hope she is.”

“I do, from my soul.”

===Chinese===
玛丽奥内塔的奔逃与斯克罗普的追赶，全落在格洛里先生眼里；当晚他便把儿子和外甥女细细观察了一番，从两人的神态断定：他们之间的默契，已超出了他乐见的程度。于是他决定，第二天一早从斯克罗普口中讨一个充分而满意的解释。因此，早饭后不久，他板着一副极严肃的面孔走进斯克罗普的塔楼，也不客套，也不开场，开门见山便说：「这么说，先生，你爱上你表妹了。」

斯克罗普也以同样少的迟疑答道：「是的，先生。」

「这至少够坦白；她也爱着你。」

「我巴不得如此，先生。」

「你知道她爱着你，先生。」

「说实在的，先生，我不知道。」

「可你盼着她爱你。」

「我打灵魂深处盼着。」

===Original===
“Now that is very provoking, Scythrop, and very disappointing: I could not have supposed that you, Scythrop Glowry, of Nightmare Abbey, would have been infatuated with such a dancing, laughing, singing, thoughtless, careless, merry-hearted thing, as Marionetta—in all respects the reverse of you and me. It is very disappointing, Scythrop. And do you know, sir, that Marionetta has no fortune?”

“It is the more reason, sir, that her husband should have one.”

===Chinese===
「这就太气人了，斯克罗普，也太叫人失望了：我万想不到，你——噩梦隐修院的斯克罗普·格洛里——竟会迷上这么一个又要跳、又要笑、又要唱、没心没肺、无忧无虑、一团欢喜的活物，像玛丽奥内塔这样——同你我处处相反。太叫人失望了，斯克罗普。再说，先生，你可知道玛丽奥内塔没有陪嫁的财产？」

「那先生，她的丈夫就更该有一份了。」

===Original===
“The more reason for her; but not for you. My wife had no fortune, and I had no consolation in my calamity. And do you reflect, sir, what an enormous slice this lawsuit has cut out of our family estate? we who used to be the greatest landed proprietors in Lincolnshire.”

“To be sure, sir, we had more acres of fen than any man on this coast: but what are fens to love? What are dykes and windmills to Marionetta?”

===Chinese===
「对她来说，理由更足；对你可不然。我妻子就没有财产，而我在我的灾难里没讨到半点安慰。你再掂量掂量，先生：这场官司从咱们的家业里剜走了多大一块？咱们家从前可是林肯郡首屈一指的大地主。」

「那是自然，先生，论沼泽地的亩数，咱们在这片海岸上比谁都多：可是爱情要沼泽何用？堤堰和风车，在玛丽奥内塔眼里又算得了什么？」

===Original===
“And what, sir, is love to a windmill? Not grist, I am certain: besides, sir, I have made a choice for you. I have made a choice for you, Scythrop. Beauty, genius, accomplishments, and a great fortune into the bargain. Such a lovely, serious creature, in a fine state of high dissatisfaction with the world, and everything in it. Such a delightful surprise I had prepared for you. Sir, I have pledged my honour to the contract—the honour of the Glowries of Nightmare Abbey: and now, sir, what is to be done?”

“Indeed, sir, I cannot say. I claim, on this occasion, that liberty of action which is the co-natal prerogative of every rational being.”

===Chinese===
「那么先生，爱情对风车又算得了什么？总不是磨坊里的粮食，这我拿得准。再说，先生，我已经替你相中了一个。我已经替你相中了，斯克罗普。美貌，天才，多才多艺，外带一份大家私。那么可爱、那么严肃的一个人儿，对这个世界、对世上的万事万物，正处在高度不满的佳境。我给你备下了这么一件又惊又喜的妙事。先生，我已把我的名誉押在这门契约上——押的是噩梦隐修院格洛里家的名誉：那么现在，先生，你说怎么办？」

「说实在的，先生，我说不上来。在眼下这件事上，我要主张那种行动自由——那是一切理性存在者与生俱来的特权。」

===Original===
“Liberty of action, sir? there is no such thing as liberty of action. We are all slaves and puppets of a blind and unpathetic necessity.”

“Very true, sir; but liberty of action, between individuals, consists in their being differently influenced, or modified, by the same universal necessity; so that the results are unconsentaneous, and their respective necessitated volitions clash and fly off in a tangent.”

===Chinese===
「行动自由，先生？天底下压根没有行动自由这回事。咱们统统是一个盲目而不动情的必然性的奴隶和傀儡。」

「完全正确，先生；可是人与人之间的行动自由，就在于他们受同一普遍必然性的影响或变形各不相同；于是结果互不协同，各自被必然化了的意志彼此冲撞，沿切线方向飞了出去。」

===Original===
“Your logic is good, sir: but you are aware, too, that one individual may be a medium of adhibiting to another a mode or form of necessity, which may have more or less influence in the production of consentaneity; and, therefore, sir, if you do not comply with my wishes in this instance (you have had your own way in everything else), I shall be under the necessity of disinheriting you, though I shall do it with tears in my eyes.” Having said these words, he vanished suddenly, in the dread of Scythrop’s logic.

===Chinese===
「你的逻辑不坏，先生：可你也明白，某一个人可以做媒介，把某一样式、某一形态的必然性施与另一个人，这在促成协同一事上未必没有几分效力；因此，先生，这一回你若不顺从我的心愿（旁的事你早都随心所欲惯了），我将不得不剥夺你的继承权——虽然我会含着眼泪来做这件事。」说完这几句，他突然就不见了——慑于斯克罗普的逻辑。

===Original===
Mr. Glowry immediately sought Mrs. Hilary, and communicated to her his views of the case in point. Mrs. Hilary, as the phrase is, was as fond of Marionetta as if she had been her own child: but—there is always a *but* on these occasions—she could do nothing for her in the way of fortune, as she had two hopeful sons, who were finishing their education at Brazen-nose, and who would not like to encounter any diminution of their prospects, when they should be brought out of the house of mental bondage—i.e. the university—to the land flowing with milk and honey—i.e. the west end of London.

===Chinese===
格洛里先生立刻去找希拉里太太，把他对本案的见解向她和盘托出。希拉里太太，套句俗话，疼玛丽奥内塔疼得如同己出：可是——这类场合永远有个「可是」（but）——要在财产上帮她的忙，她无能为力，因为她有两个前程可望的儿子，正在铜鼻学院（Brazen-nose）完成学业；等他们被领出那心灵奴役之家——即大学——迁往那流奶与蜜之地——即伦敦西区——的时候，他们断不肯看到自己的前程有任何缩水。

===Original===
Mrs. Hilary hinted to Marionetta, that propriety, and delicacy, and decorum, and dignity, etc. etc. etc.,3 would require them to leave the Abbey immediately. Marionetta listened in silent submission, for she knew that her inheritance was passive obedience; but, when Scythrop, who had watched the opportunity of Mrs. Hilary’s departure, entered, and, without speaking a word, threw himself at her feet in a paroxysm of grief, the young lady, in equal silence and sorrow, threw her arms round his neck and burst into tears. A very tender scene ensued, which the sympathetic susceptibilities of the softhearted reader can more accurately imagine than we can delineate. But when Marionetta hinted that she was to leave the Abbey immediately, Scythrop snatched from its repository his ancestor’s skull, filled it with Madeira, and presenting himself before Mr. Glowry, threatened to drink off the contents if Mr. Glowry did not immediately promise that Marionetta should not be taken from the Abbey without her own consent. Mr. Glowry, who took the Madeira to be some deadly brewage, gave the required promise in dismal panic. Scythrop returned to Marionetta with a joyful heart, and drank the Madeira by the way.

===Chinese===
希拉里太太向玛丽奥内塔暗示：体统啊，分寸啊，礼数啊，尊严啊，等等等等3，都要求她们即刻离开隐修院。玛丽奥内塔默默顺从地听着，因为她承袭的家产便是被动服从；可等到斯克罗普——他一直守着希拉里太太离去的机会——走进来，一言不发、悲恸欲绝地扑倒在她脚前时，小姐也同样一言不发、满心悲苦地张开双臂搂住他的脖颈，泪如泉涌。接下来是一幕十分缠绵的场面，心地柔软的读者诸君凭着善感的共鸣，自能想象得比我们的描摹更精确。但当玛丽奥内塔暗示她即刻就要离开隐修院时，斯克罗普从收藏处一把抓出他那位先祖的骷髅，斟满马德拉酒，往格洛里先生跟前一立，扬言格洛里先生若不立刻应允——非经玛丽奥内塔本人同意，不得带她离开隐修院——他就要把这一骷髅盏中的酒一饮而尽。格洛里先生只当那马德拉酒是什么致命的毒酿，凄凄惶惶地应下了所求的承诺。斯克罗普怀着狂喜的心回到玛丽奥内塔身边，一路上把那马德拉酒喝了个干净。

===Original===
Mr. Glowry, during his residence in London, had come to an agreement with his friend Mr. Toobad, that a match between Scythrop and Mr. Toobad’s daughter would be a very desirable occurrence. She was finishing her education in a German convent, but Mr. Toobad described her as being fully impressed with the truth of his Ahrimanic philosophy,4 and being altogether as gloomy and antithalian a young lady as Mr. Glowry himself could desire for the future mistress of Nightmare Abbey. She had a great fortune in her own right, which was not, as we have seen, without its weight in inducing Mr. Glowry to set his heart upon her as his daughter-in-law that was to be; he was therefore very much disturbed by Scythrop’s untoward attachment to Marionetta. He condoled on the occasion with Mr. Toobad; who said, that he had been too long accustomed to the intermeddling of the devil in all his affairs, to be astonished at this new trace of his cloven claw; but that he hoped to outwit him yet, for he was sure there could be no comparison between his daughter and Marionetta in the mind of anyone who had a proper perception of the fact, that, the world being a great theatre of evil, seriousness and solemnity are the characteristics of wisdom, and laughter and merriment make a human being no better than a baboon. Mr. Glowry comforted himself with this view of the subject, and urged Mr. Toobad to expedite his daughter’s return from Germany. Mr. Toobad said he was in daily expectation of her arrival in London, and would set off immediately to meet her, that he might lose no time in bringing her to Nightmare Abbey. “Then,” he added, “we shall see whether Thalia or Melpomene—whether the Allegra or the Penserosa—will carry off the symbol of victory.”

===Chinese===
格洛里先生旅居伦敦期间，已与他的朋友图巴德先生谈妥了一桩协议：斯克罗普与图巴德先生的女儿若能结亲，实乃一大美事。那位小姐当时正在德国一所修道院里完成学业，但图巴德先生描绘说，她已把他那阿里曼（Ahrimanes）哲学4的真理深铭于心，整个人阴郁而反欢乐，正是格洛里先生梦寐以求、堪当噩梦隐修院未来主母的那一号少女。她自己名下有一大笔财产——我们已经看到，这份分量，在格洛里先生认定她做未来儿媳一事上，并非无足轻重——因此，斯克罗普对玛丽奥内塔这段不凑巧的痴情，令他大为烦恼。他就此向图巴德先生吊慰了一番；图巴德先生说，魔鬼插手他的各项事务，他早习以为常，这个新的分趾爪印，不足令他惊诧；不过他仍指望最终斗过它，因为他确信，在任何一个对事实具有正当知觉的人心里，他的女儿与玛丽奥内塔之间根本没有可比性——事实便是：世界是一座巨大的罪恶剧场，严肃与庄重乃是智慧的表征，而欢笑与嬉闹，只会把人弄得比狒狒好不了多少。格洛里先生拿这套见解聊以自慰，并敦促图巴德先生赶快把他女儿从德国接回来。图巴德先生说，他正日日盼着她抵达伦敦，即刻便要动身去迎，以便不失时机地把她带到噩梦隐修院来。「到那时，」他补了一句，「咱们就瞧瞧，究竟是塔利亚（Thalia，喜剧缪斯）还是墨尔波墨涅（Melpomene，悲剧缪斯）——究竟是阿莱格拉（Allegra，「欢乐者」）还是彭塞罗萨（Penserosa，「沉思者」）——能摘走胜利的象征。」

===Original===
“There can be no doubt,” said Mr. Glowry, “which way the scale will incline, or Scythrop is no true scion of the venerable stem of the Glowries.”

===Chinese===
「天平往哪边沉，」格洛里先生说，「还用得着怀疑吗；否则，斯克罗普就不是格洛里家这株古老主干上一根正宗的苗裔了。」

## V / 第五章

===Original===
Marionetta felt secure of Scythrop’s heart; and notwithstanding the difficulties that surrounded her, she could not debar herself from the pleasure of tormenting her lover, whom she kept in a perpetual fever. Sometimes she would meet him with the most unqualified affection; sometimes with the most chilling indifference; rousing him to anger by artificial coldness—softening him to love by eloquent tenderness—or inflaming him to jealousy by coquetting with the Honourable Mr. Listless, who seemed, under her magical influence, to burst into sudden life, like the bud of the evening primrose. Sometimes she would sit by the piano, and listen with becoming attention to Scythrop’s pathetic remonstrances; but, in the most impassioned part of his oratory, she would convert all his ideas into a chaos, by striking up some *rondo allegro*, and saying, “Is it not pretty?” Scythrop would begin to storm; and she would answer him with,

===Chinese===
玛丽奥内塔对斯克罗普的心思十拿九稳；尽管处境重重为难，她也禁不住要拿折磨情人取乐，把他浸在一炉长烧不退的热度里。有时她迎他以最不打折扣的恩爱；有时又以最砭骨的冷淡；用人为的冰冷激他动怒——用雄辩的温存软他生情——再同尊贵的李斯特里斯先生调情，惹他妒火中烧；那位先生在她的魔法影响之下，仿佛月见草的花苞，倏然绽出了生机。有时她会坐在钢琴旁，以合宜的专注，倾听斯克罗普哀婉的谏诤；可一到他辞章最激越处，她便猛敲起一支快板回旋曲（*rondo allegro*），把他的全部思想搅成一团乱麻，还要问一句：「好听不好听？」斯克罗普就要发作；她便回敬他——

===Original===
> “*Zitti, zitti, piano, piano*,

 

 *Non facciamo confusione*”

===Chinese===
> 「*Zitti, zitti, piano, piano*，
 

 *Non facciamo confusione*」（轻些，轻些，慢点儿，慢点儿，咱们可千万别闹出动静）

===Original===
or some similar *facezia*, till he would start away from her, and enclose himself in his tower, in an agony of agitation, vowing to renounce her, and her whole sex, forever; and returning to her presence at the summons of the billet, which she never failed to send with many expressions of penitence and promises of amendment. Scythrop’s schemes for regenerating the world, and detecting his seven golden candlesticks, went on very slowly in this fever of his spirit.

===Chinese===
或诸如此类的戏语（*facezia*），直惹得他从她身边一跃而起，把自己关进塔楼，激动得五内如焚，赌咒发誓从此与她、与她整个性别永诀；可她的小简帖一到传唤，他又乖乖回到她跟前——那简帖她从不忘记送来，附满悔恨之辞与改过之诺。斯克罗普再造世界的大计、寻访七座金灯台的宏图，就在这场心火煎熬中慢慢地蹭。

===Original===
Things proceeded in this train for several days; and Mr. Glowry began to be uneasy at receiving no intelligence from Mr. Toobad; when one evening the latter rushed into the library, where the family and the visitors were assembled, vociferating, “The devil is come among you, having great wrath!” He then drew Mr. Glowry aside into another apartment, and after remaining some time together, they reentered the library with faces of great dismay, but did not condescend to explain to anyone the cause of their discomfiture.

The next morning, early, Mr. Toobad departed. Mr. Glowry sighed and groaned all day, and said not a word to anyone. Scythrop had quarrelled, as usual, with Marionetta, and was enclosed in his tower, in a fit of morbid sensibility. Marionetta was comforting herself at the piano, with singing the airs of *Nina Pazza per Amore*; and the Honourable Mr. Listless was listening to the harmony, as he lay supine on the sofa, with a book in his hand, into which he peeped at intervals. The Reverend Mr. Larynx approached the sofa, and proposed a game at billiards.

===Chinese===
日子就这样过了好几天；格洛里先生迟迟得不到图巴德先生的音信，渐渐坐立不安起来。这天傍晚，后者突然冲进书房——一家人和众宾客正聚在那里——嘴里大嚷：「魔鬼已来到你们中间，怒气冲冲！」随即他把格洛里先生拉到一旁的房间里，两人单独待了半晌，再回书房时一脸的惊惶沮丧，却不肯屈尊向任何人解释他们受挫的缘由。

第二天一早，图巴德先生动身走了。格洛里先生叹气哼哼了一整天，对谁也不发一言。斯克罗普照例同玛丽奥内塔吵了架，正把自己关在塔楼里犯一阵病态的易感症。玛丽奥内塔在钢琴前自宽自解，唱着《因爱而疯的妮娜》（*Nina Pazza per Amore*）里的曲调；尊贵的李斯特里斯先生仰卧在沙发上听这仙音，手里拿着一本书，隔三差五往里瞟上一眼。拉林克斯牧师踱到沙发跟前，提议来一局台球。

===Original===
**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** Billiards!

A parcel was brought in for Mr. Listless; it had been sent express. Fatout was summoned to unpack it; and it proved to contain a new novel, and a new poem, both of which had long been anxiously expected by the whole host of fashionable readers; and the last number of a popular Review, of which the editor and his coadjutors were in high favour at court, and enjoyed ample pensions5 for their services to church and state. As Fatout left the room, Mr. Flosky entered, and curiously inspected the literary arrivals.

===Chinese===
**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 台球！

有包裹送到，是给李斯特里斯先生的，专差递来。法图被唤来拆包：里面是一部新小说、一首新诗，两样都是全体时髦读者引颈久盼的；还有一份风行评论刊物的最新一期——该刊主编及其诸位同寅深得宫廷眷顾，因他们对教会与国家的功勋，坐享着丰厚的养老金5。法图退出时，弗洛斯基先生走了进来，好奇地检阅起这批文学新到货。

===Original===
**Mr. Flosky.** *Turning over the leaves.* “Devilman, a novel.” Hm. Hatred—revenge—misanthropy—and quotations from the Bible. Hm. This is the morbid anatomy of black bile.—“Paul Jones, a poem.” Hm. I see how it is. Paul Jones, an amiable enthusiast—disappointed in his affections—turns pirate from ennui and magnanimity—cuts various masculine throats, wins various feminine hearts—is hanged at the yardarm! The catastrophe is very awkward, and very unpoetical.—“The Downing Street Review.” Hm. First article—An Ode to the Red Book, by Roderick Sackbut, Esquire. Hm. His own poem reviewed by himself. Hm—m—m.

===Chinese===
**弗洛斯基先生.** *翻着书页。*「《魔鬼人》（*Devilman*），一部小说。」哼。仇恨——报复——厌世——外加《圣经》引文。哼。这是黑胆汁的病理解剖。——「《保罗·琼斯》（*Paul Jones*），一首诗。」哼。我明白了。保罗·琼斯，一位可爱的热心人——情场失意——出于倦怠与宽宏大量当了海盗——割了几条男性的喉咙，赢了几颗女性的芳心——末了在桅桁上被绞死！这结局别扭得很，而且全无诗意。——「《唐宁街评论》（*The Downing Street Review*）。」哼。头一篇文章——罗德里克·萨克巴特先生（Roderick Sackbut, Esquire）的《红皮书颂》（*An Ode to the Red Book*）。哼。他自己的诗，由他自己来评。哼——嗯——嗯。

===Original===
*Mr. Flosky proceeded in silence to look over the other articles of the review; Marionetta inspected the novel, and Mr. Listless the poem.*

**The Reverend Mr. Larynx.** For a young man of fashion and family, Mr. Listless, you seem to be of a very studious turn.

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** Studious! You are pleased to be facetious, Mr. Larynx. I hope you do not suspect me of being studious. I have finished my education. But there are some fashionable books that one must read, because they are ingredients of the talk of the day; otherwise, I am no fonder of books than I dare say you yourself are, Mr. Larynx.

===Chinese===
*弗洛斯基先生默不作声地继续翻看评论的其余各篇；玛丽奥内塔检阅那部小说，李斯特里斯先生检阅那首诗。*

**拉林克斯牧师.** 像您这样一位时髦而有门第的青年，李斯特里斯先生，看样子倒颇有几分书卷气呢。

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 书卷气！您真爱说笑，拉林克斯牧师。您总不至于疑心我是个用功的人吧。我的学业早就完成了。不过有些时髦书是不得不读的，因为它们是当日谈资的原料；除此之外，恕我斗胆说一句，我爱书之心，不比您拉林克斯牧师自己更多一分。

===Original===
**The Reverend Mr. Larynx.** Why, sir, I cannot say that I am indeed particularly fond of books; yet neither can I say that I never do read. A tale or a poem, now and then, to a circle of ladies over their work, is no very heterodox employment of the vocal energy. And I must say, for myself, that few men have a more Job-like endurance of the eternally recurring questions and answers that interweave themselves, on these occasions, with the crisis of an adventure, and heighten the distress of a tragedy.

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** And very often make the distress when the author has omitted it.

===Chinese===
**拉林克斯牧师.** 嗐，先生，我确实说不上特别爱书；可也断不能说我从来不读。时不时给一圈做针线的女士们念个故事、诵首诗，倒也算不得多么离经叛道地使用嗓力。而且我必须为自己说一句：这类场合总有些问个没完的问答，缠在一段奇遇的关口上，把悲剧的凄楚越烘越浓——论忍耐它们的约伯式功夫，胜过我的没有几人。

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 而且作者忘了写苦处的地方，还往往由她们当场补出来。

===Original===
**Marionetta.** I shall try your patience some rainy morning, Mr. Larynx; and Mr. Listless shall recommend us the very newest new book, that everybody reads.

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** You shall receive it, Miss O’Carroll, with all the gloss of novelty; fresh as a ripe greengage in all the downiness of its bloom. A mail-coach copy from Edinburgh, forwarded express from London.

===Chinese===
**玛丽奥内塔.** 哪个落雨的早晨，我可要试试您的耐心，拉林克斯牧师；再让李斯特里斯先生给我们荐一部人人都读的、顶新最新的新书。

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 您会收到的，奥卡罗尔小姐，还带着十足的新鲜光泽；新鲜得像一颗熟透的青梅，果粉茸茸、白霜未褪。爱丁堡随邮车发书、再由伦敦专递火速转来的一部。

===Original===
**Mr. Flosky.** This rage for novelty is the bane of literature. Except my works and those of my particular friends, nothing is good that is not as old as Jeremy Taylor: and, *entre nous*, the best parts of my friends’ books were either written or suggested by myself.

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** Sir, I reverence you. But I must say, modern books are very consolatory and congenial to my feelings. There is, as it were, a delightful northeast wind, an intellectual blight breathing through them; a delicious misanthropy and discontent, that demonstrates the nullity of virtue and energy, and puts me in good humour with myself and my sofa.

===Chinese===
**弗洛斯基先生.** 这种求新的狂热，是文学的痼疾。除了我的著作和我几位至交的著作，凡是不像杰里米·泰勒（Jeremy Taylor）那样古老的东西，没有一样是好的；而且，*entre nous*（咱们私下说说），我那些朋友书里最精彩的段落，不是出自我的手笔，就是我的点子。

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 先生，我敬仰您。但我必须说，现代的书非常慰藉我心、与我情投意合。那里面仿佛刮着一股可人的东北风，一阵智性的霜灾穿书而过；一种可口的厌世与不满，证明美德与精力皆是虚空，叫我自己同我的沙发相处得心情舒畅。

===Original===
**Mr. Flosky.** Very true, sir. Modern literature is a northeast wind—a blight of the human soul. I take credit to myself for having helped to make it so. The way to produce fine fruit is to blight the flower. You call this a paradox. Marry, so be it. Ponder thereon.

===Chinese===
**弗洛斯基先生.** 十分正确，先生。现代文学是一股东北风——人类灵魂的一场霜灾。我引以自傲的是，这局面也有我一份促成之功。要结出好果子，先得摧折花。你管这叫悖论。就算是吧。且去细细思量。

===Original===
The conversation was interrupted by the reappearance of Mr. Toobad, covered with mud. He just showed himself at the door, muttered “The devil is come among you!” and vanished. The road which connected Nightmare Abbey with the civilised world, was artificially raised above the level of the fens, and ran through them in a straight line as far as the eye could reach, with a ditch on each side, of which the water was rendered invisible by the aquatic vegetation that covered the surface. Into one of these ditches the sudden action of a shy horse, which took fright at a windmill, had precipitated the travelling chariot of Mr. Toobad, who had been reduced to the necessity of scrambling in dismal plight through the window. One of the wheels was found to be broken; and Mr. Toobad, leaving the postilion to get the chariot as well as he could to Claydyke for the purpose of cleaning and repairing, had walked back to Nightmare Abbey, followed by his servant with the imperial, and repeating all the way his favourite quotation from the Revelations.

===Chinese===
谈话被图巴德先生的再次露面打断了——他浑身是泥。他只在门口露了一脸，咕哝一句「魔鬼已来到你们中间！」便又没了踪影。连通噩梦隐修院与文明世界的那条路，人工垫得高于沼泽面，笔直地穿过沼泽，一眼望不到头，两侧各有一道沟，沟里的水被覆满水面的水生植物遮得看不见。就在其中一道沟里，一匹见了风车受惊的怯马猛地里一闪，把图巴德先生的旅行马车掀了进去；他本人被迫在狼狈万状中从车窗里爬将出来。一只轮子发现已经断裂；图巴德先生把马车丢给车夫，让他自想办法弄到克莱迪克去清洗修理，自己步行返回噩梦隐修院，身后跟着扛车顶行李箱的仆人，一路上反复念诵他心爱的《启示录》引文。

## VI / 第六章

===Original===
Mr. Toobad had found his daughter Celinda in London, and after the first joy of meeting was over, told her he had a husband ready for her. The young lady replied, very gravely, that she should take the liberty to choose for herself. Mr. Toobad said he saw the devil was determined to interfere with all his projects, but he was resolved on his own part, not to have on his conscience the crime of passive obedience and nonresistance to Lucifer, and therefore she should marry the person he had chosen for her. Miss Toobad replied, *très posément*, she assuredly would not. “Celinda, Celinda,” said Mr. Toobad, “you most assuredly shall.”

“Have I not a fortune in my own right, sir?” said Celinda.

“The more is the pity,” said Mr. Toobad: “but I can find means, miss; I can find means. There are more ways than one of breaking in obstinate girls.” They parted for the night with the expression of opposite resolutions, and in the morning the young lady’s chamber was found empty, and what was become of her Mr. Toobad had no clue to conjecture. He continued to investigate town and country in search of her; visiting and revisiting Nightmare Abbey at intervals, to consult with his friend, Mr. Glowry. Mr. Glowry agreed with Mr. Toobad that this was a very flagrant instance of filial disobedience and rebellion; and Mr. Toobad declared, that when he discovered the fugitive, she should find that “the devil was come unto her, having great wrath.”

===Chinese===
图巴德先生在伦敦找到了他的女儿塞琳达（Celinda）；重逢的欢喜一过，他便告诉她，他已为她备下了一位丈夫。小姐极其郑重地回答，她要冒昧自己择婿。图巴德先生说，他看明白了，魔鬼是铁了心要插手他的一切计划；但他自己这方面也拿定了主意，绝不肯让「被动服从、不抗 Lucifer（路西弗）」的罪名压在良心上，因此她必须嫁他选定的那个人。图巴德小姐答道，*très posément*（不慌不忙地），她断然不嫁。「塞琳达，塞琳达，」图巴德先生说，「你断然要嫁。」

「我自己名下不是有一份财产吗，先生？」塞琳达说。

「那才可惜呢，」图巴德先生说：「不过我有的是法子，小姐；我有的是法子。驯服倔强姑娘的路数，不止一条呢。」两人各执一辞，分头就寝；第二天早晨，小姐的闺房已经空了，她出了什么事，图巴德先生连一条可供猜度的线索也没有。他继续在城里乡下四处查访；隔三差五地重访噩梦隐修院，同他的朋友格洛里先生商议。格洛里先生同意图巴德先生的看法：这是一桩骇人听闻的忤逆反叛；图巴德先生则扬言，一旦捉住这逃亡者，她自会知道「魔鬼已来到她那里，怒气冲冲」。

===Original===
In the evening, the whole party met, as usual, in the library. Marionetta sat at the harp; the Honourable Mr. Listless sat by her and turned over her music, though the exertion was almost too much for him. The Reverend Mr. Larynx relieved him occasionally in this delightful labour. Scythrop, tormented by the demon Jealousy, sat in the corner biting his lips and fingers. Marionetta looked at him every now and then with a smile of most provoking good humour, which he pretended not to see, and which only the more exasperated his troubled spirit. He took down a volume of Dante, and pretended to be deeply interested in the Purgatorio, though he knew not a word he was reading, as Marionetta was well aware; who, tripping across the room, peeped into his book, and said to him, “I see you are in the middle of Purgatory.”

“I am in the middle of hell,” said Scythrop furiously.

“Are you?” said she; “then come across the room, and I will sing you the finale of *Don Giovanni*.”

“Let me alone,” said Scythrop.

Marionetta looked at him with a deprecating smile, and said, “You unjust, cross creature, you.”

“Let me alone,” said Scythrop, but much less emphatically than at first, and by no means wishing to be taken at his word.

===Chinese===
晚上，一行人照例聚在书房里。玛丽奥内塔坐在竖琴旁；尊贵的李斯特里斯先生坐在她身边替她翻乐谱，虽然这份卖力几乎要了他的命。拉林克斯牧师间或替下他，分担这项可人的劳动。斯克罗普受着嫉妒之魔的煎逼，缩在角落里咬嘴唇、咬手指。玛丽奥内塔时不时朝他一笑，笑得和气到叫人冒火，他只装没看见，可这反倒把他那颗烦乱的心激得更旺。他取下一卷但丁（Dante），假装读《炼狱篇》读得入神，其实一个字也没读进去——这一点玛丽奥内塔心知肚明；她轻快地穿过房间，往他书里一瞟，对他说：「我瞧见啦，你正在炼狱半中腰。」

「我在地狱半中腰，」斯克罗普怒气冲冲地说。

「是吗？」她说；「那就过这边来，我唱《唐·乔万尼》（*Don Giovanni*）的终场给你听。」

「别理我，」斯克罗普说。

玛丽奥内塔带着告饶的笑望着他，说：「你这个不讲理的、别扭的坏东西。」

「别理我，」斯克罗普说——这回的语气可比头一回软得多了，而且绝没有要人当真的意思。

===Original===
Marionetta left him immediately, and returning to the harp, said, just loud enough for Scythrop to hear—“Did you ever read Dante, Mr. Listless? Scythrop is reading Dante, and is just now in Purgatory.”

“And I,” said the Honourable Mr. Listless, “am not reading Dante, and am just now in Paradise,” bowing to Marionetta.

**Marionetta.** You are very gallant, Mr. Listless; and I dare say you are very fond of reading Dante.

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** I don’t know how it is, but Dante never came in my way till lately. I never had him in my collection, and if I had had him I should not have read him. But I find he is growing fashionable, and I am afraid I must read him some wet morning.

**Marionetta.** No, read him some evening, by all means. Were you ever in love, Mr. Listless?

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** I assure you, Miss O’Carroll, never—till I came to Nightmare Abbey. I dare say it is very pleasant; but it seems to give so much trouble that I fear the exertion would be too much for me.

===Chinese===
玛丽奥内塔立刻撇下他，回到竖琴旁，说——音量刚好够斯克罗普听见——「您读过但丁吗，李斯特里斯先生？斯克罗普正在读但丁，这会儿正在炼狱半中腰。」

「而我呢，」尊贵的李斯特里斯先生说，「没有在读但丁，这会儿正在天堂，」说着向玛丽奥内塔一躬。

**玛丽奥内塔.** 您可真会献殷勤，李斯特里斯先生；我敢说您一定顶爱读但丁。

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 不知怎么的，但丁近来才入得了我的眼。我的收藏里从来没有过他；就算有过，我也不会读他。可我发现他渐渐时髦起来了，恐怕哪个落雨的早晨，我只好读一读他了。

**玛丽奥内塔.** 不行，务必挑个晚上读。您恋爱过吗，李斯特里斯先生？

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 我向您担保，奥卡罗尔小姐，从来没有——直到来噩梦隐修院才算有了。我想那滋味必定很美；可看着这么费事，我怕这份卖力气我消受不了。

===Original===
**Marionetta.** Shall I teach you a compendious method of courtship, that will give you no trouble whatever?

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** You will confer on me an inexpressible obligation. I am all impatience to learn it.

**Marionetta.** Sit with your back to the lady and read Dante; only be sure to begin in the middle, and turn over three or four pages at once—backwards as well as forwards, and she will immediately perceive that you are desperately in love with her—desperately.

*The Honourable Mr. Listless sitting between Scythrop and Marionetta, and fixing all his attention on the beautiful speaker, did not observe Scythrop, who was doing as she described.*

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** You are pleased to be facetious, Miss O’Carroll. The lady would infallibly conclude that I was the greatest brute in town.

**Marionetta.** Far from it. She would say, perhaps, some people have odd methods of showing their affection.

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** But I should think, with submission—

===Chinese===
**玛丽奥内塔.** 要不要我教您一套简便的求爱法，保管一点也不费事？

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 那您就是赐给了我一份说不尽的恩情。我洗耳恭听，急不可待。

**玛丽奥内塔.** 背朝着那位女士坐好，读但丁；只要务必从半中腰读起，一次翻过三四页——往前翻也行，往后翻也行——她立刻就会看出，您爱她爱得发疯——发疯。

*尊贵的李斯特里斯先生坐在斯克罗普与玛丽奥内塔之间，全副注意力都钉在美丽的说话人身上，没有留意斯克罗普——他正照她说的那样做着。*

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 您真爱说笑，奥卡罗尔小姐。那位女士准会断定，我是全城头一号的畜生。

**玛丽奥内塔.** 哪里至于。她多半会说，有些人表达爱慕的法子，古怪就是了。

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 不过恕我直言，我倒以为——

===Original===
**Mr. Flosky.** *Joining them from another part of the room.* Did I not hear Mr. Listless observe that Dante is becoming fashionable?

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** I did hazard a remark to that effect, Mr. Flosky, though I speak on such subjects with a consciousness of my own nothingness, in the presence of so great a man as Mr. Flosky. I know not what is the colour of Dante’s devils, but as he is certainly becoming fashionable I conclude they are blue; for the blue devils, as it seems to me, Mr. Flosky, constitute the fundamental feature of fashionable literature.

**Mr. Flosky.** The blue are, indeed, the staple commodity; but as they will not always be commanded, the black, red, and grey may be admitted as substitutes. Tea, late dinners, and the French Revolution, have played the devil, Mr. Listless, and brought the devil into play.

**Mr. Toobad.** *Starting up.*

Having great wrath.

**Mr. Flosky.** This is no play upon words, but the sober sadness of veritable fact.

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** Tea, late dinners, and the French Revolution. I cannot exactly see the connection of ideas.

===Chinese===
**弗洛斯基先生.** *从房间另一头加入进来。* 我方才是不是听见李斯特里斯先生说，但丁渐渐时髦起来了？

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 我确实斗胆发表过一点这类的意见，弗洛斯基先生；不过在弗洛斯基先生这样一位伟人面前谈这种题目，我是深自觉着自身之渺小的。但丁笔下的魔鬼是什么颜色，我不知道；但他既然确凿地时髦起来了，我推断那魔鬼准是蓝的；因为蓝魔（blue devils，忧郁症）——据我看，弗洛斯基先生——正是时髦文学的基本特色。

**弗洛斯基先生.** 蓝的确实是主打货色；只是蓝魔不总能呼之即来，黑的、红的、灰的，也可准其充数。茶啊，深夜的正餐啊，还有法国大革命啊，李斯特里斯先生，实在是作下了大孽，把魔鬼也请上了场。

**图巴德先生.** *霍地跳起。*

怒气冲冲。

**弗洛斯基先生.** 这可不是什么文字游戏，是千真万确的事实，字字清醒、句句沉痛。

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 茶，深夜的正餐，还有法国大革命。这几层意思的联系，我一时还看不大出来。

===Original===
**Mr. Flosky.** I should be sorry if you could; I pity the man who can see the connection of his own ideas. Still more do I pity him, the connection of whose ideas any other person can see. Sir, the great evil is, that there is too much commonplace light in our moral and political literature; and light is a great enemy to mystery, and mystery is a great friend to enthusiasm. Now the enthusiasm for abstract truth is an exceedingly fine thing, as long as the truth, which is the object of the enthusiasm, is so completely abstract as to be altogether out of the reach of the human faculties; and, in that sense, I have myself an enthusiasm for truth, but in no other, for the pleasure of metaphysical investigation lies in the means, not in the end; and if the end could be found, the pleasure of the means would cease. The mind, to be kept in health, must be kept in exercise. The proper exercise of the mind is elaborate reasoning. Analytical reasoning is a base and mechanical process, which takes to pieces and examines, bit by bit, the rude material of knowledge, and extracts therefrom a few hard and obstinate things called facts, everything in the shape of which I cordially hate. But synthetical reasoning, setting up as its goal some unattainable abstraction, like an imaginary quantity in algebra, and commencing its course with taking for granted some two assertions which cannot be proved, from the union of these two assumed truths produces a third assumption, and so on in infinite series, to the unspeakable benefit of the human intellect. The beauty of this process is, that at every step it strikes out into two branches, in a compound ratio of ramification; so that you are perfectly sure of losing your way, and keeping your mind in perfect health, by the perpetual exercise of an interminable quest; and for these reasons I have christened my eldest son Emanuel Kant Flosky.

**The Reverend Mr. Larynx.** Nothing can be more luminous.

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** And what has all that to do with Dante, and the blue devils?

**Mr. Hilary.** Not much, I should think, with Dante, but a great deal with the blue devils.

===Chinese===
**弗洛斯基先生.** 您若看得出来，我倒要难过了；一个人若能看清自己思想的联系，我可怜他。一个人思想的联系若连旁人都看得清，我就更可怜他。先生，大弊病在于：咱们的道德与政治文学里，平凡的光太多了；光是神秘的大敌，而神秘是热忱的大友。要知道，对抽象真理的热忱是件绝妙的东西——只要那热忱的对象、那个真理，抽象得足够彻底，彻底到全然够不着人类的一切官能；在这个意义上，我自己对真理也怀有热忱——但仅止于这个意义；因为形而上学探究的乐趣在手段，不在目的；目的若能找着，手段的乐趣也就完了。心灵要保住健康，就得保住锻炼。心灵正当的锻炼，是精微的推理。分析的推理是个下等而机械的工序：把知识的粗料一件件拆开、检验，从中榨出几样又硬又倔的东西，名曰事实——凡是这形状的东西，我一律衷心憎恨。而综合的推理，立一个够不着的抽象当目标——好比代数里的虚量——一开步就先认定两条无法证明的断语为真，再由这两条假定真理之结合，产出第三条假定，如此递进，以至无穷级数，于人类智性，功德不可胜言。这道工序的美处在于：每一步都岔出两支，歧路之繁复按复利增长；于是您稳稳当当迷了路，又靠这场无尽追寻的永恒操练，把心灵保养得十分康健；缘此种种，我给长子行洗礼，取名伊曼努尔·康德·弗洛斯基。

**拉林克斯牧师.** 再明白不过了。

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 可这同但丁、同蓝魔，又有什么相干？

**希拉里先生.** 同但丁，我想相干不多；同蓝魔，可就大有干系了。

===Original===
**Mr. Flosky.** It is very certain, and much to be rejoiced at, that our literature is hag-ridden. Tea has shattered our nerves; late dinners make us slaves of indigestion; the French Revolution has made us shrink from the name of philosophy, and has destroyed, in the more refined part of the community (of which number I am one), all enthusiasm for political liberty. That part of the *reading public* which shuns the solid food of reason for the light diet of fiction, requires a perpetual adhibition of *sauce piquante* to the palate of its depraved imagination. It lived upon ghosts, goblins, and skeletons (I and my friend Mr. Sackbut served up a few of the best), till even the devil himself, though magnified to the size of Mount Athos, became too base, common, and popular, for its surfeited appetite. The ghosts have therefore been laid, and the devil has been cast into outer darkness, and now the delight of our spirits is to dwell on all the vices and blackest passions of our nature, tricked out in a masquerade dress of heroism and disappointed benevolence; the whole secret of which lies in forming combinations that contradict all our experience, and affixing the purple shred of some particular virtue to that precise character, in which we should be most certain not to find it in the living world; and making this single virtue not only redeem all the real and manifest vices of the character, but make them actually pass for necessary adjuncts, and indispensable accompaniments and characteristics of the said virtue.

**Mr. Toobad.** That is, because the devil is come among us, and finds it for his interest to destroy all our perceptions of the distinctions of right and wrong.

**Marionetta.** I do not precisely enter into your meaning, Mr. Flosky, and should be glad if you would make it a little more plain to me.

===Chinese===
**弗洛斯基先生.** 千真万确、大可庆幸的是：咱们的文学正被梦魇骑着走。茶把咱们的神经喝得粉碎；深夜的正餐叫咱们做了消化不良的奴隶；法国大革命叫咱们听见哲学的名头就发缩，还在社会上更精致的那一部分人（我忝列其中）心里，毁掉了一切对政治自由的热忱。读者公众里那一部分人——避着理性的固体粮、专吃小说的流食——需要往它那堕落想象力的味蕾上，永远浇洒 *sauce piquante*（辛辣的酱汁）。它靠鬼魂、妖精和骷髅过活（我和我的朋友萨克巴特先生就供奉过几道上品），直到连魔鬼本人——哪怕放大到阿陀斯山（Mount Athos）的尺寸——对它吃腻了的胃口，也嫌太下等、太寻常、太大众了。于是鬼魂被收了，魔鬼被丢进外边的黑暗里；如今咱们心神的乐处，是流连于人性中一切的恶德与最黑的情欲——只要给它们穿上英雄主义与失意善行的化装舞衣。全套诀窍全在于：拼凑出同咱们全部经验相悖的组合，再把某一种美德的紫布条，恰好缀到那个在活人世界里咱们最有把握找不到它的人物身上；再叫这一样美德，不但赎清该人物一切真实显著的恶德，还当真叫这些恶德冒充那美德必不可少的附件、缺不得的陪衬与标志。

**图巴德先生.** 这是因为魔鬼已来到咱们中间，又觉得抹掉咱们对是非之辨的一切知觉，正合他的利益。

**玛丽奥内塔.** 我没有完全听懂您的用意，弗洛斯基先生；您若肯给我讲得更浅显些，我就感激不尽了。

===Original===
**Mr. Flosky.** One or two examples will do it, Miss O’Carroll. If I were to take all the mean and sordid qualities of a money-dealing Jew, and tack on to them, as with a nail, the quality of extreme benevolence, I should have a very decent hero for a modern novel; and should contribute my quota to the fashionable method of administering a mass of vice, under a thin and unnatural covering of virtue, like a spider wrapt in a bit of gold leaf, and administered as a wholesome pill. On the same principle, if a man knocks me down, and takes my purse and watch by main force, I turn him to account, and set him forth in a tragedy as a dashing young fellow, disinherited for his romantic generosity, and full of a most amiable hatred of the world in general, and his own country in particular, and of a most enlightened and chivalrous affection for himself: then, with the addition of a wild girl to fall in love with him, and a series of adventures in which they break all the Ten Commandments in succession (always, you will observe, for some sublime motive, which must be carefully analysed in its progress), I have as amiable a pair of tragic characters as ever issued from that new region of the *belles lettres*, which I have called the Morbid Anatomy of Black Bile, and which is greatly to be admired and rejoiced at, as affording a fine scope for the exhibition of mental power.

**Mr. Hilary.** Which is about as well employed as the power of a hothouse would be in forcing up a nettle to the size of an elm. If we go on in this way, we shall have a new art of poetry, of which one of the first rules will be: To remember to forget that there are any such things as sunshine and music in the world.

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** It seems to be the case with us at present, or we should not have interrupted Miss O’Carroll’s music with this exceedingly dry conversation.

===Chinese===
**弗洛斯基先生.** 举一两个例子就成，奥卡罗尔小姐。倘若我把一个放钱营生的犹太人的全部卑鄙龌龊捞起来，再像钉钉子似的，往上面钉一份极端的仁慈，我就有了一位满体面的现代小说主人公；也就为这门时新法门尽了我一份配额：把一大团恶德，裹上一层又薄又造作的德行外衣给人吞服——好比一只蜘蛛，包上一小片金箔，当健身药丸发付。依同一原理，倘若有人把我打翻在地，凭蛮力抢走我的钱袋和怀表，我便把他变废为宝，在一出悲剧里把他捧成一位神气的少年：因浪漫的豪爽被剥夺了继承权，满怀着对全世界——尤其是对他自己祖国——顶可爱的憎恶，以及对他自己顶开明、顶侠义的热爱：然后添上一个野姑娘爱上他，再来一连串的奇遇，让他们把十诫挨个破尽（你会注意到，永远是为了某种崇高的动机，而这动机在进展中还必须被细细剖析），我便得到了一对悲剧人物，可爱得不亚于从纯文学（*belles lettres*）那片新疆域里走出来的任何一对——那疆域，我名之曰「黑胆汁的病理解剖」；它大大值得赞美庆幸，因为它给心智之力的展览，提供了绝好的用武之地。

**希拉里先生.** 这份力气用得，同花房的热力用来把一株荨麻催成榆树一般大小，差不多少。再这样下去，咱们就要有一门新的诗艺了，头几条规则之一便是：牢记别记住，世上还有阳光和音乐这等东西。

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 眼下咱们像正是这光景，不然也不至于拿这场干得要命的谈话，打断奥卡罗尔小姐的音乐了。

===Original===
**Mr. Flosky.** I should be most happy if Miss O’Carroll would remind us that there are yet both music and sunshine—

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** In the voice and the smile of beauty. May I entreat the favour of—*turning over the pages of music.*

All were silent, and Marionetta sung:

===Chinese===
**弗洛斯基先生.** 倘若奥卡罗尔小姐肯提醒咱们，世上毕竟还有音乐、也有阳光——那我真是三生有幸——

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 就在美的嗓音与微笑之中。可否容我恳请——*翻动乐谱的页子。*

众人都静下来，玛丽奥内塔唱道：

===Original===
> Why are thy looks so blank, grey friar?

 

 Why are thy looks so blue?

 

 Thou seem’st more pale and lank, grey friar,

 

 Than thou wast used to do:—

 

 Say, what has made thee rue?
> Thy form was plump, and a light did shine

 

 In thy round and ruby face,

 

 Which showed an outward visible sign

 

 Of an inward spiritual grace:—

 

 Say, what has changed thy case?
> Yet will I tell thee true, grey friar,

 

 I very well can see,

 

 That, if thy looks are blue, grey friar,

 

 ’Tis all for love of me—

 

 ’Tis all for love of me.
> But breathe not thy vows to me, grey friar,

 

 Oh, breathe them not, I pray;

 

 For ill beseems in a reverend friar,

 

 The love of a mortal may;

 

 And I needs must say thee nay.
> But, could’st thou think my heart to move

 

 With that pale and silent scowl?

 

 Know, he who would win a maiden’s love,

 

 Whether clad in cap or cowl,

 

 Must be more of a lark than an owl.

===Chinese===
> 灰衣修士，你的脸色为何这般发白？

 

 灰衣修士，你的容颜为何这般发青？

 

 你瞧上去又灰又瘦，灰衣修士，

 

 同往常大不相似：——

 

 说吧，是什么叫你伤情？
> 你的身量本丰满，一团光焰

 

 在你红润的圆脸上闪，

 

 那原是外头看得见的凭证，

 

 证着里头灵魂的恩典：——

 

 说吧，是什么改了你的命？
> 不过我愿对你说实话，灰衣修士，

 

 我心里看得明明白白：

 

 你这脸色若是发青，灰衣修士，

 

 那全是为了爱我的缘故——

 

 那全是为了爱我的缘故。
> 可别把你的誓愿吐给我，灰衣修士，

 

 哦，千万别吐，我求你；

 

 因为一位可敬的修士

 

 爱上一个凡间女子，实在不相宜；

 

 我也只得对你说声不可。
> 不过，你若以为凭你那

 

 灰白无言的一张苦脸，就能打动我的心？

 

 要知道，想赢得姑娘的爱情，

 

 不管戴的是便帽还是兜帽，

 

 就得做云雀，别做猫头鹰。

===Original===
Scythrop immediately replaced Dante on the shelf, and joined the circle round the beautiful singer. Marionetta gave him a smile of approbation that fully restored his complacency, and they continued on the best possible terms during the remainder of the evening.
