The Honourable Mr. Listless turned over the leaves with double alacrity, saying, “You are severe upon invalids, Miss O’Carroll: to escape your satire, I must try to be sprightly, though the exertion is too much for me.”

===Chinese===
斯克罗普立刻把但丁放回架上，凑到美丽的歌者身边那一圈里。玛丽奥内塔报他一个嘉许的微笑，把他的自得全数召回；这晚余下的时光，两人好得不能再好。尊贵的李斯特里斯先生翻乐谱翻得加倍起劲，说道：「您对病号可真够狠的，奥卡罗尔小姐：要躲过您的讥刺，我只好试试活泼一点——虽然这份卖力气，实在要了我的命。」

## VII / 第七章

===Original===
A new visitor arrived at the Abbey, in the person of Mr. Asterias, the ichthyologist. This gentleman had passed his life in seeking the living wonders of the deep through the four quarters of the world; he had a cabinet of stuffed and dried fishes, of shells, seaweeds, corals, and madrepores, that was the admiration and envy of the Royal Society. He had penetrated into the watery den of the Sepia Octopus, disturbed the conjugal happiness of that turtledove of the ocean, and come off victorious in a sanguinary conflict. He had been becalmed in the tropical seas, and had watched, in eager expectation, though unhappily always in vain, to see the colossal polypus rise from the water, and entwine its enormous arms round the masts and the rigging. He maintained the origin of all things from water, and insisted that the polypodes were the first of animated things, and that, from their round bodies and many-shooting arms, the Hindus had taken their gods, the most ancient of deities. But the chief object of his ambition, the end and aim of his researches, was to discover a triton and a mermaid, the existence of which he most potently and implicitly believed, and was prepared to demonstrate, *a priori, a posteriori, a fortiori*, synthetically and analytically, syllogistically and inductively, by arguments deduced both from acknowledged facts and plausible hypotheses. A report that a mermaid had been seen “sleeking her soft alluring locks” on the seacoast of Lincolnshire, had brought him in great haste from London, to pay a long-promised and often-postponed visit to his old acquaintance, Mr. Glowry.

===Chinese===
一位新客人来到隐修院——鱼类学家阿斯特里亚斯先生（Mr. Asterias）。这位先生一生都在遍访四海，寻猎深水中的活奇观；他藏有一柜剥制的和干制的鱼、贝壳、海藻、珊瑚和石珊瑚，令皇家学会又赞叹又眼红。他曾潜入乌贼章鱼的巢穴，搅扰过这海中斑鸠的琴瑟之好，并在一场血战中凯旋。他曾在热带海上遭遇无风，满怀热望地守望——虽然始终不幸落空——想看那巨大的螅怪从水中升起，把硕大无朋的臂膀缠上桅杆和索具。他主张万物起源于水，坚称多螅类（polypodes）乃一切有生之物之首，而印度人的诸神——最古老的众神——正是从它们浑圆的身躯和四射的手臂中取的形象。但他平生夙愿之首、研究之终点，是发现一尾雄人鱼和一尾美人鱼；这两者的存在，他深信不疑，且随时准备予以证明——*a priori*（先天地）、*a posteriori*（后天地）、*a fortiori*（更遑论地），综合地、分析地，三段论地、归纳地，用公认事实推出的论证，也用貌似有理的假说推出的论证。有报告说，林肯郡海岸上有人见过一条美人鱼「梳理她柔软诱人的秀发」，他闻讯便从伦敦火速赶来，拜访老相识格洛里先生——这一趟拜访他久已应承，也久已延宕。

===Original===
Mr. Asterias was accompanied by his son, to whom he had given the name of Aquarius—flattering himself that he would, in the process of time, become a constellation among the stars of ichthyological science. What charitable female had lent him the mould in which this son was cast, no one pretended to know; and, as he never dropped the most distant allusion to Aquarius’s mother, some of the wags of London maintained that he had received the favours of a mermaid, and that the scientific perquisitions which kept him always prowling about the seashore, were directed by the less philosophical motive of regaining his lost love.

===Chinese===
阿斯特里亚斯先生身边带着他的儿子，取名阿夸里乌斯（Aquarius，「宝瓶座」）——他沾沾自喜，指望这孩子假以时日，将成为鱼类学群星之中的一座星座。哪位慈悲的女性借了他浇铸这个儿子的模子，无人妄称知晓；而他对阿夸里乌斯的母亲从不作最间接的暗示，于是伦敦有些促狭鬼一口咬定：他是得过一位美人鱼的恩情的；而他常年流连海边的那番科学搜查，其实另有一个哲学味较淡的动机——寻回他失去的爱人。

===Original===
Mr. Asterias perlustrated the seacoast for several days, and reaped disappointment, but not despair. One night, shortly after his arrival, he was sitting in one of the windows of the library, looking towards the sea, when his attention was attracted by a figure which was moving near the edge of the surf, and which was dimly visible through the moonless summer night. Its motions were irregular, like those of a person in a state of indecision. It had extremely long hair, which floated in the wind. Whatever else it might be, it certainly was not a fisherman. It might be a lady; but it was neither Mrs. Hilary nor Miss O’Carroll, for they were both in the library. It might be one of the female servants; but it had too much grace, and too striking an air of habitual liberty, to render it probable. Besides, what should one of the female servants be doing there at this hour, moving to and fro, as it seemed, without any visible purpose? It could scarcely be a stranger; for Claydyke, the nearest village, was ten miles distant; and what female would come ten miles across the fens, for no purpose but to hover over the surf under the walls of Nightmare Abbey? Might it not be a mermaid? It was possibly a mermaid. It was probably a mermaid. It was very probably a mermaid. Nay, what else could it be but a mermaid? It certainly was a mermaid. Mr. Asterias stole out of the library on tiptoe, with his finger on his lips, having beckoned Aquarius to follow him.

===Chinese===
阿斯特里亚斯先生沿海岸巡行了几日，收获的是失望，但不是绝望。到后不久的一夜，他正坐在书房的一扇窗里望海，忽被一个身影引去了注意：那身影在浪缘附近移动，隔着无月的夏夜，隐约可辨。它的动作没有规律，像一个踌躇不决的人。它有一头极长的头发，随风飘拂。不论别的，它总归不是渔夫。它兴许是位女士；但既非希拉里太太，也非奥卡罗尔小姐，因为她们都在书房里。它兴许是哪个女仆；可它仪态太娴雅，那副自在惯了的气派又太扎眼，不像是。再说，女仆这个时辰到那里去做什么，来来回回，似乎并无可见的目的？总不会是生人；因为最近的村子克莱迪克也在十英里外；哪位女士会为着在噩梦隐修院墙外看浪，横穿十英里沼泽？它会不会是美人鱼？它可能是美人鱼。它很可能是美人鱼。它非常可能是美人鱼。嗐，除了美人鱼还能是别的什么？它必定是美人鱼。阿斯特里亚斯先生踮着脚尖溜出书房，一根手指压在唇上，招手叫阿夸里乌斯跟上。

===Original===
The rest of the party was in great surprise at Mr. Asterias’s movement, and some of them approached the window to see if the locality would tend to elucidate the mystery. Presently they saw him and Aquarius cautiously stealing along on the other side of the moat, but they saw nothing more; and Mr. Asterias returning, told them, with accents of great disappointment, that he had had a glimpse of a mermaid, but she had eluded him in the darkness, and was gone, he presumed, to sup with some enamoured triton, in a submarine grotto.

“But, seriously, Mr. Asterias,” said the Honourable Mr. Listless, “do you positively believe there are such things as mermaids?”

===Chinese===
满座对阿斯特里亚斯先生的动作大为诧异，有几个人凑到窗前，想看看这方位能否解一解这个谜。不久他们望见他和阿夸里乌斯蹑手蹑脚，摸过护宅河对岸，此后就再没看见什么；阿斯特里亚斯先生回来后，用大失所望的声调告诉他们：他望见了一眼美人鱼，可她趁着黑暗溜掉了，想必是到某座海底岩洞里，赴哪位害相思的雄人鱼的晚饭去了。

「不过说正经的，阿斯特里亚斯先生，」尊贵的李斯特里斯先生说，「您当真相信世上有美人鱼这种东西？」

===Original===
**Mr. Asterias.** Most assuredly; and tritons too.

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** What! things that are half human and half fish?

**Mr. Asterias.** Precisely. They are the oran-outangs of the sea. But I am persuaded that there are also complete sea men, differing in no respect from us, but that they are stupid, and covered with scales; for, though our organisation seems to exclude us essentially from the class of amphibious animals, yet anatomists well know that the *foramen ovale* may remain open in an adult, and that respiration is, in that case, not necessary to life: and how can it be otherwise explained that the Indian divers, employed in the pearl fishery, pass whole hours under the water; and that the famous Swedish gardener of Troningholm lived a day and a half under the ice without being drowned? A nereid, or mermaid, was taken in the year 1403 in a Dutch lake, and was in every respect like a French woman, except that she did not speak. Towards the end of the seventeenth century, an English ship, a hundred and fifty leagues from land, in the Greenland seas, discovered a flotilla of sixty or seventy little skiffs, in each of which was a triton, or sea man: at the approach of the English vessel the whole of them, seized with simultaneous fear, disappeared, skiffs and all, under the water, as if they had been a human variety of the nautilus. The illustrious Don Feijoo has preserved an authentic and well-attested story of a young Spaniard, named Francis de la Vega, who, bathing with some of his friends in June, 1674, suddenly dived under the sea and rose no more. His friends thought him drowned; they were plebeians and pious Catholics; but a philosopher might very legitimately have drawn the same conclusion.

===Chinese===
**阿斯特里亚斯先生.** 千真万确；雄人鱼也有。

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 什么！半人半鱼的东西？

**阿斯特里亚斯先生.** 一点不差。它们就是海里的猩猩（oran-outang）。但我还深信，海里也有十足的海人，同咱们毫无二致，只是愚笨些，浑身披鳞；因为，虽然咱们的构造似乎在本质上把咱们排除在两栖动物之外，可解剖学家们深知，卵圆孔（*foramen ovale*）在成年人身上可能保持开通，那样一来，呼吸之于生命便非必需：不然又怎么解释，采珠场雇的印度潜水人，能在水下一待好几个钟头？又怎么解释，特罗宁霍尔姆那位著名的瑞典园丁，在冰下活了一天半也没淹死？公元 1403 年，一片荷兰湖里捕到过一条海仙女（nereid），或者说美人鱼，她处处像一位法国女人，只是不会说话。十七世纪末，一艘英国船在格陵兰海、离陆一百五十里格的地方，撞见一支由六七十只小划子组成的船队，每只划子里坐着一个雄人鱼，或者说海人：英国船一靠近，他们全体同时惊惶，连人带船一齐没入水下，活像一族人形的鹦鹉螺。显赫的唐·费伊霍（Don Feijoo）保存着一个证据确凿、证人齐全的故事：一个名叫弗朗西斯·德·拉·维加（Francis de la Vega）的西班牙青年，1674 年 6 月同几位朋友洗浴，猛然潜入海中，再没有上来。朋友们以为他淹死了；他们是平民，是虔诚的天主教徒；不过换个哲学家，满可以同样名正言顺地得出这个结论。

===Original===
**The Reverend Mr. Larynx.** Nothing could be more logical.

**Mr. Asterias.** Five years afterwards, some fishermen near Cadiz found in their nets a triton, or sea man; they spoke to him in several languages—

**The Reverend Mr. Larynx.** They were very learned fishermen.

**Mr. Hilary.** They had the gift of tongues by especial favour of their brother fisherman, Saint Peter.

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** Is Saint Peter the tutelar saint of Cadiz?

*None of the company could answer this question, and Mr. Asterias proceeded.*

===Chinese===
**拉林克斯牧师.** 再合逻辑不过了。

**阿斯特里亚斯先生.** 五年后，加的斯附近一些渔夫在网里发现一个雄人鱼，或者说海人；他们用好几种语言同他说话——

**拉林克斯牧师.** 好一班有学问的渔夫。

**希拉里先生.** 他们得了说方言的恩赐，全托他们那渔夫同行圣彼得的格外眷顾。

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 圣彼得是加的斯的主保圣人吗？

*座中无人能答这个问题，阿斯特里亚斯先生继续说。*

===Original===
**Mr. Asterias.** They spoke to him in several languages, but he was as mute as a fish. They handed him over to some holy friars, who exorcised him; but the devil was mute too. After some days he pronounced the name Lierganes. A monk took him to that village. His mother and brothers recognised and embraced him; but he was as insensible to their caresses as any other fish would have been. He had some scales on his body, which dropped off by degrees; but his skin was as hard and rough as shagreen. He stayed at home nine years, without recovering his speech or his reason: he then disappeared again; and one of his old acquaintance, some years after, saw him pop his head out of the water near the coast of the Asturias. These facts were certified by his brothers, and by Don Gaspardo de la Riba Aguero, Knight of Saint James, who lived near Lierganes, and often had the pleasure of our triton’s company to dinner.—Pliny mentions an embassy of the Olyssiponians to Tiberius, to give him intelligence of a triton which had been heard playing on its shell in a certain cave; with several other authenticated facts on the subject of tritons and nereids.

===Chinese===
**阿斯特里亚斯先生.** 他们用好几种语言同他说话，可他哑得像条鱼。他们把他交给几位圣洁的修士，给他行驱魔；可魔鬼也哑。过了几天，他吐出「列尔加内斯」（Lierganes）这个名字。一位修士把他领到那个村子。他的母亲和兄弟认出了他，拥抱他；可他对他们的爱抚毫无知觉，同任何别的鱼毫无二致。他身上有些鳞，渐渐脱落了；可他的皮肤又硬又糙，赛过鲨革。他在家待了九年，言语和理性都没有恢复：随后他又失踪了；几年后，他的一位旧相识看见他在阿斯图里亚斯海岸附近，把头探出水面。这些事实由他的兄弟们、以及住在列尔加内斯附近的圣雅各骑士唐·加斯帕多·德·拉·里巴·阿圭罗（Don Gaspardo de la Riba Aguero, Knight of Saint James）出具证明——这位骑士常有机会请咱们的雄人鱼来家吃饭，引为乐事。——普林尼（Pliny）记过一桩奥利西波人（Olyssiponians，即古里斯本人）派往提比略（Tiberius）处的使团，为的是奏报一头雄人鱼在某个岩洞里吹螺为乐的音讯；此外还有若干件经证实的事实，事关雄人鱼与海仙女。

===Original===
**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** You astonish me. I have been much on the seashore, in the season, but I do not think I ever saw a mermaid. *He rang, and summoned Fatout, who made his appearance half-seas-over.* Fatout! did I ever see a mermaid?

**Fatout.** Mermaid! mer-r-m-m-aid! Ah! merry maid! *Oui, monsieur!* Yes, sir, very many. I vish dere vas von or two here in de kitchen—*ma foi!* Dey be all as melancholic as so many tombstone.

===Chinese===
**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 您叫我惊诧。当令时节，我在海边待得不少，可我想我不曾见过美人鱼。*他摇铃唤来法图，法图露面时已是半醉。* 法图！我见过美人鱼吗？

**法图.** 美人鱼！me-r-r-maid（美人鱼儿）！啊哈！merry maid（快活的姑娘）！*Oui, monsieur*（是的，先生）！见过，先生，见过许许多多。俺倒盼着厨房里能有一两个才好——*ma foi*（我的天）！她们一个个愁眉苦脸，赛过一块块的墓碑。

===Original===
**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** I mean, Fatout, an odd kind of human fish.

**Fatout.** De odd fish! *Ah, oui!* I understand de phrase: ve have seen nothing else since ve left town—*ma foi!*

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** You seem to have a cup too much, sir.

**Fatout.** Non, monsieur: de cup too little. De fen be very unwholesome, and I drink-a-de ponch vid Raven de butler, to keep out de bad air.

===Chinese===
**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 我的意思是，法图，一种古怪的人鱼。

**法图.** 古怪的鱼（odd fish，怪家伙）！*Ah, oui*（啊，是的）！这话俺懂：出了城，咱们见的净是这个——*ma foi*（我的天）！

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 看来您这杯数是多了一杯，先生。

**法图.** 不，先生：是少了一杯。沼泽的瘴气毒得很，俺同总管雷文喝潘趣酒，好把瘴气挡在外头。

===Original===
**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** Fatout! I insist on your being sober.

**Fatout.** *Oui, monsieur*; I vil be as sober as de *révérendissime père Jean*. I should be ver glad of de merry maid; but de butler be de odd fish, and he swim in de bowl de ponch. Ah! ah! I do recollect de leetle-a song:—“About fair maids, and about fair maids, and about my merry maids all.” *Fatout reeled out, singing.*

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** I am overwhelmed: I never saw the rascal in such a condition before. But will you allow me, Mr. Asterias, to inquire into the *cui bono* of all the pains and expense you have incurred to discover a mermaid? The *cui bono*, sir, is the question I always take the liberty to ask when I see anyone taking much trouble for any object. I am myself a sort of Signor Pococurante, and should like to know if there be anything better or pleasanter, than the state of existing and doing nothing?

===Chinese===
**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 法图！我命令你清醒。

**法图.** *Oui, monsieur*（是的，先生）；俺清醒得像*最最可敬的约翰神父*（révérendissime père Jean）。俺倒是真心盼一位快活的姑娘（merry maid）；可总管是条古怪的鱼（odd fish），他泡在潘趣酒碗里游泳。啊！啊！俺倒想起一支小曲儿来了：——「好姑娘啊好姑娘，还有我快快活活的姑娘们全体。」*法图摇晃着身子，一路唱着退了出去。*

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 我算彻底完了：我从没见过这无赖这副模样。不过，阿斯特里亚斯先生，可否容我探问一句：您为寻美人鱼破费的这番辛苦与钱财，究竟 *cui bono*（图个什么）？先生，这个「图什么」，是我一见别人为任何目标大费周章时，照例要冒昧一问的问题。我自己算是个波科库兰特（Signor Pococurante，《老实人》里那位万事不上心的爵爷）一流的人物，很想知道世上还有没有什么比「存在着、并且什么都不干」这种状态更好、更惬意的？

===Original===
**Mr. Asterias.** I have made many voyages, Mr. Listless, to remote and barren shores: I have travelled over desert and inhospitable lands: I have defied danger—I have endured fatigue—I have submitted to privation. In the midst of these I have experienced pleasures which I would not at any time have exchanged for that of existing and doing nothing. I have known many evils, but I have never known the worst of all, which, as it seems to me, are those which are comprehended in the inexhaustible varieties of *ennui*: spleen, chagrin, vapours, blue devils, time-killing, discontent, misanthropy, and all their interminable train of fretfulness, querulousness, suspicions, jealousies, and fears, which have alike infected society, and the literature of society; and which would make an arctic ocean of the human mind, if the more humane pursuits of philosophy and science did not keep alive the better feelings and more valuable energies of our nature.

===Chinese===
**阿斯特里亚斯先生.** 李斯特里斯先生，我做过许多次航行，到过遥远荒凉的海岸；我跋涉过荒漠与不毛之地；我蔑视过危险——忍受过疲劳——甘受过匮乏。就在这些当中，我尝到过种种乐趣，任凭何时，我都不肯拿它们去换「存在着、并且什么都不干」。我见识过许多祸事，可我从未见识过最坏的那一桩——在我看来，那便囊括在倦怠（*ennui*）的无穷品类之中：郁沉、懊恼、忧郁、蓝魔、杀时间、不满、厌世，连同它们那无穷无尽的扈从：烦躁、抱怨、猜疑、嫉妒与恐惧；这些货色把社会、也把社会的文学一并传染了；倘若不是哲学与科学这些更有人情味的追求，还在维系着咱们天性中较好的情感与更可贵的精力，它们早把人类心智变成一片北冰洋了。

===Original===
**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** You are pleased to be severe upon our fashionable *belles lettres*.

**Mr. Asterias.** Surely not without reason, when pirates, highwaymen, and other varieties of the extensive genus Marauder, are the only *beau idéal* of the active, as splenetic and railing misanthropy is of the speculative energy. A gloomy brow and a tragical voice seem to have been of late the characteristics of fashionable manners: and a morbid, withering, deadly, antisocial sirocco, loaded with moral and political despair, breathes through all the groves and valleys of the modern Parnassus; while science moves on in the calm dignity of its course, affording to youth delights equally pure and vivid—to maturity, calm and grateful occupation—to old age, the most pleasing recollections and inexhaustible materials of agreeable and salutary reflection; and, while its votary enjoys the disinterested pleasure of enlarging the intellect and increasing the comforts of society, he is himself independent of the caprices of human intercourse and the accidents of human fortune. Nature is his great and inexhaustible treasure. His days are always too short for his enjoyment: *ennui* is a stranger to his door. At peace with the world and with his own mind, he suffices to himself, makes all around him happy, and the close of his pleasing and beneficial existence is the evening of a beautiful day.6

===Chinese===
**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 您又爱对咱们的时髦纯文学（*belles lettres*）下狠话了。

**阿斯特里亚斯先生.** 决非无因。当海盗、路劫客连同「劫掠者」这一大属的各色品种，成了行动派的唯一*至美典范*（beau idéal），正如阴郁叫骂的厌世是玄思精力的唯一至美典范，这光景岂能不遭针砭。近来，一副阴沉的眉毛、一副悲怆的嗓音，俨然成了时髦风度的标记；一股病态的、摧折生机的、致死的、反社会的热风，载满道德与政治的绝望，吹遍现代帕纳索斯的所有林壑；与此同时，科学却以其航程的静穆庄严前行——它给青春以同样纯粹而鲜明的欢愉——给盛年以宁静而可感的营生——给暮年以最愉快的回忆，以及供惬意而有益之沉思的、永不枯竭的材料；而且，当它的信徒享受着拓展智识、增益社会福祉的无私之乐时，他自己却独立于人际的反复无常与人世荣枯的意外之外。自然是他伟大而不竭的宝库。他的日子对于他的享受，永远太短；倦怠（*ennui*）从不登他的门。他与世界、与自己的心灵相安无事，自给自足，使他身边人人幸福；他这场愉快而有益的存在之落幕，是一日的美丽黄昏。6

===Original===
**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** Really I should like very well to lead such a life myself, but the exertion would be too much for me. Besides, I have been at college. I contrive to get through my day by sinking the morning in bed, and killing the evening in company; dressing and dining in the intermediate space, and stopping the chinks and crevices of the few vacant moments that remain with a little easy reading. And that amiable discontent and antisociality which you reprobate in our present drawing-room-table literature, I find, I do assure you, a very fine mental tonic, which reconciles me to my favourite pursuit of doing nothing, by showing me that nobody is worth doing anything for.

**Marionetta.** But is there not in such compositions a kind of unconscious self-detection, which seems to carry their own antidote with them? For surely no one who cordially and truly either hates or despises the world will publish a volume every three months to say so.

===Chinese===
**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 说真的，这样的日子我自己倒很想过过，无奈那份卖力气我消受不了。再说，我上过大学。我的一天是这样对付过去的：早晨淹死在床上，晚上杀戮在交际里；两头之间穿衣吃饭；剩下那几段空档的缝隙，再拿一点轻松的读物塞严实。至于您所弹劾的、咱们当今客厅案头文学里那可爱的不满与反社交性——我向您担保，我发现它是一味顶好的精神补剂：它指给我看，没有一个人值得为他做什么，于是把我调解得同我心爱的营生——什么都不干——握手言欢。

**玛丽奥内塔.** 可是这一类作品里，难道没有一种不自觉的自我招供，仿佛随身带着自己的解药？因为，凡是真心实意憎恨或鄙弃世界的人，断不会每三个月出版一卷书来宣布这件事。

===Original===
**Mr. Flosky.** There is a secret in all this, which I will elucidate with a dusky remark. According to Berkeley, the *esse* of things is *percipi*. They exist as they are perceived. But, leaving for the present, as far as relates to the material world, the materialists, hyloists, and antihyloists, to settle this point among them, which is indeed

===Chinese===
**弗洛斯基先生.** 这里面有一个秘密，我愿用一句幽暗的评论来阐明。照贝克莱（Berkeley）的说法，万物的*存在*（esse）即*被感知*（percipi）。它们如何被感知，便如何存在。不过，眼下——就物质世界而言——还是让唯物论者、物质论者（hyloists）与反物质论者（antihyloists）自己去解决这一点吧，它确实是——

===Original===
A subtle question, raised among

 

 Those out o’ their wits, and those i’ the wrong

===Chinese===
一个微妙的问题，起于

 

 那些失了心窍的人，与那些理亏的人之间

===Original===
for only we transcendentalists are in the right: we may very safely assert that the *esse* of happiness is *percipi*. It exists as it is perceived. “It is the mind that maketh well or ill.” The elements of pleasure and pain are everywhere. The degree of happiness that any circumstances or objects can confer on us depends on the mental disposition with which we approach them. If you consider what is meant by the common phrases, a happy disposition and a discontented temper, you will perceive that the truth for which I am contending is universally admitted.

===Chinese===
——因为唯有咱们先验论者站在正确一边：我们可以万无一失地断言，幸福的*存在*即*被感知*。它如何被感知，便如何存在。「使人安好或致人病，都在于心。」快乐与痛苦的要素无处不在。任何环境或对象能赐予我们的幸福之程度，全看我们以何种心境去接近它们。您若想一想「乐天性情」与「不满脾气」这些俗语的意思，便会看出我所力争的这条真理，是举世公认的。

===Original===
*Mr. Flosky suddenly stopped: he found himself unintentionally trespassing within the limits of common sense.*

**Mr. Hilary.** It is very true; a happy disposition finds materials of enjoyment everywhere. In the city, or the country—in society, or in solitude—in the theatre, or in the forest—in the hum of the multitude, or in the silence of the mountains, are alike materials of reflection and elements of pleasure. It is one mode of pleasure to listen to the music of *Don Giovanni*, in a theatre glittering with light, and crowded with elegance and beauty: it is another to glide at sunset over the bosom of a lonely lake, where no sound disturbs the silence but the motion of the boat through the waters. A happy disposition derives pleasure from both, a discontented temper from neither, but is always busy in detecting deficiencies, and feeding dissatisfaction with comparisons. The one gathers all the flowers, the other all the nettles, in its path. The one has the faculty of enjoying everything, the other of enjoying nothing. The one realises all the pleasure of the present good; the other converts it into pain, by pining after something better, which is only better because it is not present, and which, if it were present, would not be enjoyed. These morbid spirits are in life what professed critics are in literature; they see nothing but faults, because they are predetermined to shut their eyes to beauties. The critic does his utmost to blight genius in its infancy; that which rises in spite of him he will not see; and then he complains of the decline of literature. In like manner, these cankers of society complain of human nature and society, when they have wilfully debarred themselves from all the good they contain, and done their utmost to blight their own happiness and that of all around them. Misanthropy is sometimes the product of disappointed benevolence; but it is more frequently the offspring of overweening and mortified vanity, quarrelling with the world for not being better treated than it deserves.

===Chinese===
*弗洛斯基先生突然刹住：他发现自己无意间越境，踏进了常识的疆界。*

**希拉里先生.** 这话千真万确；乐天的性情到处都能找到享受的材料。在城中，或在乡间——在社交里，或在孤独中——在剧场里，或在森林里——在人群的嗡嗡声中，或在群山的寂静里，都同样是反思的材料、快乐的元素。在华灯闪烁、雅士美人济济一堂的剧场里，听《唐·乔万尼》的音乐，是一种快乐；在日落时分滑过一座孤湖的湖心，除了船行水面的声音，再无一声惊扰那寂静，是另一种快乐。乐天的性情从两者都得快乐，不满的脾气从两者皆无所得，只忙着侦察缺欠、拿比较喂养不满。一个在路旁采撷百花，一个在路旁采撷荨麻。一个享有享受一切的天赋，一个享有享受一切皆无的天赋。一个把眼前好处的一切快乐坐实；一个把它化作痛苦，只因苦念着某个更好的东西——它之所以更好，只因为它不在眼前；真到了眼前，照样享受不着。这些病态的心灵，在人生中便是职业批评家在文学中：他们只见毛病，因为他们打定主意对美闭上眼。批评家竭尽全力扼杀天才于襁褓；那不顾他阻挠而挺立起来的，他不看；然后他又抱怨文学的衰颓。同样，这些社会的溃疡抱怨人性和社会，其实他们已亲手把自己关在人间一切好处之外，并且竭尽全力，摧折了自己的幸福、也摧折了四周围每个人的幸福。厌世有时是善心失意的产物；但它更常见的是自大受挫的虚荣的孽种——同世界吵架，怪世界没有给它超出它应得的优待。

===Original===
**Scythrop.** *To Marionetta.* These remarks are rather uncharitable. There is great good in human nature, but it is at present ill-conditioned. Ardent spirits cannot but be dissatisfied with things as they are; and, according to their views of the probabilities of amelioration, they will rush into the extremes of either hope or despair—of which the first is enthusiasm, and the second misanthropy; but their sources in this case are the same, as the Severn and the Wye run in different directions, and both rise in Plinlimmon.

**Marionetta.** “And there is salmon in both;” for the resemblance is about as close as that between Macedon and Monmouth.

===Chinese===
**斯克罗普.** *对玛丽奥内塔。* 这些话未免太不厚道。人性中有大善，只是眼下处境不良。热烈的心灵，面对现状势必要不满；而依照他们对改良之可能性的看法，他们会一头扎进希望或绝望的极端——前者是热狂，后者是厌世；但两者在此情形下同出一源，正如塞文河与怀河流向各异，却同发源于普林利蒙山（Plinlimmon）。

**玛丽奥内塔.** 「两条河里也都产鲑鱼」；因为这一对相似的程度，同马其顿与蒙茅斯那一对，实在不相上下。

## VIII / VIII

===Original===
Marionetta observed the next day a remarkable perturbation in Scythrop, for which she could not imagine any probable cause. She was willing to believe at first that it had some transient and trifling source, and would pass off in a day or two; but, contrary to this expectation, it daily increased. She was well aware that Scythrop had a strong tendency to the love of mystery, for its own sake; that is to say, he would employ mystery to serve a purpose, but would first choose his purpose by its capability of mystery. He seemed now to have more mystery on his hands than the laws of the system allowed, and to wear his coat of darkness with an air of great discomfort. All her little playful arts lost by degrees much of their power either to irritate or to soothe; and the first perception of her diminished influence produced in her an immediate depression of spirits, and a consequent sadness of demeanour, that rendered her very interesting to Mr. Glowry; who, duly considering the improbability of accomplishing his wishes with respect to Miss Toobad (which improbability naturally increased in the diurnal ratio of that young lady's absence), began to reconcile himself by degrees to the idea of Marionetta being his daughter.

===Chinese===
次日，玛丽奥内塔便察觉斯克罗普心绪不宁，异乎寻常，而缘由何在，她百思不得其解。起初她乐于相信这不过是某桩一时的小事作祟，过不了一两天自会消散；孰料事与愿违，这心绪竟与日俱增。她深知斯克罗普素来嗜好神秘，且是为神秘而神秘——也就是说，他固然会用神秘去为一个目的效力，却先要看一件事有无弄得神秘的潜力，才来选定目的。眼下他手头的神秘似乎已超出该体系法度所容许的限额，那件黑暗的外衣穿在身上，一副极不自在的模样。她那些小小的戏谑手段，无论撩拨还是抚慰，威力都渐渐失灵；自己的影响力一衰，她当即精神抑郁，举止间也随之染上几分哀愁，这倒使她在格洛里先生眼中显得十分动人。格洛里先生掂量再三，鉴于他有关图巴德小姐的那桩心愿实现之日愈发渺茫（这位小姐离家愈久，渺茫程度自然按日递增），便渐渐回心转意，说服自己接受让玛丽奥内塔做他女儿的念头。

===Original===
Marionetta made many ineffectual attempts to extract from Scythrop the secret of his mystery; and, in despair of drawing it from himself, began to form hopes that she might find a clue to it from Mr. Flosky, who was Scythrop's dearest friend, and was more frequently than any other person admitted to his solitary tower. Mr. Flosky, however, had ceased to be visible in a morning. He was engaged in the composition of a dismal ballad; and, Marionetta's uneasiness overcoming her scruples of decorum, she determined to seek him in the apartment which he had chosen for his study. She tapped at the door, and at the sound "Come in," entered the apartment. It was noon, and the sun was shining in full splendour, much to the annoyance of Mr. Flosky, who had obviated the inconvenience by closing the shutters, and drawing the window-curtains. He was sitting at his table by the light of a solitary candle, with a pen in one hand, and a muffineer in the other, with which he occasionally sprinkled salt on the wick, to make it burn blue. He sat with "his eye in a fine frenzy rolling," and turned his inspired gaze on Marionetta as if she had been the ghastly lady of a magical vision; then placed his hand before his eyes, with an appearance of manifest pain—shook his head—withdrew his hand—rubbed his eyes, like a waking man—and said, in a tone of ruefulness most jeremitaylorically pathetic, "To what am I to attribute this very unexpected pleasure, my dear Miss O'Carroll?"

===Chinese===
玛丽奥内塔多方设法，想从斯克罗普口中套出他那份神秘的底细，皆归无效，绝望之余，转而生出一线希望：或可从弗洛斯基先生那里寻得线索——此公是斯克罗普最亲密的朋友，比任何人都更常获准入他那座孤塔。然而近来，弗洛斯基先生早晨已不复露面。他正埋头创作一支阴郁的歌谣；玛丽奥内塔心神不宁，顾不得体面的顾忌，决意到他选作书房的那间屋子里去找他。她敲了敲门，应声传来一声「进来」，她便走进房间。时当正午，艳阳高照，这大大惹恼了弗洛斯基先生，好在他早有对策：百叶窗关死，窗帘拉严。他就着一支孤烛的光坐在桌前，一手执笔，一手握着一只松饼调料瓶（muffineer），不时往烛芯上撒盐，好让火苗烧出幽蓝色。他坐着，「眼中滚动着美妙的疯狂」，把那灵感沛然的目光投向玛丽奥内塔，仿佛她是魔幻异象中显形的苍白女子；随即抬手遮在眼前，露出明显的痛楚——摇了摇头——放下手来——像个初醒的人那样揉了揉眼睛——然后用一种杰里米·泰勒式（jeremitaylorically）哀婉至极的声调说道：「这份意想不到的荣幸，我该归因于什么呢，亲爱的奥卡罗尔小姐？」

===Original===
**Marionetta.** I must apologise for intruding on you, Mr. Flosky; but the interest which I—you—take in my cousin Scythrop—

**Mr. Flosky.** Pardon me, Miss O'Carroll; I do not take any interest in any person or thing on the face of the earth; which sentiment, if you analyse it, you will find to be the quintessence of the most refined philanthropy.

**Marionetta.** I will take it for granted that it is so, Mr. Flosky; I am not conversant with metaphysical subtleties, but—

**Mr. Flosky.** Subtleties! my dear Miss O'Carroll. I am sorry to find you participating in the vulgar error of the *reading public*, to whom an unusual collocation of words, involving a juxtaposition of antiperistatical ideas, immediately suggests the notion of hyperoxysophistical paradoxology.

===Chinese===
**玛丽奥内塔.** 我冒昧打扰，实在抱歉，弗洛斯基先生；只因那份我——您——对我表兄斯克罗普的关切——

**弗洛斯基先生.** 恕我直言，奥卡罗尔小姐；我对世上的任何人、任何事物都不怀任何关切；这份情操，您若加以分析，便会发现它乃是至为精致的博爱之精粹。

**玛丽奥内塔.** 我姑且当它是如此吧，弗洛斯基先生；玄学的微妙我不在行，可是——

**弗洛斯基先生.** 微妙！我亲爱的奥卡罗尔小姐。很遗憾地发现，您竟也沾染了*阅读公众*的俗见——在那班公众看来，只要词语排列稍异其常、相互对峙的观念彼此并置，便立刻联想到超锐精微的悖论学（hyperoxysophistical paradoxology）。

===Original===
**Marionetta.** Indeed, Mr. Flosky, it suggests no such notion to me. I have sought you for the purpose of obtaining information.

**Mr. Flosky.** *Shaking his head.* No one ever sought me for such a purpose before.

**Marionetta.** I think, Mr. Flosky—that is, I believe—that is, I fancy—that is, I imagine—

**Mr. Flosky.** The *τουτεστι*, the *id est*, the *cioè*, the *c'est à dire*, the *that is*, my dear Miss O'Carroll, is not applicable in this case—if you will permit me to take the liberty of saying so. Think is not synonymous with believe—for belief, in many most important particulars, results from the total absence, the absolute negation of thought, and is thereby the sane and orthodox condition of mind; and thought and belief are both essentially different from fancy, and fancy, again, is distinct from imagination. This distinction between fancy and imagination is one of the most abstruse and important points of metaphysics. I have written seven hundred pages of promise to elucidate it, which promise I shall keep as faithfully as the bank will its promise to pay.

===Chinese===
**玛丽奥内塔.** 真的，弗洛斯基先生，这类名堂我可一概联想不到。我来找您，是为了讨一点信息。

**弗洛斯基先生.** *摇着头.* 从前还不曾有谁为这个目的来找过我。

**玛丽奥内塔.** 我想，弗洛斯基先生——也就是说，我相信——也就是说，我幻想——也就是说，我想象——

**弗洛斯基先生.** *τουτεστι*、*id est*、*cioè*、*c'est à dire*、「也就是说」，亲爱的奥卡罗尔小姐，在此场合一概不适用——若蒙俯允，请容我冒昧直言。想与信并非同义——因为信仰在许多至为紧要的关节上，恰恰源于思考之全然缺席、之绝对否定，因而乃是心智健全而正统的状态；而思考与信仰又都本质地有别于幻想，幻想复又与想象判然不同。幻想与想象之别，乃玄学中最深奥、最紧要的论题之一。我已立下七百页的诺言来阐明它，这诺言我必将信守，其忠实程度，一如银行信守它付款的承诺。

===Original===
**Marionetta.** I assure you, Mr. Flosky, I care no more about metaphysics than I do about the bank; and, if you will condescend to talk to a simple girl in intelligible terms—

**Mr. Flosky.** Say not condescend! Know you not that you talk to the most humble of men, to one who has buckled on the armour of sanctity, and clothed himself with humility as with a garment?

**Marionetta.** My cousin Scythrop has of late had an air of mystery about him, which gives me great uneasiness.

**Mr. Flosky.** That is strange: nothing is so becoming to a man as an air of mystery. Mystery is the very keystone of all that is beautiful in poetry, all that is sacred in faith, and all that is recondite in transcendental psychology. I am writing a ballad which is all mystery; it is "such stuff as dreams are made of," and is, indeed, stuff made of a dream; for, last night I fell asleep as usual over my book, and had a vision of pure reason. I composed five hundred lines in my sleep; so that, having had a dream of a ballad, I am now officiating as my own Peter Quince, and making a ballad of my dream, and it shall be called Bottom's Dream, because it has no bottom.

===Chinese===
**玛丽奥内塔.** 我向您保证，弗洛斯基先生，我对玄学的那点关切，并不比我对那家银行的更多；您若肯屈尊，用听得懂的话跟一个头脑简单的姑娘谈谈——

**弗洛斯基先生.** 休说屈尊！您可知道您是在同世上最谦卑的人说话？此人早已扣上圣洁的铠甲，以谦卑为衣裳，披挂齐整。

**玛丽奥内塔.** 我表兄斯克罗普近来周身带着一股神秘气息，令我不安得很。

**弗洛斯基先生.** 那可就怪了：神秘气息乃男子最相宜的装束。神秘，正是诗歌中一切之美、信仰中一切之圣、先验心理学中一切之奥的拱心石。我正在写一支通篇神秘的歌谣；它是「构成梦的那种材料」，也确乎是用一场梦造出的材料；因为昨夜我照例对着书本睡去，得了一个纯粹理性的幻象。我在睡梦中作了五百行诗；于是，既已做过一支歌谣的梦，我如今便亲自充任自己的彼得·昆斯（Peter Quince），用我的梦造一支歌谣，它将名为《波顿之梦》（Bottom's Dream），因为它全无底。

===Original===
**Marionetta.** I see, Mr. Flosky, you think my intrusion unseasonable, and are inclined to punish it, by talking nonsense to me. *Mr. Flosky gave a start at the word nonsense, which almost overturned the table.* I assure you, I would not have intruded if I had not been very much interested in the question I wish to ask you.—*Mr. Flosky listened in sullen dignity.*—My cousin Scythrop seems to have some secret preying on his mind.—*Mr. Flosky was silent.*—He seems very unhappy—Mr. Flosky.—Perhaps you are acquainted with the cause.—*Mr. Flosky was still silent.*—I only wish to know—Mr. Flosky—if it is anything—that could be remedied by anything—that anyone—of whom I know anything—could do.

**Mr. Flosky.** *After a pause.* There are various ways of getting at secrets. The most approved methods, as recommended both theoretically and practically in philosophical novels, are eavesdropping at keyholes, picking the locks of chests and desks, peeping into letters, steaming wafers, and insinuating hot wire under sealing wax; none of which methods I hold it lawful to practise.

**Marionetta.** Surely, Mr. Flosky, you cannot suspect me of wishing to adopt or encourage such base and contemptible arts.

**Mr. Flosky.** Yet are they recommended, and with well-strung reasons, by writers of gravity and note, as simple and easy methods of studying character, and gratifying that laudable curiosity which aims at the knowledge of man.

===Chinese===
**玛丽奥内塔.** 我明白了，弗洛斯基先生，您是觉得我来得不是时候，有意拿胡说八道来惩罚我。*听到「胡说八道」四字，弗洛斯基先生惊得一跳，险些掀翻桌子。*我向您保证，若不是我对要问的问题关切至深，我是决不会来打扰您的。——*弗洛斯基先生阴沉而威严地听着。*——我表兄斯克罗普心里似乎压着一桩秘密。——*弗洛斯基先生沉默不语。*——他看上去很不快活——弗洛斯基先生。——或许您知道其中缘由。——*弗洛斯基先生依旧沉默。*——我只想知道——弗洛斯基先生——那究竟是不是——什么可以用什么办法——由随便哪一位——我所知道底细的人——来补救的事。

**弗洛斯基先生.** *顿了一顿.* 打探秘密的办法有多种。哲学小说中理论与实践双重推荐的种种最可称许的法子是：贴着钥匙孔偷听，撬开箱柜与书桌的锁，窥看信件，用蒸汽熏开胶封，把烧红的铁丝从火漆底下悄悄探入——这些法子，没有一种是我认为可以合法采用的。

**玛丽奥内塔.** 当然啦，弗洛斯基先生，您总不至于疑心我想采用、或想怂恿这类卑鄙可鄙的伎俩吧。

**弗洛斯基先生.** 然而它们确乎受到一些庄重知名的作者们振振有词的推荐，奉为研究性格的简便法门，用以满足那种以认识人为鹄的的可嘉好奇心。

===Original===
**Marionetta.** I am as ignorant of this morality which you do not approve, as of the metaphysics which you do: I should be glad to know by your means, what is the matter with my cousin; I do not like to see him unhappy, and I suppose there is some reason for it.

**Mr. Flosky.** Now I should rather suppose there is no reason for it: it is the fashion to be unhappy. To have a reason for being so would be exceedingly commonplace: to be so without any is the province of genius: the art of being miserable for misery's sake, has been brought to great perfection in our days; and the ancient Odyssey, which held forth a shining example of the endurance of real misfortune, will give place to a modern one, setting out a more instructive picture of querulous impatience under imaginary evils.

**Marionetta.** Will you oblige me, Mr. Flosky, by giving me a plain answer to a plain question?

**Mr. Flosky.** It is impossible, my dear Miss O'Carroll. I never gave a plain answer to a question in my life.

===Chinese===
**玛丽奥内塔.** 您不以为然的这套道德，与您以为然的那套玄学，于我同样陌生；我倒乐意借您之力，弄明白我表兄到底怎么了；我不愿见他快快不乐，而这想必总有个缘由。

**弗洛斯基先生.** 我倒宁愿设想其中并无缘由：不快活乃是时下的风气。不快活还须有个缘由，未免太平庸；无缘无故而不快活，方是天才的本分：为愁苦而愁苦的艺术，在我们这个时代已臻于至善；古代的《奥德赛》既树立了忍受真实灾祸的光辉典范，自将让位于一部现代《奥德赛》，为我们描出一幅更富教益的图画：在虚构的祸殃之下怨天尤人、全无耐心。

**玛丽奥内塔.** 弗洛斯基先生，可否劳您大驾，对一个直截了当的问题，给一个直截了当的回答？

**弗洛斯基先生.** 不可能，我亲爱的奥卡罗尔小姐。我平生从未对任何问题给过一个直截了当的回答。

===Original===
**Marionetta.** Do you, or do you not, know what is the matter with my cousin?

**Mr. Flosky.** To say that I do not know, would be to say that I am ignorant of something; and God forbid, that a transcendental metaphysician, who has pure anticipated cognitions of everything, and carries the whole science of geometry in his head without ever having looked into Euclid, should fall into so empirical an error as to declare himself ignorant of anything: to say that I do know, would be to pretend to positive and circumstantial knowledge touching present matter of fact, which, when you consider the nature of evidence, and the various lights in which the same thing may be seen—

**Marionetta.** I see, Mr. Flosky, that either you have no information, or are determined not to impart it; and I beg your pardon for having given you this unnecessary trouble.

**Mr. Flosky.** My dear Miss O'Carroll, it would have given me great pleasure to have said anything that would have given you pleasure; but if any person living could make report of having obtained any information on any subject from Ferdinando Flosky, my transcendental reputation would be ruined forever.

===Chinese===
**玛丽奥内塔.** 您到底知不知道，我表兄究竟怎么了？

**弗洛斯基先生.** 若说我不知道，便等于说我于某事无知；一位对万事怀有纯粹先在的认知、脑袋里装着整门几何学却从未翻开过欧几里得一眼的先验形而上学家，竟堕入如此经验主义的谬误，自称对任何事物无知——上天不容；若说我知道，便等于声称自己握有关于当下事实的正面而详明的知识——而您只要考量一下证据的本性，以及同一事物可以被看见的种种不同光线——

**玛丽奥内塔.** 我明白了，弗洛斯基先生，您要么毫不知情，要么打定主意不予奉告；无缘无故劳您这一场，请您恕罪。

**弗洛斯基先生.** 我亲爱的奥卡罗尔小姐，若能说一句令您开心的话，本会给我莫大的快乐；然而世上倘有任何一个活人能出来报告，说他曾从费迪南多·弗洛斯基嘴里问出过任何题目的任何信息，我的先验派名声便永远毁掉了。

## IX / IX

===Original===
Scythrop grew every day more reserved, mysterious, and distrait; and gradually lengthened the duration of his diurnal seclusions in his tower. Marionetta thought she perceived in all this very manifest symptoms of a warm love cooling.

===Chinese===
斯克罗普一天比一天更深藏、更神秘、更心不在焉；他每日在塔中隐居的时辰也逐渐拉长。玛丽奥内塔觉得，这一切透出再明白不过的症候：一腔热恋正在冷却。

===Original===
It was seldom that she found herself alone with him in the morning, and, on these occasions, if she was silent in the hope of his speaking first, not a syllable would he utter; if she spoke to him indirectly, he assented monosyllabically; if she questioned him, his answers were brief, constrained, and evasive. Still, though her spirits were depressed, her playfulness had not so totally forsaken her, but that it illuminated at intervals the gloom of Nightmare Abbey; and if, on any occasion, she observed in Scythrop tokens of unextinguished or returning passion, her love of tormenting her lover immediately got the better both of her grief and her sympathy, though not of her curiosity, which Scythrop seemed determined not to satisfy. This playfulness, however, was in a great measure artificial, and usually vanished with the irritable Strephon, to whose annoyance it had been exerted. The Genius Loci, the *tutela* of Nightmare Abbey, the spirit of black melancholy, began to set his seal on her pallescent countenance. Scythrop perceived the change, found his tender sympathies awakened, and did his utmost to comfort the afflicted damsel, assuring her that his seeming inattention had only proceeded from his being involved in a profound meditation on a very hopeful scheme for the regeneration of human society. Marionetta called him ungrateful, cruel, cold-hearted, and accompanied her reproaches with many sobs and tears; poor Scythrop growing every moment more soft and submissive—till, at length, he threw himself at her feet, and declared that no competition of beauty, however dazzling, genius, however transcendent, talents, however cultivated, or philosophy, however enlightened, should ever make him renounce his divine Marionetta.

===Chinese===
上午她难得与他单独相处；偶有这种时候，她若存着让他先开口的指望而保持沉默，他便一个音节也不吐；她若绕着弯子同他说话，他便单音节地应一声；她若直接发问，他的回答便简短、拘谨、躲躲闪闪。虽说如此，她的兴致固然低落，那点戏谑之心却尚未全然舍她而去，还不时照亮噩梦隐修院的阴郁；而一旦她在斯克罗普身上窥见余情未了或旧情复燃的迹象，她那折磨情郎的爱好便立刻压倒了她的悲伤与怜惜——只是压不倒她的好奇，而这份好奇，斯克罗普似乎打定主意不予满足。然而这份戏谑大半是勉强装出来的，通常随着那位易怒的斯垂丰（Strephon）的恼怒而消散——它本来就是用来恼他的。噩梦隐修院的地方神（Genius Loci）、它的守护灵（*tutela*）、黑色忧郁的精灵，开始在她日渐苍白的面容上打下印记。斯克罗普察觉了这变化，柔情的怜惜被唤醒，竭力安慰这位忧伤的少女，向她保证说，他看似的心不在焉，只因他正沉浸于一项大有希望的人类社会改造方案的深思之中。玛丽奥内塔骂他负心、狠毒、没心肝，责备声里夹着阵阵呜咽与眼泪；可怜的斯克罗普一分一秒地愈发温软、愈发驯顺——终于，他扑倒在她脚下，宣称任凭怎样炫目的美貌、怎样超凡的天才、怎样修炼的才艺、怎样开明的哲学前来相争，都休想使他背弃他神圣的玛丽奥内塔。

===Original===
"Competition!" thought Marionetta, and suddenly, with an air of the most freezing indifference, she said, "You are perfectly at liberty, sir, to do as you please; I beg you will follow your own plans, without any reference to me."

Scythrop was confounded. What was become of all her passion and her tears? Still kneeling, he kissed her hand with rueful timidity, and said, in most pathetic accents, "Do you not love me, Marionetta?"

"No," said Marionetta, with a look of cold composure: "No." Scythrop still looked up incredulously. "No, I tell you."

"Oh! very well, madam," said Scythrop, rising, "if that is the case, there are those in the world—"

===Chinese===
「争！」玛丽奥内塔心里暗想。忽然，她摆出一副冷到冰点的漠然神气，说道：「您完全有自由，先生，爱怎么做就怎么做；我请您按您自己的计划行事，不必有半分顾到我。」

斯克罗普被这一下打懵了。她的满腔激情和眼泪都到哪里去了？他仍旧跪着，惶惶然怯怯地吻她的手，用最凄楚的声调说：「你难道不爱我了吗，玛丽奥内塔？」

「不爱。」玛丽奥内塔说，面上一副冷静的沉着：「不爱。」斯克罗普仍旧仰望着她，一脸难以置信。「不爱，我告诉你。」

「哦！很好，小姐，」斯克罗普说着站起身来，「既然如此，这世上自有——」

===Original===
"To be sure there are, sir;—and do you suppose I do not see through your designs, you ungenerous monster?"

"My designs? Marionetta!"

"Yes, your designs, Scythrop. You have come here to cast me off, and artfully contrive that it should appear to be my doing, and not yours, thinking to quiet your tender conscience with this pitiful stratagem. But do not suppose that you are of so much consequence to me: do not suppose it: you are of no consequence to me at all—none at all: therefore, leave me: I renounce you: leave me; why do you not leave me?"

===Chinese===
「当然自有，先生；——您还当我看不透您的心思不成，您这个薄情的怪物？」

「我的心思？玛丽奥内塔！」

「是的，就是您的心思，斯克罗普。您到这儿来，为的是把我甩掉，还处心积虑地安排好，让这事看起来是我的主意，不是您的，指望用这个可怜的伎俩安抚您那颗软弱的良心。可是您别以为您在我心里就有那么要紧：别这么以为：您在我心里一点也不要紧——一点也不要紧：所以，走开：我把您甩了：走开；您怎么还不走？」

===Original===
Scythrop endeavoured to remonstrate, but without success. She reiterated her injunctions to him to leave her, till, in the simplicity of his spirit, he was preparing to comply. When he had nearly reached the door, Marionetta said, "Farewell." Scythrop looked back. "Farewell, Scythrop," she repeated, "you will never see me again."

"Never see you again, Marionetta?"

"I shall go from hence tomorrow, perhaps today; and before we meet again, one of us will be married, and we might as well be dead, you know, Scythrop."

The sudden change of her voice in the last few words, and the burst of tears that accompanied them, acted like electricity on the tenderhearted youth; and, in another instant, a complete reconciliation was accomplished without the intervention of words.

===Chinese===
斯克罗普想要辩解，却全然无效。她翻来覆去地喝令他走开，直到他那颗单纯的心当真准备从命。他快走到门口时，玛丽奥内塔说：「别了。」斯克罗普回头张望。「别了，斯克罗普，」她重复道，「你再也见不到我了。」

「再也见不到你了，玛丽奥内塔？」

「我明天就离开这儿，也许今天就走；等我们再见的时候，两人中总有一个已经结婚了，那你我之间，就跟死了一样，你知道的，斯克罗普。」

最后几个字里她嗓音的骤变，以及随之夺眶而出的泪水，像电流一般击中了这位心软的青年；转瞬之间，一场彻底的和解便告完成，全然无须言语居间斡旋。

===Original===
There are, indeed, some learned casuists, who maintain that love has no language, and that all the misunderstandings and dissensions of lovers arise from the fatal habit of employing words on a subject to which words are inapplicable; that love, beginning with looks, that is to say, with the physiognomical expression of congenial mental dispositions, tends through a regular gradation of signs and symbols of affection, to that consummation which is most devoutly to be wished; and that it neither is necessary that there should be, nor probable that there would be, a single word spoken from first to last between two sympathetic spirits, were it not that the arbitrary institutions of society have raised, at every step of this very simple process, so many complicated impediments and barriers in the shape of settlements and ceremonies, parents and guardians, lawyers, Jew-brokers, and parsons, that many an adventurous knight (who, in order to obtain the conquest of the Hesperian fruit, is obliged to fight his way through all these monsters), is either repulsed at the onset, or vanquished before the achievement of his enterprise: and such a quantity of unnatural talking is rendered inevitably necessary through all the stages of the progression, that the tender and volatile spirit of love often takes flight on the pinions of some of the *επεα πτεροεντα*, or *winged words* which are pressed into his service in despite of himself.

===Chinese===
诚然，有几位博学的决疑学者主张：爱情没有语言；恋人之间的一切误会与龃龉，皆源于那个致命的习惯——在一个语言无能为力的话题上使用语言；爱情始于目光，也就是说，始于心智气质相投在面容上的流露，继而经由亲昵符号与表征的规则递进，趋向那桩最虔诚祈望的圆满；而且，两个情投意合的灵魂之间，本不需要、也未必会从头到尾说出一个字——若不是社会的种种武断建制，在这段极简单历程的每一步上都设下重重复杂的障碍与壁垒，其形制名曰财产协议与婚礼仪节、父母与监护人、律师、犹太掮客和牧师，害得多少冒险的骑士（为摘取赫斯珀里亚的金果，他必须一路斩杀这些妖物）或是出师便遭击退，或是功业未成先自败亡：而在这整个进程的各个阶段，又不可避免地非说这么多违反天性的话不可，以致爱情那温柔而轻浮的精灵，往往就乘着几句被迫为它服役的*επεα πτεροεντα*（有翼之言），振翅飞去。

===Original===
At this conjuncture, Mr. Glowry entered, and sitting down near them, said, "I see how it is; and, as we are all sure to be miserable do what we may, there is no need of taking pains to make one another more so; therefore, with God's blessing and mine, there"—joining their hands as he spoke.

Scythrop was not exactly prepared for this decisive step; but he could only stammer out, "Really, sir, you are too good;" and Mr. Glowry departed to bring Mr. Hilary to ratify the act.

Now, whatever truth there may be in the theory of love and language, of which we have so recently spoken, certain it is, that during Mr. Glowry's absence, which lasted half an hour, not a single word was said by either Scythrop or Marionetta.

===Chinese===
正当此时，格洛里先生走了进来，在他们身旁坐下，说道：「我看明白了；反正我们怎么做都注定要痛苦，也就犯不上再费心让彼此更痛苦；所以，托上帝的福，也托我的福，你们俩」——说着便拉起两人的手合在一处。

斯克罗普对这决定性的一步并无准备；却只能结结巴巴地说：「真的，先生，您太好了；」格洛里先生便动身去请希拉里先生来把这件事敲定。

且不论我们方才谈到的这套爱情与语言的理论含几分真理，有一点是确定无疑的：格洛里先生离开的这半个钟头里，斯克罗普和玛丽奥内塔谁也没有说出一个字。

===Original===
Mr. Glowry returned with Mr. Hilary, who was delighted at the prospect of so advantageous an establishment for his orphan niece, of whom he considered himself in some manner the guardian, and nothing remained, as Mr. Glowry observed, but to fix the day.

Marionetta blushed, and was silent. Scythrop was also silent for a time, and at length hesitatingly said, "My dear sir, your goodness overpowers me; but really you are so precipitate."

===Chinese===
格洛里先生带着希拉里先生回来了。希拉里先生眼见他这位孤女外甥女前景如此美满，喜不自胜——他多少以她的监护人自居——而且，正如格洛里先生所说，如今万事俱备，只待择定吉日了。

玛丽奥内塔红了脸，默然无语。斯克罗普也沉默了一阵，终于吞吞吐吐地说：「亲爱的先生，您的好意叫我消受不起；可您实在也太性急了。」

===Original===
Now, this remark, if the young lady had made it, would, whether she thought it or not—for sincerity is a thing of no account on these occasions, nor indeed on any other, according to Mr. Flosky—this remark, if the young lady had made it, would have been perfectly *comme il faut*; but, being made by the young gentleman, it was *toute autre chose*, and was, indeed, in the eyes of his mistress, a most heinous and irremissible offence. Marionetta was angry, very angry, but she concealed her anger, and said, calmly and coldly, "Certainly, you are much too precipitate, Mr. Glowry. I assure you, sir, I have by no means made up my mind; and, indeed, as far as I know it, it inclines the other way; but it will be quite time enough to think of these matters seven years hence." Before surprise permitted reply, the young lady had locked herself up in her own apartment.

===Chinese===
不过，这句话若出自那位小姐之口，无论她心里想不想——因为按照弗洛斯基先生的高见，真诚在这种场合根本不算一回事，在任何别的场合其实也一样——这句话若出自那位小姐之口，便会*comme il faut*（合乎分寸）之至；可它偏偏出自那位先生之口，那就是*toute autre chose*（全然另一回事）了，而且，在他的意中人眼里，实在是一桩罪大恶极、无可宽贷的过犯。玛丽奥内塔恼了，恼得很，却把怒气藏住，冷静而冷淡地说：「当然，您确实太性急了，格洛里先生。我向您保证，先生，我的心意还远远没有拿定；而且，就我对自己心意的了解，它还偏向另一头；不过，这些事等到七年之后再考虑，也还绰绰有余。」不等对方从惊讶中回过神来作答，那位小姐已经把自己锁进了自己的房间。

===Original===
"Why, Scythrop," said Mr. Glowry, elongating his face exceedingly, "the devil is come among us sure enough, as Mr. Toobad observes: I thought you and Marionetta were both of a mind."

"So we are, I believe, sir," said Scythrop, gloomily, and stalked away to his tower.

"Mr. Glowry," said Mr. Hilary, "I do not very well understand all this."

===Chinese===
「哎呀，斯克罗普，」格洛里先生说，脸拉得老长，「魔鬼确确实实已经来到我们中间了，正如图巴德先生常说的那样：我还以为你和玛丽奥内塔是一条心来着。」

「我想是一条心，先生。」斯克罗普阴郁地说，大步流星地回他的塔里去了。

「格洛里先生，」希拉里先生说，「这出戏我实在看不很懂。」

===Original===
"Whims, brother Hilary," said Mr. Glowry; "some little foolish love quarrel, nothing more. Whims, freaks, April showers. They will be blown over by tomorrow."

"If not," said Mr. Hilary, "these April showers have made us April fools."

"Ah!" said Mr. Glowry, "you are a happy man, and in all your afflictions you can console yourself with a joke, let it be ever so bad, provided you crack it yourself. I should be very happy to laugh with you, if it would give you any satisfaction; but, really, at present, my heart is so sad, that I find it impossible to levy a contribution on my muscles."

===Chinese===
「心血来潮罢了，希拉里兄弟，」格洛里先生说；「小两口闹点愚蠢的情别扭，仅此而已。心血来潮、一时任性、四月的阵雨。到明天就都风吹云散了。」

「要是吹不散，」希拉里先生说，「这阵四月雨可就把我们浇成四月傻瓜了。」

「啊！」格洛里先生说，「你是个有福气的人，无论遭多大的难，都能拿一个玩笑自我宽慰，哪怕那玩笑再糟，只要是你自己说出来的就行。要是能叫你称心，我很乐意陪你笑一笑；可是说真的，这会儿我心里太苦了，实在没法向我的肌肉课一分税。」

## X / X

===Original===
On the evening on which Mr. Asterias had caught a glimpse of a female figure on the seashore, which he had translated into the visual sign of his interior cognition of a mermaid, Scythrop, retiring to his tower, found his study pre-occupied. A stranger, muffled in a cloak, was sitting at his table. Scythrop paused in surprise. The stranger rose at his entrance, and looked at him intently a few minutes, in silence. The eyes of the stranger alone were visible. All the rest of the figure was muffled and mantled in the folds of a black cloak, which was raised, by the right hand, to the level of the eyes. This scrutiny being completed, the stranger, dropping the cloak, said, "I see, by your physiognomy, that you may be trusted;" and revealed to the astonished Scythrop a female form and countenance of dazzling grace and beauty, with long flowing hair of raven blackness, and large black eyes of almost oppressive brilliancy, which strikingly contrasted with a complexion of snowy whiteness. Her dress was extremely elegant, but had an appearance of foreign fashion, as if both the lady and her mantua-maker were of "a far countree."

> "I guess 'twas frightful there to see

 A lady so richly clad as she,

 Beautiful exceedingly."

===Chinese===
在阿斯特里亚斯先生于海岸边瞥见一个女子身影、并将之解读为他内在认知中美人鱼的视觉符号的那个晚上，斯克罗普回到他的塔里，发现书房已被占。一个裹着斗篷的陌生人坐在他的桌前。斯克罗普惊愕地停住脚步。陌生人见他进来便起身，一言不发地凝视了他几分钟。只见那陌生人一双眼睛：周身其余的部分全裹藏、笼罩在一袭黑斗篷的褶层里，右手把斗篷一直提到眼际。这番审视完毕，陌生人放下斗篷，说道：「我从你的面相看出，你是个可以信托的人；」随即向惊愕不已的斯克罗普显出一位女子的身形与面容，风姿焕采，美艳夺目：一头长垂的鸦黑秀发，一双大黑眼睛亮得几乎逼人，与她雪白的肤色恰成醒目的对照。她的衣装极其雅致，却带着外国时样的气派，仿佛这位女士和她的女装裁缝都来自「遥远的国度」（a far countree）。

> 「我猜，在那里望见
> 一位衣饰如此华贵的女子，
> 美得超凡入圣，想必骇人。」

===Original===
For, if it be terrible to one young lady to find another under a tree at midnight, it must, *a fortiori*, be much more terrible to a young gentleman to find a young lady in his study at that hour. If the logical consecutiveness of this conclusion be not manifest to my readers, I am sorry for their dullness, and must refer them, for more ample elucidation, to a treatise which Mr. Flosky intends to write, on the Categories of Relation, which comprehend Substance and Accident, Cause and Effect, Action and Reaction.

Scythrop, therefore, either was or ought to have been frightened; at all events, he was astonished; and astonishment, though not in itself fear, is nevertheless a good stage towards it, and is, indeed, as it were, the halfway house between respect and terror, according to Mr. Burke's graduated scale of the sublime.7

===Chinese===
因为，倘若一位小姐在午夜的大树底下遇见另一位小姐是可怕的，那么，就更不消说得（*a fortiori*），一位年轻绅士在那个时辰于自己书房里遇见一位小姐，便必定可怕得多。倘若这一结论的逻辑连贯性在读者诸君眼中还不够显豁，我只好为诸君的鲁钝惋惜，并请诸君移步参阅弗洛斯基先生有意撰写的一部论「关系的范畴」的专著——该范畴涵盖实体与偶性、原因与结果、作用与反作用——以求更充分的阐明。

因此，斯克罗普要么吓着了，要么本该吓着；无论如何，他是惊愕了；而惊愕虽然本身不是恐惧，却是通向恐惧的良好台阶，而且，依照伯克先生那把崇高的分级标尺7，实可谓敬意与恐怖之间的半路客栈。

===Original===
"You are surprised," said the lady; "yet why should you be surprised? If you had met me in a drawing-room, and I had been introduced to you by an old woman, it would have been a matter of course: can the division of two or three walls, and the absence of an unimportant personage, make the same object essentially different in the perception of a philosopher?"

"Certainly not," said Scythrop; "but when any class of objects has habitually presented itself to our perceptions in invariable conjunction with particular relations, then, on the sudden appearance of one object of the class divested of those accompaniments, the essential difference of the relation is, by an involuntary process, transferred to the object itself, which thus offers itself to our perceptions with all the strangeness of novelty."

"You are a philosopher," said the lady, "and a lover of liberty. You are the author of a treatise, called *Philosophical Gas; or, a Project for a General Illumination of the Human Mind*."

"I am," said Scythrop, delighted at this first blossom of his renown.

===Chinese===
「你是吃惊了，」女士说；「可你何必吃惊？倘若你在客厅里遇见我，由一位老太太引见给你，那便是理所当然的事：难道隔开两三堵墙、又缺了一位无关紧要的人物，就能使同一对象在一位哲学家的知觉里发生本质的不同？」

「当然不能，」斯克罗普说；「但是，当某一类对象一向总是与某些特定关系一成不变地一同呈现在我们的知觉之中，那么，该类中有一个对象忽然剥离了这些伴随物而单独出现，关系上的本质差异便会经由一种不由自主的过程，转移到对象本身之上，于是它便带着新奇事物的全部陌生感，呈现在我们的知觉面前。」

「你是位哲学家，」女士说，「又是自由的热爱者。你是一部论著的作者，题为《哲学瓦斯：或，人类心智普遍照明方略》。」

「正是，」斯克罗普说，他的声名初绽第一朵花，不由得心花怒放。

===Original===
"I am a stranger in this country," said the lady; "I have been but a few days in it, yet I find myself immediately under the necessity of seeking refuge from an atrocious persecution. I had no friend to whom I could apply; and, in the midst of my difficulties, accident threw your pamphlet in my way. I saw that I had, at least, one kindred mind in this nation, and determined to apply to you."

"And what would you have me do?" said Scythrop, more and more amazed, and not a little perplexed.

"I would have you," said the young lady, "assist me in finding some place of retreat, where I can remain concealed from the indefatigable search that is being made for me. I have been so nearly caught once or twice already, that I cannot confide any longer in my own ingenuity."

===Chinese===
「我初来这个国度，」女士说；「到此不过几天，却发觉自己立刻就有必要寻一处庇护，以躲避一场凶残的迫害。我没有可以求助的朋友；正当进退维谷之际，偶然间你的小册子落到我手里。我看出来，至少在这个国度里，我还有一个同心同德的心灵，便决意来求你。」

「那你要我做什么呢？」斯克罗普说，愈来愈惊，又颇觉狼狈。

「我要你，」少女说，「帮我寻一处退隐之地，好让我躲开那场为搜我而进行的不倦搜寻。我已经有一两次险些被捉住，再不能单信我自己的机智了。」

===Original===
Doubtless, thought Scythrop, this is one of my golden candlesticks. "I have constructed," said he, "in this tower, an entrance to a small suite of unknown apartments in the main building, which I defy any creature living to detect. If you would like to remain there a day or two, till I can find you a more suitable concealment, you may rely on the honour of a transcendental eleutherarch."

"I rely on myself," said the lady. "I act as I please, go where I please, and let the world say what it will. I am rich enough to set it at defiance. It is the tyrant of the poor and the feeble, but the slave of those who are above the reach of its injury."

Scythrop ventured to inquire the name of his fair protégé. "What is a name?" said the lady: "any name will serve the purpose of distinction. Call me Stella. I see by your looks," she added, "that you think all this very strange. When you know me better, your surprise will cease. I submit not to be an accomplice in my sex's slavery. I am, like yourself, a lover of freedom, and I carry my theory into practice. 'They alone are subject to blind authority who have no reliance on their own strength.'"

===Chinese===
毫无疑问，斯克罗普心想，这是我的一座金烛台了。「我在这座塔里，」他说，「造了一条通道，通向主楼里一小套无人知晓的房间，我倒要看看世上有什么活人能识破它。您若愿意在那里暂住一两日，等我去给您觅一处更合适的藏身之所，您可以信赖一位先验的自由执政官的信义。」

「我只信赖我自己，」女士说。「我爱怎么做就怎么做，爱去哪里就去哪里，随世人爱说什么说什么。我的富有，足以向它挑战。它是穷人与弱者的暴君，却是那些它伤不着的人的奴仆。」

斯克罗普斗胆请问这位受他保护的美人的名姓。「名字算什么？」女士说：「任何名字都足以供彼此区分。就叫我斯特拉吧。我从你的神色看得出，」她补充道，「你觉得这一切非常蹊跷。等你更了解我，你就不会惊讶了。我不肯充当我的性别受奴役的同谋。我和你一样热爱自由，并且把我的理论付诸实践。『唯有不信赖自身力量的人，才屈服于盲目的权威。』」

===Original===
Stella took possession of the recondite apartments. Scythrop intended to find her another asylum; but from day to day he postponed his intention, and by degrees forgot it. The young lady reminded him of it from day to day, till she also forgot it. Scythrop was anxious to learn her history; but she would add nothing to what she had already communicated, that she was shunning an atrocious persecution. Scythrop thought of Lord C. and the Alien Act, and said, "As you will not tell your name, I suppose it is in the green bag." Stella, not understanding what he meant, was silent; and Scythrop, translating silence into acquiescence, concluded that he was sheltering an *illuminée* whom Lord S. suspected of an intention to take the Tower, and set fire to the Bank: exploits, at least, as likely to be accomplished by the hands and eyes of a young beauty, as by a drunken cobbler and doctor, armed with a pamphlet and an old stocking.

===Chinese===
斯特拉住进了那套秘室。斯克罗普原打算给她另觅一处庇护所；但他把这事一日推一日，渐渐也就忘了。少女一日又一日地提醒他，直到她自己也忘了。斯克罗普极想了解她的来历；可除了她已经说过的那句——她在躲避一场凶残的迫害——之外，她什么也不肯再添。斯克罗普想起C——勋爵和《外国人法案》（the Alien Act），便说：「您既然不肯说出名姓，想必是装在绿袋子里了。」斯特拉不懂他的意思，沉默不语；斯克罗普把沉默译作默认，便断定自己窝藏的是一位光照派的女信徒（*illuminée*），S——勋爵疑心她意图攻占伦敦塔、火烧英格兰银行——这等功业，至少，由一位年轻美人的双手与眼睛来建树的把握，同由一个醉醺醺的鞋匠兼医生、带上一本小册子和一只旧长袜去建树的把握，不相上下。

===Original===
Stella, in her conversations with Scythrop, displayed a highly cultivated and energetic mind, full of impassioned schemes of liberty, and impatience of masculine usurpation. She had a lively sense of all the oppressions that are done under the sun; and the vivid pictures which her imagination presented to her of the numberless scenes of injustice and misery which are being acted at every moment in every part of the inhabited world, gave a habitual seriousness to her physiognomy, that made it seem as if a smile had never once hovered on her lips. She was intimately conversant with the German language and literature; and Scythrop listened with delight to her repetitions of her favourite passages from Schiller and Goethe, and to her encomiums on the sublime Spartacus Weishaupt, the immortal founder of the sect of the Illuminati. Scythrop found that his soul had a greater capacity of love than the image of Marionetta had filled. The form of Stella took possession of every vacant corner of the cavity, and by degrees displaced that of Marionetta from many of the outworks of the citadel; though the latter still held possession of the *keep*. He judged, from his new friend calling herself Stella, that, if it were not her real name, she was an admirer of the principles of the German play from which she had taken it, and took an opportunity of leading the conversation to that subject; but to his great surprise, the lady spoke very ardently of the singleness and exclusiveness of love, and declared that the reign of affection was one and indivisible; that it might be transferred, but could not be participated. "If I ever love," said she, "I shall do so without limit or restriction. I shall hold all difficulties light, all sacrifices cheap, all obstacles gossamer. But for love so total, I shall claim a return as absolute. I will have no rival: whether more or less favoured will be of little moment. I will be neither first nor second—I will be alone. The heart which I shall possess I will possess entirely, or entirely renounce."

===Chinese===
斯特拉在与斯克罗普的交谈中，显示出一颗高度修养、精力充沛的心灵，满是对自由的炽热宏图，以及对男子僭越的不耐。她对日光之下一切压迫都有鲜活的感受；她的想象力向她呈现出一幅幅鲜明的图景——有人居住的世界上每个角落、每时每刻都在上演的无数不义与苦难——这使她的面容带上一种惯常的严肃，仿佛一抹微笑从不曾在她唇边逗留。她精通德语与德国文学；斯克罗普听她吟诵心爱的席勒与歌德的段落，听她称颂光照派不朽的创立者、崇高的斯巴达克斯·魏斯豪普特，不禁心醉神怡。斯克罗普发现，自己的灵魂所能容纳的爱，比玛丽奥内塔的形象所填满的更大。斯特拉的形貌占据了腔内每一个空着的角落，并渐渐把玛丽奥内塔的形貌从城堡的许多外围工事上排挤出去；不过后者依旧据守着主楼（the keep）。他从这位新朋友自称斯特拉推断：即便这不是她的真名，她也是那出德国戏剧——她取名之所自——所持原则的仰慕者，便寻了个机会把话头引向那个题目；然而令他大吃一惊的是，女士热切地谈起爱的专一与排他，宣称情的王国有且唯一、不可分割；它可以转移，却不可分享。「我若恋爱，」她说，「我将爱得没有限度、没有保留。我将视一切困难为轻，视一切牺牲为贱，视一切障碍如蛛丝。但面对这样全然的爱，我将要求同样绝对的回报。我不要任何情敌：对手受宠更多还是更少，都无关紧要。我不要第一，也不要第二——我只要独一无二。我所要占据的那颗心，我要么完全占据，要么完全放弃。」

===Original===
Scythrop did not dare to mention the name of Marionetta; he trembled lest some unlucky accident should reveal it to Stella, though he scarcely knew what result to wish or anticipate, and lived in the double fever of a perpetual dilemma. He could not dissemble to himself that he was in love, at the same time, with two damsels of minds and habits as remote as the antipodes. The scale of predilection always inclined to the fair one who happened to be present; but the absent was never effectually outweighed, though the degrees of exaltation and depression varied according to accidental variations in the outward and visible signs of the inward and spiritual graces of his respective charmers. Passing and repassing several times a day from the company of the one to that of the other, he was like a shuttlecock between two battledores, changing its direction as rapidly as the oscillations of a pendulum, receiving many a hard knock on the cork of a sensitive heart, and flying from point to point on the feathers of a super-sublimated head. This was an awful state of things. He had now as much mystery about him as any romantic transcendentalist or transcendental romancer could desire. He had his esoterical and his exoterical love. He could not endure the thought of losing either of them, but he trembled when he imagined the possibility that some fatal discovery might deprive him of both. The old proverb concerning two strings to a bow gave him some gleams of comfort; but that concerning two stools occurred to him more frequently, and covered his forehead with a cold perspiration. With Stella, he could indulge freely in all his romantic and philosophical visions. He could build castles in the air, and she would pile towers and turrets on the imaginary edifices. With Marionetta it was otherwise: she knew nothing of the world and society beyond the sphere of her own experience. Her life was all music and sunshine, and she wondered what anyone could see to complain of in such a pleasant state of things. She loved Scythrop, she hardly knew why; indeed she was not always sure that she loved him at all: she felt her fondness increase or diminish in an inverse ratio to his. When she had manoeuvred him into a fever of passionate love, she often felt and always assumed indifference: if she found that her coldness was contagious, and that Scythrop either was, or pretended to be, as indifferent as herself, she would become doubly kind, and raise him again to that elevation from which she had previously thrown him down. Thus, when his love was flowing, hers was ebbing: when his was ebbing, hers was flowing. Now and then there were moments of level tide, when reciprocal affection seemed to promise imperturbable harmony; but Scythrop could scarcely resign his spirit to the pleasing illusion, before the pinnace of the lover's affections was caught in some eddy of the lady's caprice, and he was whirled away from the shore of his hopes, without rudder or compass, into an ocean of mists and storms. It resulted, from this system of conduct, that all that passed between Scythrop and Marionetta, consisted in making and unmaking love. He had no opportunity to take measure of her understanding by conversations on general subjects, and on his favourite designs; and, being left in this respect to the exercise of indefinite conjecture, he took it for granted, as most lovers would do in similar circumstances, that she had great natural talents, which she wasted at present on trifles: but coquetry would end with marriage, and leave room for philosophy to exert its influence on her mind. Stella had no coquetry, no disguise: she was an enthusiast in subjects of general interest; and her conduct to Scythrop was always uniform, or rather showed a regular progression of partiality which seemed fast ripening into love.

===Chinese===
斯克罗普不敢说出玛丽奥内塔的名字；他战战兢兢，惟恐什么倒霉的意外在斯特拉面前泄露了这个名字，尽管他自己也几乎不知道该期望或预见什么结果，只能活在一种永续两难的双重高烧里。他无法向自己掩饰：他同时爱着两位少女，其心智与习性相隔之远，犹如分居地球两极。感情的天平总是倒向碰巧在场的那一位美人；可缺席的那一位也从没有被真正压下去，尽管意气飞扬与垂头丧气的程度，随着他两位心上人各自的内在心灵之美在外的可见征象的偶然变化而起落。他一天数次从这一位的座畔穿行到那一位的座畔，像一只夹在两块球拍之间的羽毛球，变换方向之快，赛过钟摆的振荡，那颗敏感的心做软木托，挨了一记又一记重击，那颗过分升华的脑袋做羽毛，带着他从一点飞到另一点。这是一种骇人的局面。他周身的神秘，如今已厚到任何浪漫的先验论者或先验论的浪漫作家所能渴望的极限。他有他的密传之爱，也有他的显传之爱。一想到失去其中任何一位，他都无法忍受；可一想到某种致命的败露可能叫他两位尽失，他又浑身发抖。「一张弓拴两根弦」的古老谚语给了他几线安慰的光亮；可「脚踩两张凳」那句浮现得更勤，把他额头浸出一片冷汗。同斯特拉在一起，他可以纵情驰骋他全部浪漫的哲学的幻梦。他可以在空中筑城堡，她还会在那些想象的大厦上再堆起塔楼与角楼。同玛丽奥内塔在一起却是另一回事：她对自己经验范围之外的世界与社交一无所知。她的生活全是音乐与阳光，她奇怪这样的良辰美景里还有什么可抱怨的。她爱斯克罗普，自己也说不大清为什么；说真的，她连自己究竟爱不爱他也拿不稳：她觉得自己的一腔柔情，与他的恰成反比地涨落。当她把他撩拨到炽恋的高烧，她常常真的感到冷漠，而且总要装出冷漠；一旦发觉她的冷淡会传染、斯克罗普当真或假装同她一样冷漠，她便加倍温存，重新把他捧上先前被她摔下来的高处。于是他的爱涨潮时，她的爱退潮；他的爱退潮时，她的爱涨潮。偶尔也有水位相平的时刻，两情相洽，仿佛许诺着波澜不惊的和谐；可斯克罗普还没来得及把心安放在这悦人的错觉里，这只爱情的轻舟便卷进女士任性的某个漩涡，被卷离希望的岸边，没了舵，没了罗盘，卷进一片雾雨风暴的汪洋。从这套行为方式里产生的结果是：斯克罗普与玛丽奥内塔之间的一切往来，无非是谈情与翻脸。他没有机会借谈天说地、谈他心爱的宏图来掂量她的悟性；而在这方面既被撇下，只好作漫无边际的臆测，他便想当然地认定——大多数情人在同样处境下都会如此——她天赋甚高，只是眼下浪费在琐碎小事上：卖弄风情总要以婚姻告终，届时自有哲学来施展它对她心灵的影响。斯特拉没有卖弄，没有伪装：她醉心于关乎大众的题目；她对斯克罗普的态度始终如一，或不如说呈现出一条稳定递进的偏爱轨迹，而且眼看就要瓜熟蒂落，成熟为爱。

## XI / XI

===Original===
Scythrop, attending one day the summons to dinner, found in the drawing-room his friend Mr. Cypress the poet, whom he had known at college, and who was a great favourite of Mr. Glowry. Mr. Cypress said, he was on the point of leaving England, but could not think of doing so without a farewell-look at Nightmare Abbey and his respected friends, the moody Mr. Glowry and the mysterious Mr. Scythrop, the sublime Mr. Flosky and the pathetic Mr. Listless; to all of whom, and the morbid hospitality of the melancholy dwelling in which they were then assembled, he assured them he should always look back with as much affection as his lacerated spirit could feel for anything. The sympathetic condolence of their respective replies was cut short by Raven's announcement of "dinner on table."

===Chinese===
一日，斯克罗普应召赴膳，在客厅里见到他的大学旧友、诗人赛普里斯先生——此公也是格洛里先生极相得的客人。赛普里斯先生说他即将离开英国，但总想着不能不辞而别，定要对噩梦隐修院和他敬重的诸位友人——阴郁的格洛里先生与神秘的斯克罗普先生、崇高的弗洛斯基先生与哀婉的李斯特里斯先生——再看最后一眼；他还向诸位保证，对这几位、对众人当时聚首的这座忧郁宅邸的病态盛情，他此生回首，怀着他的破碎心灵对任何事物所能怀有的最大眷恋。众人各自致以同情的宽慰，正说之间，雷文来报「酒席上台」，就此打断。

===Original===
The conversation that took place when the wine was in circulation, and the ladies were withdrawn, we shall report with our usual scrupulous fidelity.

===Chinese===
酒过三巡、女士们离席之后的那场谈话，我们将以我们一贯一丝不苟的忠实，原样呈报。

===Original===
**Mr. Glowry.** You are leaving England, Mr. Cypress. There is a delightful melancholy in saying farewell to an old acquaintance, when the chances are twenty to one against ever meeting again. A smiling bumper to a sad parting, and let us all be unhappy together.

**Mr. Cypress.** *Filling a bumper.* This is the only social habit that the disappointed spirit never unlearns.

**The Reverend Mr. Larynx.** *Filling.* It is the only piece of academical learning that the finished educatee retains.

**Mr. Flosky.** *Filling.* It is the only objective fact which the sceptic can realise.

===Chinese===
**格洛里先生.** 您就要离开英国了，赛普里斯先生。同故人作别，重逢之机渺茫到二十比一，其中自有一种愉快的悲哀。来，为伤心的离别干一杯含笑的满杯酒，让我们大家一起不快活吧。

**赛普里斯先生.** *斟满一杯.* 这是失望的心灵永远改不掉的唯一社交习惯。

**拉林克斯牧师.** *斟酒.* 这是学成毕业的学子唯一还记得的一点学问。

**弗洛斯基先生.** *斟酒.* 这是怀疑论者唯一能领会的客观事实。

===Original===
**Scythrop.** *Filling.* It is the only styptic for a bleeding heart.

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** *Filling.* It is the only trouble that is very well worth taking.

**Mr. Asterias.** *Filling.* It is the only key of conversational truth.

**Mr. Toobad.** *Filling.* It is the only antidote to the great wrath of the devil.

**Mr. Hilary.** *Filling.* It is the only symbol of perfect life. The inscription *hic non bibitur* will suit nothing but a tombstone.

===Chinese===
**斯克罗普.** *斟酒.* 这是唯一能止住一颗流血之心的止血药。

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** *斟酒.* 这是唯一值得一费的辛苦。

**阿斯特里亚斯先生.** *斟酒.* 这是打开谈话真理的唯一钥匙。

**图巴德先生.** *斟酒.* 这是解魔鬼大怒的唯一解药。

**希拉里先生.** *斟酒.* 这是完满生命的唯一象征。*hic non bibitur*（此处不饮）这样的铭文，只有墓碑配用。

===Original===
**Mr. Glowry.** You will see many fine old ruins, Mr. Cypress; crumbling pillars, and mossy walls—many a one-legged Venus and headless Minerva—many a Neptune buried in sand—many a Jupiter turned topsy-turvy—many a perforated Bacchus doing duty as a water-pipe—many reminiscences of the ancient world, which I hope was better worth living in than the modern; though, for myself, I care not a straw more for one than the other, and would not go twenty miles to see anything that either could show.

**Mr. Cypress.** It is something to seek, Mr. Glowry. The mind is restless, and must persist in seeking, though to find is to be disappointed. Do you feel no aspirations towards the countries of Socrates and Cicero? No wish to wander among the venerable remains of the greatness that has passed forever?

**Mr. Glowry.** Not a grain.

**Scythrop.** It is, indeed, much the same as if a lover should dig up the buried form of his mistress, and gaze upon relics which are anything but herself, to wander among a few mouldy ruins, that are only imperfect indexes to lost volumes of glory, and meet at every step the more melancholy ruins of human nature—a degenerate race of stupid and shrivelled slaves, grovelling in the lowest depths of servility and superstition.

===Chinese===
**格洛里先生.** 您会看到许多美好的古废墟，赛普里斯先生；崩碎的圆柱、生苔的墙垣——许多独腿的维纳斯、无头的密涅瓦——许多埋进沙里的尼普顿——许多四脚朝天的朱庇特——许多打穿了孔当水管用的巴克斯——许多古代世界的遗迹。但愿古代世界比现代世界更值得一活；至于我自己，对哪一个都不多爱一根草，随便哪一边能拿给我看的东西，我也不肯多走二十里路去看。

**赛普里斯先生.** 那总归是可寻之物，格洛里先生。心灵是躁动不安的，必须坚持寻觅，尽管寻到了便是失望。您对苏格拉底与西塞罗的故国，竟无一动于衷？竟不想在那一去不返的伟大的庄严遗迹之间漫步一番？

**格洛里先生.** 一星半点也没有。

**斯克罗普.** 那实在同情人去掘出亡妻下葬的形骸、凝视那些全然不是她的遗物差不多：在几处发霉的废墟之间漫游——那不过是失传的光荣卷册的残缺索引——而且步步都要遇上人性更可悲的废墟——一族退化了的、愚蠢干瘜的奴隶，在奴性与迷信的最深泥淖里匍匐。

===Original===
**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** It is the fashion to go abroad. I have thought of it myself, but am hardly equal to the exertion. To be sure, a little eccentricity and originality are allowable in some cases; and the most eccentric and original of all characters is an Englishman who stays at home.

**Scythrop.** I should have no pleasure in visiting countries that are past all hope of regeneration. There is great hope of our own; and it seems to me that an Englishman, who, either by his station in society, or by his genius, or (as in your instance, Mr. Cypress), by both, has the power of essentially serving his country in its arduous struggle with its domestic enemies, yet forsakes his country, which is still so rich in hope, to dwell in others which are only fertile in the ruins of memory, does what none of those ancients, whose fragmentary memorials you venerate, would have done in similar circumstances.

**Mr. Cypress.** Sir, I have quarrelled with my wife; and a man who has quarrelled with his wife is absolved from all duty to his country. I have written an ode to tell the people as much, and they may take it as they list.

**Scythrop.** Do you suppose, if Brutus had quarrelled with his wife, he would have given it as a reason to Cassius for having nothing to do with his enterprise? Or would Cassius have been satisfied with such an excuse?

===Chinese===
**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 出国是时兴事。我自己也动过念头，只怕吃不得那份辛苦。当然，人有时候未免要一点古怪、一点别致；而天下最古怪、最别致的人物，莫过于一个待在家里的英国人。

**斯克罗普.** 去游历毫无再生之望的国度，我毫无兴致。我们自己的国度希望正大；依我看，一个英国人，无论凭他在社会上的地位，还是凭他的天才，或者（如您的情形，赛普里斯先生）凭两者兼备，本有能力在国家同内部敌人艰苦搏斗之时从根本上为国效力，却抛下这尚且富于希望的国度，去住在只富于记忆的废墟的别国——这种事，那些您所崇敬的、只留残篇断碑供人凭吊的古人，处在同样的境况下，是哪一个也不会干的。

**赛普里斯先生.** 先生，我同妻子吵了一架；同妻子吵过架的男人，对国家的一切义务就此豁免。我写了一首颂歌把这话晓谕公众，他们爱怎么领受就怎么领受。

**斯克罗普.** 您以为，布鲁图斯若同妻子吵了架，会拿这个当理由，告诉卡西乌斯他不参加他的大业吗？卡西乌斯又会满足于这样一个借口吗？

===Original===
**Mr. Flosky.** Brutus was a senator; so is our dear friend: but the cases are different. Brutus had some hope of political good: Mr. Cypress has none. How should he, after what we have seen in France?

**Scythrop.** A Frenchman is born in harness, ready saddled, bitted, and bridled, for any tyrant to ride. He will fawn under his rider one moment, and throw him and kick him to death the next; but another adventurer springs on his back, and by dint of whip and spur on he goes as before. We may, without much vanity, hope better of ourselves.

**Mr. Cypress.** I have no hope for myself or for others. Our life is a false nature; it is not in the harmony of things; it is an all-blasting *upas*, whose root is earth, and whose leaves are the skies which rain their poison-dews upon mankind. We wither from our youth; we gasp with unslaked thirst for unattainable good; lured from the first to the last by phantoms—love, fame, ambition, avarice—all idle, and all ill—one meteor of many names, that vanishes in the smoke of death.8

===Chinese===
**弗洛斯基先生.** 布鲁图斯是元老；我们这位亲爱的朋友也是：但两种情形不同。布鲁图斯对政治之善还怀有几分希望：赛普里斯先生则毫无。他怎么可能还有，在我们亲眼见了法国的那一切之后？

**斯克罗普.** 法国人天生套着缰鞍，鞍辔嚼环一应俱全，随便哪位暴君都可跨骑。前一刻还在骑者胯下摇尾乞怜，下一刻便把他掀翻在地、踢死踏平；可另一个冒险家纵身一跃又骑上它背，靠着一顿鞭抽刺踢，它照旧驮着往前走。我们不妨对自己抱更好的指望，这也算不得自命不凡。

**赛普里斯先生.** 我对自己、对旁人都不抱指望。我们的生命是一场虚假的自然；它不在万物的和谐之中；它是一株毁灭一切的毒树（*upas*），根扎在大地，叶铺在天穹，把毒露淅沥淅沥降在人类头上。我们自青春便枯萎；我们渴求不可得的至善，渴得永远无从解渴；从生到死被幻影引诱着——爱情、名声、雄心、贪欲——样样无聊，样样有害——一个有许多名字的流星，在死亡的烟里熄灭。8

===Original===
**Mr. Flosky.** A most delightful speech, Mr. Cypress. A most amiable and instructive philosophy. You have only to impress its truth on the minds of all living men, and life will then, indeed, be the desert and the solitude; and I must do you, myself, and our mutual friends, the justice to observe, that let society only give fair play at one and the same time, as I flatter myself it is inclined to do, to your system of morals, and my system of metaphysics, and Scythrop's system of politics, and Mr. Listless's system of manners, and Mr. Toobad's system of religion, and the result will be as fine a mental chaos as even the immortal Kant himself could ever have hoped to see; in the prospect of which I rejoice.

**Mr. Hilary.** "Certainly, ancient, it is not a thing to rejoice at:" I am one of those who cannot see the good that is to result from all this mystifying and blue-devilling of society. The contrast it presents to the cheerful and solid wisdom of antiquity is too forcible not to strike anyone who has the least knowledge of classical literature. To represent vice and misery as the necessary accompaniments of genius, is as mischievous as it is false, and the feeling is as unclassical as the language in which it is usually expressed.

===Chinese===
**弗洛斯基先生.** 一番顶动人的演说，赛普里斯先生。一套顶仁慈、顶有教益的哲学。您只须把它的真理印进所有活人的心灵，人生便真会成为荒漠与荒原了；而且我必须为您、为我自己、为我们共同的朋友们说一句公道话：只消社会同时给点公平的机会——我颇为自信它是愿意的——给您的道德体系，给我的玄学体系，给斯克罗普的政治体系，给李斯特里斯先生的礼俗体系，给图巴德先生的宗教体系，其结果将是连不朽的康德本人所能指望见到的那种第一等的精神混沌；念及此，我不胜欣悦。

**希拉里先生.** 「当然喽，老先生，这可不是值得欢喜的事：」我属于那样一种人，看不出把社会这样故弄玄虚、这样「蓝魔」化（blue-devilling），能结出什么好果子。它同古人欢快而坚实的智慧对照之鲜明，凡对古典文学略知一二的人，无不为之触目。把罪恶与苦难说成天才的必然伴侣，既有害，又是假；这种情操同它惯常借以表达的语言一样，全无古典风范。

===Original===
**Mr. Toobad.** It is our calamity. The devil has come among us, and has begun by taking possession of all the cleverest fellows. Yet, forsooth, this is the enlightened age. Marry, how? Did our ancestors go peeping about with dark lanterns, and do we walk at our ease in broad sunshine? Where is the manifestation of our light? By what symptoms do you recognise it? What are its signs, its tokens, its symptoms, its symbols, its categories, its conditions? What is it, and why? How, where, when is it to be seen, felt, and understood? What do we see by it which our ancestors saw not, and which at the same time is worth seeing? We see a hundred men hanged, where they saw one. We see five hundred transported, where they saw one. We see five thousand in the workhouse, where they saw one. We see scores of Bible Societies, where they saw none. We see paper, where they saw gold. We see men in stays, where they saw men in armour. We see painted faces, where they saw healthy ones. We see children perishing in manufactories, where they saw them flourishing in the fields. We see prisons, where they saw castles. We see masters, where they saw representatives. In short, they saw true men, where we see false knaves. They saw Milton, and we see Mr. Sackbut.

===Chinese===
**图巴德先生.** 这是我们的灾难。魔鬼已经来到我们中间，而且开头就先占住了所有最聪明的家伙。然而，偏又号称这是个「启蒙时代」。请问，怎么个启蒙法？难道我们的祖先提着遮光灯笼到处窥探，我们却在朗朗白日下信步闲行？我们的光显现在哪里？你凭什么征候认出它？它有何标记、信物、征兆、象征、范畴、条件？它是什么，又为什么？在何处、何时、如何能被看见、被触摸、被理解？借着它，我们看见了什么我们的祖先不曾看见、同时又值得一看的东西？我们看见绞死一百个人，他们看见绞死一个。我们看见流放五百个，他们看见流放一个。我们看见五千人进济贫院，他们看见一个。我们看见几十个圣经会，他们一个也没有。我们看见纸，他们看见金子。我们看见穿束胸的男人，他们看见穿铠甲的男人。我们看见涂脂抹粉的脸，他们看见健康的脸。我们看见孩子们在工厂里夭折，他们看见孩子们在田野里茁壮。我们看见监狱，他们看见城堡。我们看见主子，他们看见代表。总而言之，他们看见真君子，我们看见假无赖。他们看见弥尔顿，我们看见萨克巴特先生。

===Original===
**Mr. Flosky.** The false knave, sir, is my honest friend; therefore, I beseech you, let him be countenanced. God forbid but a knave should have some countenance at his friend's request.

**Mr. Toobad.** "Good men and true" was their common term, like the *χαλος χάγαδος* of the Athenians. It is so long since men have been either good or true, that it is to be questioned which is most obsolete, the fact or the phraseology.

**Mr. Cypress.** There is no worth nor beauty but in the mind's idea. Love sows the wind and reaps the whirlwind.9 Confusion, thrice confounded, is the portion of him who rests even for an instant on that most brittle of reeds—the affection of a human being. The sum of our social destiny is to inflict or to endure.10

===Chinese===
**弗洛斯基先生.** 这位假无赖，先生，正是我诚实的朋友；所以，求您了，务必赏他几分「体面」。一个无赖在朋友求情之下，竟连几分「体面」也讨不着——上天不容。

**图巴德先生.** 「善良而真诚的人」是他们的口头禅，好比雅典人的*χαλος χάγαδος*（美而善者）。人类既不做善人、又不做真人的年头太久，究竟哪一个更陈旧——那事实，还是那说法——都成问题了。

**赛普里斯先生.** 价值与美，只存在于心灵的观念里。爱种下的是风，收割的是风暴。9 谁哪怕只把一瞬间的依靠，放在那最脆的芦苇——一个凡人的情爱——之上，混乱，三重加料的混乱，便是他的份。10 我们社交命运的总和，不是伤人，便是受苦。10

===Original===
**Mr. Hilary.** Rather to bear and forbear, Mr. Cypress—a maxim which you perhaps despise. Ideal beauty is not the mind's creation: it is real beauty, refined and purified in the mind's alembic, from the alloy which always more or less accompanies it in our mixed and imperfect nature. But still the gold exists in a very ample degree. To expect too much is a disease in the expectant, for which human nature is not responsible; and, in the common name of humanity, I protest against these false and mischievous ravings. To rail against humanity for not being abstract perfection, and against human love for not realising all the splendid visions of the poets of chivalry, is to rail at the summer for not being all sunshine, and at the rose for not being always in bloom.

**Mr. Cypress.** Human love! Love is not an inhabitant of the earth. We worship him as the Athenians did their unknown God: but broken hearts are the martyrs of his faith, and the eye shall never see the form which fantasy paints, and which passion pursues through paths of delusive beauty, among flowers whose odours are agonies, and trees whose gums are poison.11

**Mr. Hilary.** You talk like a Rosicrucian, who will love nothing but a sylph, who does not believe in the existence of a sylph, and who yet quarrels with the whole universe for not containing a sylph.

===Chinese===
**希拉里先生.** 与其那样，不如忍受与克制，赛普里斯先生——这条格言您大概瞧不上。理想美并非心灵的创造：它是真实的美，在心灵的蒸馏瓶里提纯精炼，滤去那在我们混合而不完全的天性里多少总要伴着它的杂质。但金子终究大量存在。期望过高，是期望者自身的病，人性不该为之负责；而且，凭人道的共同名义，我抗议这些虚假而有害的呓语。责怪人性不是抽象的完美，责怪人的爱实现不了骑士诗人的全部绚丽幻梦，就等于责怪夏天不全是阳光，责怪玫瑰不常开不谢。

**赛普里斯先生.** 人的爱！爱不是大地的居民。我们崇拜他，如雅典人崇拜他们的未知之神：但破碎的心才是他信仰的殉道者，而那个幻想所描绘、激情在迷人之美的路径上苦苦追赶的形体，肉眼永远不得一见——在那路上，花朵的芬芳是极苦，树木的树脂是毒汁。11

**希拉里先生.** 您说话活像一位玫瑰十字会的术士：只肯爱气精（sylph），又不信世上有气精，却还要怨整个宇宙容不下一尊气精。

===Original===
**Mr. Cypress.** The mind is diseased of its own beauty, and fevers into false creation. The forms which the sculptor's soul has seized exist only in himself.12

**Mr. Flosky.** Permit me to discept. They are the mediums of common forms combined and arranged into a common standard. The ideal beauty of the Helen of Zeuxis was the combined medium of the real beauty of the virgins of Crotona.

**Mr. Hilary.** But to make ideal beauty the shadow in the water, and, like the dog in the fable, to throw away the substance in catching at the shadow, is scarcely the characteristic of wisdom, whatever it may be of genius. To reconcile man as he is to the world as it is, to preserve and improve all that is good, and destroy or alleviate all that is evil, in physical and moral nature—have been the hope and aim of the greatest teachers and ornaments of our species. I will say, too, that the highest wisdom and the highest genius have been invariably accompanied with cheerfulness. We have sufficient proofs on record that Shakespeare and Socrates were the most festive of companions. But now the little wisdom and genius we have seem to be entering into a conspiracy against cheerfulness.

===Chinese===
**赛普里斯先生.** 心灵为它自身的美害了病，发烧烧出虚假的创造。雕塑家的灵魂所攫取的那些形体，只存在于他自己之内。12

**弗洛斯基先生.** 请容许我提请「异议」（discept）。它们是共相形式的媒介，组合排列而成一个共相的标准。宙克西斯笔下海伦的理想美，是克罗顿众少女真实之美的复合媒介。

**希拉里先生.** 可是，把理想美当作水中的影子，又像寓言里的狗那样，为捞影子而丢掉肉，这无论作为天才是什么品性，总归算不得智慧的特征吧。使实际的人安于实际的世道，保存并改善物理与道德自然中一切善的，消灭或减轻一切恶的——这向来是我们这一族最伟大的导师与光耀的希望与鹄的。我还想说一句：最高的智慧与最高的天才，从来都伴有欢快。史册足证，莎士比亚与苏格拉底都是最能尽欢的伙伴。可如今，我们仅有的那一点点智慧与天才，倒仿佛在密谋联手对付欢快。

===Original===
**Mr. Toobad.** How can we be cheerful with the devil among us!

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** How can we be cheerful when our nerves are shattered?

**Mr. Flosky.** How can we be cheerful when we are surrounded by a *reading public*, that is growing too wise for its betters?

**Scythrop.** How can we be cheerful when our great general designs are crossed every moment by our little particular passions?

**Mr. Cypress.** How can we be cheerful in the midst of disappointment and despair?

**Mr. Glowry.** Let us all be unhappy together.

===Chinese===
**图巴德先生.** 魔鬼就在我们中间，叫我们怎么欢快得起来！

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 神经全震坏了，叫我们怎么欢快得起来？

**弗洛斯基先生.** 我们被一群*阅读公众*团团围住，他们的智慧眼看要长过他们的长辈，叫我们怎么欢快得起来？

**斯克罗普.** 我们宏大的总体设计，时时刻刻被我们细小的私人激情掣肘，叫我们怎么欢快得起来？

**赛普里斯先生.** 身在失望与绝望之中，叫我们怎么欢快得起来？

**格洛里先生.** 让我们大家一起不快活吧。

===Original===
**Mr. Hilary.** Let us sing a catch.

**Mr. Glowry.** No: a nice tragical ballad. "The Norfolk Tragedy" to the tune of the Hundredth Psalm.
