**Mr. Hilary.** I say a catch.

**Mr. Glowry.** I say no. A song from Mr. Cypress.

**All.** A song from Mr. Cypress.

**Mr. Cypress.** *Sung.*

There is a fever of the spirit,

The brand of Cain's unresting doom,

Which in the lone dark souls that bear it

Glows like the lamp in Tullia's tomb:

Unlike that lamp, its subtle fire

Burns, blasts, consumes its cell, the heart,

Till, one by one, hope, joy, desire,

Like dreams of shadowy smoke depart.

===Chinese===
**希拉里先生.** 我们来唱一支轮唱曲吧。

**格洛里先生.** 不：来一支妙极的悲剧歌谣。《诺福克惨史》，用《第一百篇诗篇》的调子唱。

**希拉里先生.** 我说唱轮唱曲。

**格洛里先生.** 我说不。请赛普里斯先生来一支歌。

**众人.** 请赛普里斯先生来一支歌。

**赛普里斯先生.** *唱.*

有一种心灵的高烧，

是该隐不得安息之劫的烙印，

在孤独阴郁、身负此劫的灵魂里

如图莉娅墓中的长明灯般灼灼：

又与那灯不同，它幽微的火

烧灼、摧折、焚尽它的囚室——心，

直到希望、欢乐、欲望，一个一个

如烟影迷离的梦，散尽无痕。

===Original===
When hope, love, life itself, are only

Dust—spectral memories—dead and cold—

The unfed fire burns bright and lonely,

Like that undying lamp of old:

And by that drear illumination,

Till time its clay-built home has rent,

Thought broods on feeling's desolation—

The soul is its own monument.

===Chinese===
当希望、爱、乃至生命本身

只余尘埃——幽灵的记忆——僵冷——

那无薪之火依然灼灼孤明，

恰似那盏不灭的古灯：

借着那惨淡的照临，

直到时间把泥筑的居所震崩，

思想伏在感情的荒芜上孵吟——

灵魂自成一座坟茔。

===Original===
**Mr. Glowry.** Admirable. Let us all be unhappy together.

**Mr. Hilary.** Now, I say again, a catch.

**The Reverend Mr. Larynx.** I am for you.

**Mr. Hilary.** "Seamen Three."

**The Reverend Mr. Larynx.** Agreed. I'll be Harry Gill, with the voice of three. Begin.

**Mr. Hilary and the Reverend Mr. Larynx.** Seamen three! What men be ye?

Gotham's three wise men we be.

Whither in your bowl so free?

To rake the moon from out the sea.

The bowl goes trim. The moon doth shine.

And our ballast is old wine;

And your ballast is old wine.

===Chinese===
**格洛里先生.** 妙极了。让我们大家一起不快活吧。

**希拉里先生.** 现在我再说一遍，来支轮唱曲。

**拉林克斯牧师.** 奉陪。

**希拉里先生.** 《三个水手》。

**拉林克斯牧师.** 就这么定。我来当哈里·吉尔，一人三条嗓子。起头。

**希拉里先生与拉林克斯牧师.** 水手三！你们是什么人？

我们是戈潭的三位智人。

乘着木碗自由自在，去往何方？

去把月亮从海里打捞。

木碗稳稳，月色朗朗，

我们的压舱物是陈年佳酿；

你们的压舱物是陈年佳酿。

===Original===
Who art thou, so fast adrift?

I am he they call Old Care.

Here on board we will thee lift.

No: I may not enter there.

Wherefore so? 'Tis Jove's decree,

In a bowl Care may not be;

In a bowl Care may not be.

===Chinese===
你是谁，漂得这么急？

我就是人称「忧愁翁」的那位。

上船来吧，我们拉你一把。

不：这船上我进不得。

为何进不得？这是宙斯的旨意，

木碗之中容不得忧愁；

木碗之中容不得忧愁。

===Original===
Hear ye not the waves that roll?

No: in charmed bowl we swim.

What the charm that floats the bowl?

Water may not pass the brim.

The bowl goes trim. The moon doth shine.

And our ballast is old wine;

And your ballast is old wine.

===Chinese===
听不见滚滚的波涛？

听不见：我们在魔碗里浮游。

是什么魔咒叫碗漂浮？

滴水也漫不过碗口。

木碗稳稳，月色朗朗，

我们的压舱物是陈年佳酿；

你们的压舱物是陈年佳酿。

===Original===
This catch was so well executed by the spirit and science of Mr. Hilary, and the deep triune voice of the reverend gentleman, that the whole party, in spite of themselves, caught the contagion, and joined in chorus at the conclusion, each raising a bumper to his lips:

**All.** The bowl goes trim: the moon doth shine:

And our ballast is old wine.

===Chinese===
这支轮唱曲，经希拉里先生的兴致与技艺、以及那位可敬牧师先生三重浑厚嗓音的演绎，唱得如此出色，满座不由自主地受了感染，到结尾时齐声加入合唱，人人举杯齐唇：

**众人.** 木碗稳稳：月色朗朗：

我们的压舱物是陈年佳酿。

===Original===
Mr. Cypress, having his ballast on board, stepped, the same evening, into his bowl, or travelling chariot, and departed to rake seas and rivers, lakes and canals, for the moon of ideal beauty.

===Chinese===
赛普里斯先生既已把压舱物载上船，当晚便跨进他的木碗——亦即他的旅行马车——扬帆而去，到江海湖泊与运河里打捞理想美的月亮去了。

## XII / XII

===Original===
It was the custom of the Honourable Mr. Listless, on adjourning from the bottle to the ladies, to retire for a few moments to make a second toilette, that he might present himself in becoming taste. Fatout, attending as usual, appeared with a countenance of great dismay, and informed his master that he had just ascertained that the abbey was haunted. Mrs. Hilary's *gentlewoman*, for whom Fatout had lately conceived a *tendresse*, had been, as she expressed it, "fritted out of her seventeen senses" the preceding night, as she was retiring to her bedchamber, by a ghastly figure which she had met stalking along one of the galleries, wrapped in a white shroud, with a bloody turban on its head. She had fainted away with fear; and, when she recovered, she found herself in the dark, and the figure was gone. "*Sacre—cochon—bleu!*" exclaimed Fatout, giving very deliberate emphasis to every portion of his terrible oath—"I vould not meet de *revenant*, de ghost—*non*—not for all de *bowl-de-ponch* in de vorld."

===Chinese===
尊贵的李斯特里斯先生有个习惯：从酒瓶这头移步到女士们那头之前，总要退开片刻，再梳妆修饰一番，好以得体的品味现身。法图照例随侍在侧，这回却带着一脸大惊失色进来，禀告主人说，他刚刚查明：这座隐修院闹鬼。希拉里太太的*贴身女仆*（*gentlewoman*）——法图近来对她生出了一段*钟情*（*tendresse*）——按她自己的说法，前一天夜里正要回卧房时，被一个狰狞的身影吓得「十七条感官一齐出窍」：那身影裹着白裹尸布，头顶血染的头巾，在一条长廊里大步而行，同她打了个照面。她吓得晕死过去；醒来时四下漆黑，那身影已不知去向。「*Sacre—cochon—bleu!*（该死的——猪猡——天杀的！）」法图嚷道，把他这句可怕咒语的每个成分都咬得铿锵分明——「就是拿世上全部的*bowl-de-ponch*（五味酒）来换，我也不要撞见那*revenant*（还魂客），那鬼——*non*（不）——绝不。」

===Original===
"Fatout," said the Honourable Mr. Listless, "did I ever see a ghost?"

"*Jamais*, monsieur, never."

"Then I hope I never shall, for, in the present shattered state of my nerves, I am afraid it would be too much for me. There—loosen the lace of my stays a little, for really this plebeian practice of eating—Not too loose—consider my shape. That will do. And I desire that you bring me no more stories of ghosts; for, though I do not believe in such things, yet, when one is awake in the night, one is apt, if one thinks of them, to have fancies that give one a kind of a chill, particularly if one opens one's eyes suddenly on one's dressing gown, hanging in the moonlight, between the bed and the window."

===Chinese===
「法图，」尊贵的李斯特里斯先生说，「我从前见过鬼吗？」

「*Jamais*（从来没有），先生，从来没有。」

「那么但愿我永远不要见着，因为，照我眼下神经震坏的程度，只怕它一来我就受不住。好了——把我束胸的系带松一松，这种平民的勾当——吃东西——真是要命。别太松——要顾着我的体形。这就行了。还有，我吩咐你，别再拿鬼故事来烦我；因为，我虽然不信这些玩意儿，可夜里睡不着的时候，人一想起来，就容易生出些叫人发冷的幻象，尤其是猛一睁眼，看见自己的晨衣挂在月光里、正卡在床和窗户中间的时候。」

===Original===
The Honourable Mr. Listless, though he had prohibited Fatout from bringing him any more stories of ghosts, could not help thinking of that which Fatout had already brought; and, as it was uppermost in his mind, when he descended to the tea and coffee cups, and the rest of the company in the library, he almost involuntarily asked Mr. Flosky, whom he looked up to as a most oraculous personage, whether any story of any ghost that had ever appeared to anyone, was entitled to any degree of belief?

===Chinese===
尊贵的李斯特里斯先生虽然禁止法图再给他带鬼故事来，却忍不住琢磨起法图已经带来的那一桩；这事在他脑子里盘踞不去，待他下楼来到书房里的茶盏咖啡盏与其余众人中间时，他几乎是身不由己地问起了弗洛斯基先生——他把此公仰望为一位十足神谕的人物——问自古以来凡是向什么人显过形的鬼故事，是否都还配得上几分可信？

===Original===
**Mr. Flosky.** By far the greater number, to a very great degree.

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** Really, that is very alarming!

**Mr. Flosky.** *Sunt geminoe somni portoe*. There are two gates through which ghosts find their way to the upper air: fraud and self-delusion. In the latter case, a ghost is a *deceptio visus*, an ocular spectrum, an idea with the force of a sensation. I have seen many ghosts myself. I dare say there are few in this company who have not seen a ghost.

===Chinese===
**弗洛斯基先生.** 绝大多数都配得上，而且可信到极高的程度。

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 真的，这可吓人得很！

**弗洛斯基先生.** *Sunt geminoe somni portoe*（睡眠有两重门）。鬼魂有两条路升到地面：欺诈，与自欺。就后者而言，鬼是一种*deceptio visus*（视觉错觉），一种眼内的幻象，一个具有感觉之力量的观念。我自己就见过许多鬼。我看这一屋子人里，没见过鬼的怕是没有几个。

===Original===
**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** I am happy to say, I never have, for one.

**The Reverend Mr. Larynx.** We have such high authority for ghosts, that it is rank scepticism to disbelieve them. Job saw a ghost, which came for the express purpose of asking a question, and did not wait for an answer.

**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** Because Job was too frightened to give one.

===Chinese===
**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 我倒可以说，我就是一个没见过的。

**拉林克斯牧师.** 鬼的事，我们有极高的权威，不信鬼才是十足的怀疑论。约伯就见过一个鬼，那鬼专为问一个问题而来，问完没等回答就走了。

**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 那是因为约伯吓得答不上来了。

===Original===
**The Reverend Mr. Larynx.** Spectres appeared to the Egyptians during the darkness with which Moses covered Egypt. The witch of Endor raised the ghost of Samuel. Moses and Elias appeared on Mount Tabor. An evil spirit was sent into the army of Sennacherib, and exterminated it in a single night.

**Mr. Toobad.** Saying, "The devil is come among you, having great wrath."

**Mr. Flosky.** Saint Macarius interrogated a skull, which was found in the desert, and made it relate, in presence of several witnesses, what was going forward in hell. Saint Martin of Tours, being jealous of a pretended martyr, who was the rival saint of his neighbourhood, called up his ghost, and made him confess that he was damned. Saint Germain, being on his travels, turned out of an inn a large party of ghosts, who had every night taken possession of the table d'hôte, and consumed a copious supper.

===Chinese===
**拉林克斯牧师.** 摩西使埃及遍地黑暗的时候，埃及人眼前就现过鬼影。隐多珥的女巫召来了撒母耳的亡魂。摩西和以利亚在他泊山上显形。一支邪灵被派进西拿基立的大军，一夜之间把它歼灭净尽。

**图巴德先生.** 有言道：「魔鬼已来到你们中间，怒气冲冲。」

**弗洛斯基先生.** 圣马卡里乌斯审问过一颗在沙漠里找到的骷髅，让它在好几位证人面前讲述地狱里正进行的勾当。图尔的圣马丁吃一个假殉道者的醋——那人是他那一带的对头圣徒——便召来他的鬼魂，叫他招认自己下了地狱。圣日耳曼旅行途中，把一大帮鬼从一家客栈里赶了出去——那帮鬼夜夜霸占客饭桌（*table d'hôte*），吃掉一顿丰盛的晚餐。

===Original===
**Mr. Hilary.** Jolly ghosts, and no doubt all friars. A similar party took possession of the cellar of M. Swebach, the painter, in Paris, drank his wine, and threw the empty bottles at his head.

**The Reverend Mr. Larynx.** An atrocious act.

**Mr. Flosky.** Pausanias relates, that the neighing of horses and the tumult of combatants were heard every night on the field of Marathon: that those who went purposely to hear these sounds suffered severely for their curiosity; but those who heard them by accident passed with impunity.

===Chinese===
**希拉里先生.** 快活的鬼，而且八成全是托钵修士。同样的一帮，占过巴黎画家斯韦巴赫先生的地窖，喝干他的酒，还把空酒瓶朝他脑袋上扔。

**拉林克斯牧师.** 暴行啊。

**弗洛斯基先生.** 保萨尼亚斯记载说，马拉松战场上每夜都听得见战马嘶鸣与战士的喧杀：存心去听这些声响的人，都为他们的好奇心吃了大苦头；而无意听见的人，却安然无恙。

===Original===
**The Reverend Mr. Larynx.** I once saw a ghost myself, in my study, which is the last place where anyone but a ghost would look for me. I had not been into it for three months, and was going to consult Tillotson, when, on opening the door, I saw a venerable figure in a flannel dressing gown, sitting in my armchair, and reading my Jeremy Taylor. It vanished in a moment, and so did I; and what it was or what it wanted I have never been able to ascertain.

**Mr. Flosky.** It was an idea with the force of a sensation. It is seldom that ghosts appeal to two senses at once; but, when I was in Devonshire, the following story was well attested to me. A young woman, whose lover was at sea, returning one evening over some solitary fields, saw her lover sitting on a stile over which she was to pass. Her first emotions were surprise and joy, but there was a paleness and seriousness in his face that made them give place to alarm. She advanced towards him, and he said to her, in a solemn voice, "The eye that hath seen me shall see me no more. Thine eye is upon me, but I am not." And with these words he vanished; and on that very day and hour, as it afterwards appeared, he had perished by shipwreck.

===Chinese===
**拉林克斯牧师.** 我自己就在我的书房里见过一回鬼——除了鬼，谁也不会到那地方去找我。我有三个月没进去过了，正要去查蒂洛森，一开门，只见一位穿法兰绒晨衣的可敬身影，坐在我的扶手椅里，读我的杰里米·泰勒。它一晃就没了，我也一晃就没了；它是什么、来干什么，我至今查不明白。

**弗洛斯基先生.** 那是一个具有感觉之力量的观念。鬼极少同时惊动两种感官；不过我在德文郡的时候，下面这个故事是有确凿证词的。一个少女，情人在海上，一天傍晚独自穿过一片荒凉的田畴，看见她的情人坐在她要跨过的那道栅梯上。她先是又惊又喜，可他脸上有一种苍白与凝重，教惊喜立刻让位给惊恐。她向他走去，他用一种肃穆的声音对她说：「见过我的眼睛，将不再见我。你的眼睛望着我，我却不在此处。」话音一落，他就不见了；而后来查明，正是在那一日、那一时，他已葬身海难。

===Original===
The whole party now drew round in a circle, and each related some ghostly anecdote, heedless of the flight of time, till, in a pause of the conversation, they heard the hollow tongue of midnight sounding twelve.

===Chinese===
满座这时围拢成一圈，各人轮流讲一段鬼话逸闻，浑忘了时光飞逝，直到谈话一歇，听见午夜那空洞的舌头敲响了十二下。

===Original===
**Mr. Hilary.** All these anecdotes admit of solution on psychological principles. It is more easy for a soldier, a philosopher, or even a saint, to be frightened at his own shadow, than for a dead man to come out of his grave. Medical writers cite a thousand singular examples of the force of imagination. Persons of feeble, nervous, melancholy temperament, exhausted by fever, by labour, or by spare diet, will readily conjure up, in the magic ring of their own fantasy, spectres, gorgons, chimaeras, and all the objects of their hatred and their love. We are most of us like Don Quixote, to whom a windmill was a giant, and Dulcinea a magnificent princess: all more or less the dupes of our own imagination, though we do not all go so far as to see ghosts, or to fancy ourselves pipkins and teapots.

**Mr. Flosky.** I can safely say I have seen too many ghosts myself to believe in their external existence. I have seen all kinds of ghosts: black spirits and white, red spirits and grey. Some in the shapes of venerable old men, who have met me in my rambles at noon; some of beautiful young women, who have peeped through my curtains at midnight.

===Chinese===
**希拉里先生.** 所有这些逸闻，都可以按心理学原理一一索解。一个士兵、一个哲学家、乃至一位圣徒，被自己的影子吓着，总比一个死人从坟墓里爬出来容易。医学作家们引证过上千个想象力威力的奇例。禀性孱弱、神经质、忧郁的人，被热病、操劳或斋戒拖垮了的人，很容易在自己幻想的魔环里召出幽灵、戈耳工、喀迈拉，以及他们一切恨的对象和爱的对象。我们多半都像堂吉诃德：风车在他眼里是巨人，杜尔西内娅是绝世的公主；我们多多少少都是自己想象力的受骗者，尽管我们并非人人都走到见鬼那一步，也没人把自己想成瓦罐和茶壶。

**弗洛斯基先生.** 我可以担保：我自己见鬼见得太多了，多到不能相信它们存在于我之外。各色各样的鬼我都见过：黑的白的，红的灰的。有的是庄严老者的模样，正午在我的漫游途中迎面而来；有的化作美丽的少女，午夜从我的帐幔间探头张望。

===Original===
**The Honourable Mr. Listless.** And have proved, I doubt not, "palpable to feeling as to sight."

**Mr. Flosky.** By no means, sir. You reflect upon my purity. Myself and my friends, particularly my friend Mr. Sackbut, are famous for our purity. No, sir, genuine untangible ghosts. I live in a world of ghosts. I see a ghost at this moment.

===Chinese===
**尊贵的李斯特里斯先生.** 而且我毫不怀疑，个个都「看得见，也摸得着」吧。

**弗洛斯基先生.** 决无此事，先生。您这是糟蹋我的清白。我和我的朋友们，尤其是我的朋友萨克巴特先生，是以清白闻名的。不，先生，是真正的、摸不着的鬼。我住在一个鬼的世界里。此刻我就看见一个鬼。

===Original===
Mr. Flosky fixed his eyes on a door at the farther end of the library. The company looked in the same direction. The door silently opened, and a ghastly figure, shrouded in white drapery, with the semblance of a bloody turban on its head, entered and stalked slowly up the apartment. Mr. Flosky, familiar as he was with ghosts, was not prepared for this apparition, and made the best of his way out at the opposite door. Mrs. Hilary and Marionetta followed, screaming. The Honourable Mr. Listless, by two turns of his body, rolled first off the sofa and then under it. The Reverend Mr. Larynx leaped up and fled with so much precipitation, that he overturned the table on the foot of Mr. Glowry. Mr. Glowry roared with pain in the ear of Mr. Toobad. Mr. Toobad's alarm so bewildered his senses, that, missing the door, he threw up one of the windows, jumped out in his panic, and plunged over head and ears in the moat. Mr. Asterias and his son, who were on the watch for their mermaid, were attracted by the splashing, threw a net over him, and dragged him to land.

===Chinese===
弗洛斯基先生把眼睛盯住书房另一头的一扇门。众人朝同一方向望去。门无声地开了，一个狰狞的身影走进来，裹着白衣，头上顶着血染头巾的模样，一步一步缓缓穿堂而来。弗洛斯基先生素来同鬼稔熟，却也没防着这一场显形，抢先从对面的门夺路而出。希拉里太太和玛丽奥内塔尖叫着跟了出去。尊贵的李斯特里斯先生扭了两扭身子，先从沙发上滚下来，再滚进沙发底下。拉林克斯牧师一跃而起，逃得那样仓皇，把桌子掀翻在格洛里先生的脚上。格洛里先生疼得对着图巴德先生的耳朵大吼。图巴德先生这一惊非同小可，神智全乱，摸不着门，竟推开一扇窗，仓皇中一跃而出，一头扎进壕沟，没顶及耳。阿斯特里亚斯先生和他的儿子正守候他们的美人鱼，被扑通声引来，撒下一张网把他罩住，拖上了岸。

===Original===
Scythrop and Mr. Hilary meanwhile had hastened to his assistance, and, on arriving at the edge of the moat, followed by several servants with ropes and torches, found Mr. Asterias and Aquarius busy in endeavouring to extricate Mr. Toobad from the net, who was entangled in the meshes, and floundering with rage. Scythrop was lost in amazement; but Mr. Hilary saw, at one view, all the circumstances of the adventure, and burst into an immoderate fit of laughter; on recovering from which, he said to Mr. Asterias, "You have caught an odd fish, indeed." Mr. Toobad was highly exasperated at this unseasonable pleasantry; but Mr. Hilary softened his anger, by producing a knife, and cutting the Gordian knot of his reticular envelopment. "You see," said Mr. Toobad, "you see, gentlemen, in my unfortunate person proof upon proof of the present dominion of the devil in the affairs of this world; and I have no doubt but that the apparition of this night was Apollyon himself in disguise, sent for the express purpose of terrifying me into this complication of misadventures. The devil is come among you, having great wrath, because he knoweth that he hath but a short time."

===Chinese===
斯克罗普和希拉里先生这时已赶去救援，到了壕沟边上——身后跟着几名拿绳索火把的仆人——只见阿斯特里亚斯先生和阿夸里乌斯正忙着把图巴德先生从网里往外摘：他缠在网眼里，气得直扑腾。斯克罗普看得目瞪口呆；希拉里先生却一眼看透了这场奇遇的全部情状，放声爆笑不止；笑罢，对阿斯特里亚斯先生说：「您果然捞着了一条怪鱼。」图巴德先生对这不合时宜的俏皮话大为光火；希拉里先生却掏出小刀，割断了这团网裹的戈尔迪之结，把他的火气也一并割软了。「你们瞧，」图巴德先生说，「诸位，瞧瞧我这不幸的身子，魔鬼如今主宰尘世事务，铁证接着铁证；我毫不怀疑，今夜这个显形正是亚玻伦（Apollyon）亲自改扮的，专程而来，就为把我吓进这一连串的祸事里。魔鬼已来到你们中间，怒气冲冲，因为他知道自己的时日无多了。」

## XIII / XIII

===Original===
Mr. Glowry was much surprised, on occasionally visiting Scythrop's tower, to find the door always locked, and to be kept sometimes waiting many minutes for admission: during which he invariably heard a heavy rolling sound like that of a ponderous mangle, or of a wagon on a weighing-bridge, or of theatrical thunder.

===Chinese===
格洛里先生偶尔到斯克罗普的塔里走走，大为诧异：门总是锁着，有时要等上好几分钟才得进门：等待期间，他总听得见一种沉重的滚动声，像一台笨重的轧布机，像磅桥上的一辆货车，又像戏台上的雷声。

===Original===
He took little notice of this for some time; at length his curiosity was excited, and, one day, instead of knocking at the door, as usual, the instant he reached it, he applied his ear to the keyhole, and like Bottom, in the *Midsummer Night's Dream*, "spied a voice," which he guessed to be of the feminine gender, and knew to be not Scythrop's, whose deeper tones he distinguished at intervals. Having attempted in vain to catch a syllable of the discourse, he knocked violently at the door, and roared for immediate admission. The voices ceased, the accustomed rolling sound was heard, the door opened, and Scythrop was discovered alone. Mr. Glowry looked round to every corner of the apartment, and then said, "Where is the lady?"

===Chinese===
起初他没大在意；后来好奇心被勾起来了，一天，他没有照例一走到门口就敲门，而是把耳朵贴上钥匙孔，像《仲夏夜之梦》（*Midsummer Night's Dream*）里的波顿一样「窥见了一个声音」：他猜那声音属于阴性，并且断定不是斯克罗普的——斯克罗普那更深沉的音色，他隔着空当分辨得出来。他试图抓住话语中的一个音节，终归徒劳，便狠狠捶门，吼着要立刻进门。说话声停了，那惯常的滚动声响起，门开了，只见斯克罗普独自一人。格洛里先生把屋里每个角落都扫视了一遍，然后说：「那位小姐呢？」

===Original===
"The lady, sir?" said Scythrop.

"Yes, sir, the lady."

"Sir, I do not understand you."

"You don't, sir?"

"No, indeed, sir. There is no lady here."

"But, sir, this is not the only apartment in the tower, and I make no doubt there is a lady upstairs."

"You are welcome to search, sir."

"Yes, and while I am searching, she will slip out from some lurking place, and make her escape."

"You may lock this door, sir, and take the key with you."

"But there is the terrace door: she has escaped by the terrace."

"The terrace, sir, has no other outlet, and the walls are too high for a lady to jump down."

"Well, sir, give me the key."

===Chinese===
「小姐，先生？」斯克罗普说。

「是的，先生，那位小姐。」

「先生，我不明白您的意思。」

「您不明白，先生？」

「真的不明白，先生。这里没有什么小姐。」

「可是，先生，这塔里不止这一间屋子，我毫不怀疑楼上有位小姐。」

「欢迎您搜，先生。」

「好啊，我一搜，她就从哪个藏身的角落溜出去，逃之夭夭。」

「您可以把这扇门锁上，先生，钥匙随身带走。」

「可还有露台的门：她已经从露台逃走了。」

「露台，先生，没有别的出口，墙又太高，小姐跳不下来。」

「好吧，先生，钥匙给我。」

===Original===
Mr. Glowry took the key, searched every nook of the tower, and returned.

"You are a fox, Scythrop; you are an exceedingly cunning fox, with that demure visage of yours. What was that lumbering sound I heard before you opened the door?"

"Sound, sir?"

"Yes, sir, sound."

"My dear sir, I am not aware of any sound, except my great table, which I moved on rising to let you in."

"The table!—let me see that. No, sir; not a tenth part heavy enough, not a tenth part."

===Chinese===
格洛里先生接过钥匙，把塔里每个旮旯都搜了一遍，回来了。

「你是一只狐狸，斯克罗普；一只极其狡猾的狐狸，还端着那么一副假正经的脸。我进门之前听见的那阵隆隆声，是哪里来的？」

「声音，先生？」

「是的，先生，声音。」

「亲爱的先生，我不记得有什么声音，除了我那张大桌子——我起身给您开门的时候，挪了挪它。」

「桌子！——拿来我瞧瞧。不对，先生；分量连十分之一都不够，连十分之一都不够。」

===Original===
"But, sir, you do not consider the laws of acoustics: a whisper becomes a peal of thunder in the focus of reverberation. Allow me to explain this: sounds striking on concave surfaces are reflected from them, and, after reflection, converge to points which are the foci of these surfaces. It follows, therefore, that the ear may be so placed in one, as that it shall hear a sound better than when situated nearer to the point of the first impulse: again, in the case of two concave surfaces placed opposite to each other—"

"Nonsense, sir. Don't tell me of foci. Pray, sir, will concave surfaces produce two voices when nobody speaks? I heard two voices, and one was feminine; feminine, sir: what say you to that?"

"Oh, sir, I perceive your mistake: I am writing a tragedy, and was acting over a scene to myself. To convince you, I will give you a specimen; but you must first understand the plot. It is a tragedy on the German model. The Great Mogul is in exile, and has taken lodgings at Kensington, with his only daughter, the Princess Rantrorina, who takes in needlework, and keeps a day school. *The princess is discovered hemming a set of shirts for the parson of the parish: they are to be marked with a large R. Enter to her the Great Mogul. A pause, during which they look at each other expressively. The princess changes colour several times. The Mogul takes snuff in great agitation. Several grains are heard to fall on the stage. His heart is seen to beat through his upper benjamin.*—**The Mogul** *with a mournful look at his left shoe*. 'My shoestring is broken.'—**The Princess** *after an interval of melancholy reflection*. 'I know it.' **The Mogul**. 'My second shoestring! The first broke when I lost my empire: the second has broken today. When will my poor heart break?'—**The Princess**. 'Shoestrings, hearts, and empires! Mysterious sympathy!' "

===Chinese===
「可是，先生，您没有考虑声学定律：一句耳语，到了回响的焦点上，就会变成一声炸雷。请容我解释：声音打在凹面上，便从凹面反射出去，反射之后，汇聚于各点，这些点就是各该凹面的焦点。由此可见，耳朵若恰好处在这样一个焦点上，听某个声音可以比靠近第一冲源时听得更清楚：再者，倘有两个凹面彼此相对——」

「胡说，先生。少跟我提焦点。请问先生，没人说话的时候，凹面会发出两个声音吗？我听见了两个声音，其中一个是女人的；女人的，先生：这一层您怎么说？」

「哦，先生，我明白您的误会了：我在写一部悲剧，方才正在自演其中一场。为了让您信服，我给您念一段标本；不过您得先明白剧情。这是一部照德国样式写的悲剧。莫卧儿大帝流亡在外，在肯辛顿赁屋而居，同他的独生女儿兰特罗里娜公主住在一起；公主靠接针线活过活，还开着一所日间小学堂。*公主上场，正在给本教区牧师的一套衬衫缝边：衬衫上都要绣一个大大的 R。莫卧儿大帝上。一段停顿，二人脉脉相视。公主几度变色。大帝激动万分地取鼻烟。听得见几粒鼻烟落在台上。只见他的心隔着上身的长外衣怦怦直跳。*——**大帝***凄然望了望自己的左鞋*。「我的鞋带断了。」——**公主***悲思片刻之后*。「我知道。」**大帝**。「我的第二根鞋带！第一根断于我失去帝国之日：第二根断于今日。我这颗可怜的心，几时才断？」——**公主**。「鞋带、心、帝国！神秘的感应！」」

===Original===
"Nonsense, sir," interrupted Mr. Glowry. "That is not at all like the voice I heard."

"But, sir," said Scythrop, "a keyhole may be so constructed as to act like an acoustic tube, and an acoustic tube, sir, will modify sound in a very remarkable manner. Consider the construction of the ear, and the nature and causes of sound. The external part of the ear is a cartilaginous funnel."

"It wo'n't do, Scythrop. There is a girl concealed in this tower, and find her I will. There are such things as sliding panels and secret closets."—He sounded round the room with his cane, but detected no hollowness.—"I have heard, sir," he continued, "that during my absence, two years ago, you had a dumb carpenter closeted with you day after day. I did not dream that you were laying contrivances for carrying on secret intrigues. Young men will have their way: I had my way when I was a young man: but, sir, when your cousin Marionetta—"

===Chinese===
「胡说，先生，」格洛里先生打断道。「那同我听见的嗓音一点也不像。」

「可是，先生，」斯克罗普说，「钥匙孔的构造，可以恰好起到声管的作用，而声管，先生，会以极不寻常的方式改变声音。您想想耳朵的构造，想想声音的本性与成因。耳的外部，是一道软骨的漏斗。」

「行不通的，斯克罗普。这塔里藏着一个姑娘，我非把她找出来不可。世上还有滑板暗门、密室暗柜这一类东西呢。」——他用手杖满屋子敲了一遍，没敲出一段空心。——「我听说，先生，」他接着说，「两年前我不在家的时候，你同一个哑巴木匠日复一日地关在屋里。我做梦也没想到，你是在布置机关，好干秘密的勾当。年轻人自有年轻人的路数：我年轻时也自有我的路数：可是，先生，一想到你的表妹玛丽奥内塔——」

===Original===
Scythrop now saw that the affair was growing serious. To have clapped his hand upon his father's mouth, to have entreated him to be silent, would, in the first place, not have made him so; and, in the second, would have shown a dread of being overheard by somebody. His only resource, therefore, was to try to drown Mr. Glowry's voice; and, having no other subject, he continued his description of the ear, raising his voice continually as Mr. Glowry raised his.

"When your cousin Marionetta," said Mr. Glowry, "whom you profess to love—whom you profess to love, sir—"

"The internal canal of the ear," said Scythrop, "is partly bony and partly cartilaginous. This internal canal is—"

"Is actually in the house, sir; and, when you are so shortly to be—as I expect—"

"Closed at the further end by the *membrana tympani*—"

"Joined together in holy matrimony—"

"Under which is carried a branch of the fifth pair of nerves—"

"I say, sir, when you are so shortly to be married to your cousin Marionetta—"

"The *cavitas tympani*—"

===Chinese===
斯克罗普这才看出事态严重了。伸手捂住父亲的嘴、求他住口，一来根本捂不住，二来反倒显得怕被什么人听见。他唯一的办法，就是设法盖过格洛里先生的声音；手里又没有别的题目，只好接着讲他的耳朵，格洛里先生的嗓门抬一分，他的嗓门也跟着抬一分。

「一想到你的表妹玛丽奥内塔，」格洛里先生说，「你口口声声说爱她——你口口声声说爱她，先生——」

「耳朵的内部管道，」斯克罗普说，「一部分是骨的，一部分是软骨的。这内部管道——」

「——此刻就在这座宅子里，先生；而且，眼看你就快要——如我所料——」

「——在它的深端被鼓膜（*membrana tympani*）封闭——」

「——结为神圣的婚姻——」

「——鼓膜之下走着第五对神经的一个分支——」

「我是说，先生，眼看你就快要同你的表妹玛丽奥内塔结婚——」

「鼓室（*cavitas tympani*）——」

===Original===
A loud noise was heard behind the bookcase, which, to the astonishment of Mr. Glowry, opened in the middle, and the massy compartments, with all their weight of books, receding from each other in the manner of a theatrical scene, with a heavy rolling sound (which Mr. Glowry immediately recognised to be the same which had excited his curiosity,) disclosed an interior apartment, in the entrance of which stood the beautiful Stella, who, stepping forward, exclaimed, "Married! Is he going to be married? The profligate!"

"Really, madam," said Mr. Glowry, "I do not know what he is going to do, or what I am going to do, or what anyone is going to do; for all this is incomprehensible."

"I can explain it all," said Scythrop, "in a most satisfactory manner, if you will but have the goodness to leave us alone."

"Pray, sir, to which act of the tragedy of the Great Mogul does this incident belong?"

"I entreat you, my dear sir, leave us alone."

===Chinese===
书柜后面响起一声巨响，令格洛里先生大吃一惊的是：书柜从中间打开了，那庞大的两扇柜体连同满载的书，像戏台上换景一般彼此分开，带着沉重的滚动声（格洛里先生立刻认出，这正是勾起过他好奇心的那一声响），露出里面的一间密室；密室门口站着美丽的斯特拉，她抢步上前，喊道：「结婚！他要结婚了？这个浪荡子！」

「真的，女士，」格洛里先生说，「我不知道他要干什么，也不知道我要干什么，更不知道任何人要干什么；因为这一切全然无法理解。」

「我能把这一切解释清楚，」斯克罗普说，「解释得叫人心服口服，只要您好心让我们单独待一会儿。」

「请问先生，这一幕属于《莫卧儿大帝》悲剧的第几幕？」

「我求您了，亲爱的先生，让我们单独待着。」

===Original===
Stella threw herself into a chair, and burst into a tempest of tears. Scythrop sat down by her, and took her hand. She snatched her hand away, and turned her back upon him. He rose, sat down on the other side, and took her other hand. She snatched it away, and turned from him again. Scythrop continued entreating Mr. Glowry to leave them alone; but the old gentleman was obstinate, and would not go.

"I suppose, after all," said Mr. Glowry maliciously, "it is only a phenomenon in acoustics, and this young lady is a reflection of sound from concave surfaces."

Some one tapped at the door: Mr. Glowry opened it, and Mr. Hilary entered. He had been seeking Mr. Glowry, and had traced him to Scythrop's tower. He stood a few moments in silent surprise, and then addressed himself to Mr. Glowry for an explanation.

===Chinese===
斯特拉跌坐进椅子里，眼泪滂沱如骤雨。斯克罗普挨着她坐下，握住她的一只手。她把手抽回去，掉转身子背对他。他站起来，绕到另一边坐下，握住她另一只手。她又抽回去，又转了过去。斯克罗普只顾央告格洛里先生让他们单独待着；可老先生犟得很，就是不走。

「我猜想，说到底，」格洛里先生不怀好意地说，「这不过是一个声学现象，这位小姐是凹面反射出来的一道回声。」

有人敲门：格洛里先生开了门，进来的是希拉里先生。他一直在找格洛里先生，一路寻到斯克罗普的塔里。他默默惊讶地站了片刻，便向格洛里先生讨一个解释。

===Original===
"The explanation," said Mr. Glowry, "is very satisfactory. The Great Mogul has taken lodgings at Kensington, and the external part of the ear is a cartilaginous funnel."

"Mr. Glowry, that is no explanation."

"Mr. Hilary, it is all I know about the matter."

"Sir, this pleasantry is very unseasonable. I perceive that my niece is sported with in a most unjustifiable manner, and I shall see if she will be more successful in obtaining an intelligible answer." And he departed in search of Marionetta.

===Chinese===
「解释，」格洛里先生说，「再圆满没有了。莫卧儿大帝在肯辛顿赁屋而居，耳朵的外部是一道软骨的漏斗。」

「格洛里先生，这算什么解释。」

「希拉里先生，这件事，我知道的全在这儿了。」

「先生，这种玩笑开得实在不是时候。我看出来了，我的外甥女正被人以最说不过去的方式耍弄，我倒要看看，她能不能比我更问出一个明白的答案。」说完他便去找玛丽奥内塔。

===Original===
Scythrop was now in a hopeless predicament. Mr. Hilary made a hue and cry in the abbey, and summoned his wife and Marionetta to Scythrop's apartment. The ladies, not knowing what was the matter, hastened in great consternation. Mr. Toobad saw them sweeping along the corridor, and judging from their manner that the devil had manifested his wrath in some new shape, followed from pure curiosity.

Scythrop meanwhile vainly endeavoured to get rid of Mr. Glowry and to pacify Stella. The latter attempted to escape from the tower, declaring she would leave the abbey immediately, and he should never see her or hear of her more. Scythrop held her hand and detained her by force, till Mr. Hilary reappeared with Mrs. Hilary and Marionetta. Marionetta, seeing Scythrop grasping the hand of a strange beauty, fainted away in the arms of her aunt. Scythrop flew to her assistance; and Stella with redoubled anger sprang towards the door, but was intercepted in her intended flight by being caught in the arms of Mr. Toobad, who exclaimed—"Celinda!"

===Chinese===
斯克罗普这下陷入绝境了。希拉里先生在隐修院里大嚷大喊，把他太太和玛丽奥内塔都召到斯克罗普的屋里来。两位女士不知出了什么事，惶惶张张地赶来。图巴德先生看见她们沿着走廊疾行而过，从神气上判断，魔鬼又换了个新花样显怒，便纯出于好奇跟了来。

这边斯克罗普还在白费力气，一边想打发走格洛里先生，一边想安抚斯特拉。后者一心要逃出塔去，声称她马上就离开隐修院，从此不许他再见到她、再听到她的消息。斯克罗普攥住她的手，硬把她留了下来，直到希拉里先生领着希拉里太太和玛丽奥内塔重新出现。玛丽奥内塔看见斯克罗普攥着一位陌生美人的手，当场晕倒在姨妈怀里。斯克罗普飞身去救；斯特拉怒上加怒，朝门口扑去，一心要逃，却一头扑进图巴德先生的怀里，只听他喊了一声——「塞琳达！」

===Original===
"Papa!" said the young lady disconsolately.

"The devil is come among you," said Mr. Toobad, "how came my daughter here?"

"Your daughter!" exclaimed Mr. Glowry.

"Your daughter!" exclaimed Scythrop, and Mr. and Mrs. Hilary.

"Yes," said Mr. Toobad, "my daughter Celinda."

===Chinese===
「爸爸！」少女凄凄惨惨地说。

「魔鬼已来到你们中间，」图巴德先生说，「我女儿怎么会上这儿来了？」

「您的女儿！」格洛里先生喊道。

「您的女儿！」斯克罗普和希拉里先生、希拉里太太一齐喊道。

「正是，」图巴德先生说，「我的女儿塞琳达。」

===Original===
Marionetta opened her eyes and fixed them on Celinda; Celinda in return fixed hers on Marionetta. They were at remote points of the apartment. Scythrop was equidistant from both of them, central and motionless, like Muhammad's coffin.

"Mr. Glowry," said Mr. Toobad, "can you tell by what means my daughter came here?"

"I know no more," said Mr. Glowry, "than the Great Mogul."

===Chinese===
玛丽奥内塔睁开眼睛，直盯住塞琳达；塞琳达也回盯住玛丽奥内塔。她们俩一个在屋子的这头，一个在那头。斯克罗普同两人距离相等，居中不动，像穆罕默德的棺材。

「格洛里先生，」图巴德先生说，「您能告诉我，我女儿是靠什么法子到这儿来的吗？」

「我知道的，」格洛里先生说，「不比莫卧儿大帝多。」

===Original===
"Mr. Scythrop," said Mr. Toobad, "how came my daughter here?"

"I did not know, sir, that the lady was your daughter."

"But how came she here?"

"By spontaneous locomotion," said Scythrop, sullenly.

"Celinda," said Mr. Toobad, "what does all this mean?"

"I really do not know, sir."

===Chinese===
「斯克罗普先生，」图巴德先生说，「我女儿怎么会上这儿来？」

「我先前不知道，先生，这位小姐是您的女儿。」

「可她是怎么来的？」

「靠自发运动，」斯克罗普悻悻地说。

「塞琳达，」图巴德先生说，「这都是怎么回事？」

「我真的不知道，先生。」

===Original===
"This is most unaccountable. When I told you in London that I had chosen a husband for you, you thought proper to run away from him; and now, to all appearance, you have run away to him."

"How, sir! was that your choice?"

"Precisely; and if he is yours too we shall be both of a mind, for the first time in our lives."

"He is not my choice, sir. This lady has a prior claim: I renounce him."

"And I renounce him," said Marionetta.

===Chinese===
「这真是莫名其妙。我在伦敦告诉你，我已替你挑好了丈夫，你偏偏要从他跟前逃走；如今，看这光景，你竟逃到他跟前来了。」

「怎么，先生！您挑的就是他？」

「正是；他若也是你挑的，我们爷俩这可是生平头一回流想到一处去。」

「他不是我挑的，先生。这位小姐有先来的名分：我弃了他。」

「我也弃了他，」玛丽奥内塔说。

===Original===
Scythrop knew not what to do. He could not attempt to conciliate the one without irreparably offending the other; and he was so fond of both, that the idea of depriving himself forever of the society of either was intolerable to him: he therefore retreated into his stronghold, mystery; maintained an impenetrable silence; and contented himself with stealing occasionally a deprecating glance at each of the objects of his idolatry. Mr. Toobad and Mr. Hilary, in the meantime, were each insisting on an explanation from Mr. Glowry, who they thought had been playing a double game on this occasion. Mr. Glowry was vainly endeavouring to persuade them of his innocence in the whole transaction. Mrs. Hilary was endeavouring to mediate between her husband and brother. The Honourable Mr. Listless, the Reverend Mr. Larynx, Mr. Flosky, Mr. Asterias, and Aquarius, were attracted by the tumult to the scene of action, and were appealed to severally and conjointly by the respective disputants. Multitudinous questions, and answers *en masse*, composed a *charivari*, to which the genius of Rossini alone could have given a suitable accompaniment, and which was only terminated by Mrs. Hilary and Mr. Toobad retreating with the captive damsels. The whole party followed, with the exception of Scythrop, who threw himself into his armchair, crossed his left foot over his right knee, placed the hollow of his left hand on the interior ankle of his left leg, rested his right elbow on the elbow of the chair, placed the ball of his right thumb against his right temple, curved the forefinger along the upper part of his forehead, rested the point of the middle finger on the bridge of his nose, and the points of the two others on the lower part of the palm, fixed his eyes intently on the veins in the back of his left hand, and sat in this position like the immoveable Theseus, who, as is well known to many who have not been at college, and to some few who have, *sedet, oeternumque sedebit*.13 We hope the admirers of the *minutiae* in poetry and romance will appreciate this accurate description of a pensive attitude.

===Chinese===
斯克罗普不知如何是好。想讨好这一个，就免不了无可挽回地开罪那一个；而他对两个都爱得厉害，一想到从此永远失去任何一位的陪伴，他就受不了：于是他退进他的堡垒——神秘里去；保持一种深不可测的沉默；只满足于不时向他的两位偶像各偷觑一眼，眼色里带着告罪。与此同时，图巴德先生和希拉里先生各揪着格洛里先生要解释，两人都疑心他在这件事里耍了两面派。格洛里先生正白费唇舌，想让他们相信他自始至终清白无辜。希拉里太太在丈夫和兄长之间居中调停。尊贵的李斯特里斯先生、拉林克斯牧师、弗洛斯基先生、阿斯特里亚斯先生和阿夸里乌斯，都被这阵喧闹引到出事的现场，又被吵架的各方分别拉拢、共同央告。成堆的问题和成片的回答，凑成了一场*charivari*（锅盆大合奏），唯有罗西尼的天才才配得上给它伴奏；这场合奏，直到希拉里太太和图巴德先生押着被俘的两位少女退场，方才收场。众人跟着都走了，只有斯克罗普除外：他一屁股陷进扶手椅，左脚架到右膝上，左手拢住左腿内侧脚踝，右肘支在椅子扶手上，右手拇指的指肚抵住右太阳穴，食指弯着贴上额头，中指尖点住鼻梁，另外两指的指尖抵在掌心下缘，眼睛死死盯住左手背上的青筋，就这样坐着，像那尊恒坐不动、且将永坐的忒修斯——此像素为许多没上过大学的人所熟知，也为上过大学的寥寥数人所知，*sedet, oeternumque sedebit*（他坐着，且将永远坐着）。13 我们希望，诗与传奇中*细节*（*minutiae*）的欣赏者们，会领赏这一幅沉思姿态的精确描绘。

## XIV / XIV

===Original===
Scythrop was still in this position when Raven entered to announce that dinner was on table.

"I cannot come," said Scythrop.

Raven sighed. "Something is the matter," said Raven: "but man is born to trouble."

"Leave me," said Scythrop: "go, and croak elsewhere."

"Thus it is," said Raven. "Five-and-twenty years have I lived in Nightmare Abbey, and now all the reward of my affection is—Go, and croak elsewhere. I have danced you on my knee, and fed you with marrow."

"Good Raven," said Scythrop, "I entreat you to leave me."

"Shall I bring your dinner here?" said Raven. "A boiled fowl and a glass of Madeira are prescribed by the faculty in cases of low spirits. But you had better join the party: it is very much reduced already."

===Chinese===
斯克罗普还这么坐着，雷文进来票报：晚饭上台了。

「我来不了，」斯克罗普说。

雷文叹了口气。「一定是出事了，」雷文说：「不过，人生在世，难免烦恼。」

「退下，」斯克罗普说：「去吧，上别处呱噪去。」

「人心就是这样，」雷文说。「我在噩梦隐修院住了二十五年，如今我这一片忠心所得的全部报偿却是——去吧，上别处呱噪去。我把您放在膝上颠过，拿骨髓喂过您。」

「好雷文，」斯克罗普说，「我求您离开我。」

「要不要把晚饭给您端到这儿来？」雷文说。「情绪低落，医家规定：白煮鸡一只，马德拉酒一杯。不过您还是入席的好：席上已经空了一大半了。」

===Original===
"Reduced! how?"

"The Honourable Mr. Listless is gone. He declared that, what with family quarrels in the morning, and ghosts at night, he could get neither sleep nor peace; and that the agitation was too much for his nerves: though Mr. Glowry assured him that the ghost was only poor Crow walking in his sleep, and that the shroud and bloody turban were a sheet and a red nightcap."

"Well, sir?"

"The Reverend Mr. Larynx has been called off on duty, to marry or bury (I don't know which) some unfortunate person or persons, at Claydyke: but man is born to trouble!"

"Is that all?"

===Chinese===
「空了！怎么就空了？」

「尊贵的李斯特里斯先生走了。他说，又是清早的家庭口角，又是夜里的鬼，弄得他觉也睡不成、安也安不了；那份激动，他的神经受不住：虽然格洛里先生向他担保，那鬼不过是可怜的克罗在梦游，那裹尸布和血头巾，不过是一条床单、一顶红睡帽。」

「还有呢，先生？」

「拉林克斯牧师被公务召走了，去给克莱迪克的什么倒霉人证婚或下葬（我记不清是哪一桩）：不过，人生在世，难免烦恼！」

「就这些？」

===Original===
"No. Mr. Toobad is gone too, and a strange lady with him."

"Gone!"

"Gone. And Mr. and Mrs. Hilary, and Miss O'Carroll: they are all gone. There is nobody left but Mr. Asterias and his son, and they are going tonight."

"Then I have lost them both."

"Won't you come to dinner?"

===Chinese===
「不止。图巴德先生也走了，还有一位陌生女士同他一道。」

「走了！」

「走了。还有希拉里先生和希拉里太太，和奥卡罗尔小姐：全走了。只剩阿斯特里亚斯先生和他的儿子，他们今晚也要走。」

「那么，我把两个都丢了。」

「您不来用饭吗？」

===Original===
"No."

"Shall I bring your dinner here?"

"Yes."

"What will you have?"

"A pint of port and a pistol."14

"A pistol!"

===Chinese===
「不。」

「把饭给您端到这儿来？」

「端来。」

「您要点什么？」

「一品脱波特酒，一把手枪。14」

「手枪！」

===Original===
"And a pint of port. I will make my exit like Werther. Go. Stay. Did Miss O'Carroll say anything?"

"No."

"Did Miss Toobad say anything?"

"The strange lady? No."

"Did either of them cry?"

"No."

"What did they do?"

===Chinese===
「还有一品脱波特酒。我要像维特一样退场了。去吧。——站住。奥卡罗尔小姐说什么了吗？」

「没有。」

「图巴德小姐说什么了吗？」

「那位陌生女士？没有。」

「她们哪个哭了吗？」

「没有。」

「她们做什么了？」

===Original===
"Nothing."

"What did Mr. Toobad say?"

"He said, fifty times over, the devil was come among us."

"And they are gone?"

"Yes; and the dinner is getting cold. There is a time for everything under the sun. You may as well dine first, and be miserable afterwards."

"True, Raven. There is something in that. I will take your advice: therefore, bring me—"

"The port and the pistol?"

"No; the boiled fowl and Madeira."

===Chinese===
「什么也没做。」

「图巴德先生说什么了？」

「他说了五十遍：魔鬼已来到我们中间。」

「她们就这么走了？」

「走了；饭菜可要凉了。天底下的事，样样都有定时。您不如先吃饭，再悲苦。」

「有理，雷文。这话有点意思。我听你的：那么，给我端——」

「波特酒和手枪？」

「不；白煮鸡和马德拉酒。」

===Original===
Scythrop had dined, and was sipping his Madeira alone, immersed in melancholy musing, when Mr. Glowry entered, followed by Raven, who, having placed an additional glass and set a chair for Mr. Glowry, withdrew. Mr. Glowry sat down opposite Scythrop. After a pause, during which each filled and drank in silence, Mr. Glowry said, "So, sir, you have played your cards well. I proposed Miss Toobad to you: you refused her. Mr. Toobad proposed you to her: she refused you. You fell in love with Marionetta, and were going to poison yourself, because, from pure fatherly regard to your temporal interests, I withheld my consent. When, at length, I offered you my consent, you told me I was too precipitate. And, after all, I find you and Miss Toobad living together in the same tower, and behaving in every respect like two plighted lovers. Now, sir, if there be any rational solution of all this absurdity, I shall be very much obliged to you for a small glimmering of information."

===Chinese===
斯克罗普用过饭，正独自啜着马德拉酒，沉浸在忧郁的沉思里，格洛里先生进来了，雷文跟着，添上一副杯盏、给格洛里先生摆好椅子，便退了出去。格洛里先生在斯克罗普对面坐下。两人各自斟酒，默默对饮，停了一阵，格洛里先生开口了：「好啊，先生，您这手牌打得漂亮。我向您提议图巴德小姐：您回绝了她。图巴德先生向她提议您：她回绝了您。您爱上了玛丽奥内塔，又要服毒自尽，只因我出于纯然的父爱、为您的现世利益着想，不肯点头。等我终于点了头，您又嫌我太性急。而到头来，我竟发现您同图巴德小姐同居一座塔里，一举一动都像一对订了婚盟的恋人。如今，先生，这一切荒唐事若还有什么理性的解答，就请您赏我一线微光的信息，我感激不尽。」

===Original===
"The solution, sir, is of little moment; but I will leave it in writing for your satisfaction. The crisis of my fate is come: the world is a stage, and my direction is *exit*."

"Do not talk so, sir;—do not talk so, Scythrop. What would you have?"

"I would have my love."

"And pray, sir, who is your love?"

"Celinda—Marionetta—either—both."

===Chinese===
「解答，先生，无关紧要；但我将把它笔录下来，留给您过目。我命运的危机到了：世界是一座舞台，我的方向是退场（*exit*）。」

「别这么说，先生；——别这么说，斯克罗普。您还想要什么？」

「我要我的爱人。」

「请问先生，谁是您的爱人？」

「塞琳达——玛丽奥内塔——随便哪个——两个都要。」

===Original===
"Both! That may do very well in a German tragedy; and the Great Mogul might have found it very feasible in his lodgings at Kensington; but it will not do in Lincolnshire. Will you have Miss Toobad?"

"Yes."

"And renounce Marionetta?"

"No."

"But you must renounce one."

"I cannot."

"And you cannot have both. What is to be done?"

===Chinese===
「两个都要！这在一个德国悲剧里也许行得通；莫卧儿大帝在他肯辛顿的寓所里，或许也觉得切实可行；可在林肯郡行不通。您到底要不要图巴德小姐？」

「要。」

「那就舍弃玛丽奥内塔？」

「不。」

「可您必须舍弃一个。」

「我做不到。」

「而您不能两个都要。怎么办呢？」

===Original===
"I must shoot myself."

"Don't talk so, Scythrop. Be rational, my dear Scythrop. Consider, and make a cool, calm choice, and I will exert myself in your behalf."

"Why should I choose, sir? Both have renounced *me*: I have no hope of either."

"Tell me which you will have, and I will plead your cause irresistibly."

"Well, sir—I will have—no, sir, I cannot renounce either. I cannot choose either. I am doomed to be the victim of eternal disappointments; and I have no resource but a pistol."

===Chinese===
「我只好开枪打死自己。」

「别这么说，斯克罗普。理智些，我亲爱的斯克罗普。想一想，冷冷静静挑定一个，我自会为您出力。」

「何必我挑，先生？两个都弃了*我*：我对哪一个都没有指望了。」

「告诉我您要哪一个，我保您马到成功。」

「好吧，先生——我要——不，先生，哪一个我也舍弃不下。哪一个我也挑不出来。我命中注定做永远的失望的牺牲品；我除了一把手枪，别无出路。」

===Original===
"Scythrop—Scythrop;—if one of them should come to you—what then?"

"That, sir, might alter the case: but that cannot be."

"It can be, Scythrop; it will be: I promise you it will be. Have but a little patience—but a week's patience; and it shall be."

"A week, sir, is an age: but, to oblige you, as a last act of filial duty, I will live another week. It is now Thursday evening, twenty-five minutes past seven. At this hour and minute, on Thursday next, love and fate shall smile on me, or I will drink my last pint of port in this world."

Mr. Glowry ordered his travelling chariot, and departed from the abbey.

===Chinese===
「斯克罗普——斯克罗普；——倘若她们中的一个回到您身边——那又如何？」

「那样的话，先生，事情或许就变了：可那不可能。」

「可能，斯克罗普；一定会：我担保一定。您只消稍有一点耐心——只消一个星期的耐心；就一定成。」

「一个星期，先生，就是一个世纪：不过，为了让您高兴，作为我孝道的最后一举，我再活一个星期。今夜是星期四晚上，七点二十五分。下星期四的这个时辰、这一分，爱情与命运若不对我一展笑颜，我就喝掉我在这个世上的最后一品脱波特酒。」

格洛里先生吩咐备好旅行马车，离开隐修院走了。

## XV / XV

===Original===
The day after Mr. Glowry's departure was one of incessant rain, and Scythrop repented of the promise he had given. The next day was one of bright sunshine: he sat on the terrace, read a tragedy of Sophocles, and was not sorry, when Raven announced dinner, to find himself alive. On the third evening, the wind blew, and the rain beat, and the owl flapped against his windows; and he put a new flint in his pistol. On the fourth day, the sun shone again; and he locked the pistol up in a drawer, where he left it undisturbed, till the morning of the eventful Thursday, when he ascended the turret with a telescope, and spied anxiously along the road that crossed the fens from Claydyke: but nothing appeared on it. He watched in this manner from ten a.m. till Raven summoned him to dinner at five; when he stationed Crow at the telescope, and descended to his own funeral-feast. He left open the communications between the tower and turret, and called aloud at intervals to Crow—"Crow, Crow, is anything coming?" Crow answered, "The wind blows, and the windmills turn, but I see nothing coming;" and, at every answer, Scythrop found the necessity of raising his spirits with a bumper. After dinner, he gave Raven his watch to set by the abbey clock. Raven brought it, Scythrop placed it on the table, and Raven departed. Scythrop called again to Crow; and Crow, who had fallen asleep, answered mechanically, "I see nothing coming." Scythrop laid his pistol between his watch and his bottle. The hour-hand passed the VII—the minute-hand moved on;—it was within three minutes of the appointed time. Scythrop called again to Crow: Crow answered as before. Scythrop rang the bell: Raven appeared.

===Chinese===
格洛里先生走后第一天，雨下个不停，斯克罗普对自己许下的诺言后悔起来。第二天阳光明媚：他坐在露台上，读一部索福克勒斯的悲剧，到雷文票报开饭时，发觉自己还活着，倒也不觉得遗憾。第三天晚上，风刮雨打，猫头鹰扑打着他的窗子；他给手枪换上了一枚新燧石。第四天，太阳又出来了；他把手枪锁进抽屉，任它安安静静地躺着，直到那个性命攸关的星期四早晨：他带着望远镜登上角楼，焦灼地沿那条从克莱迪克穿越沼泽的大路张望：路上却什么也没有。他就这么从上午十点一直守到五点雷文唤他用饭；然后他让克罗代守望远镜，自己下来赴他自己的丧宴。他把塔与角楼之间的通道一路敞开，隔一阵就朝克罗高喊——「克罗，克罗，有什么来了吗？」克罗答道：「风在刮，风车在转，可我什么也没望见。」每得一次这样的回答，斯克罗普就觉得有必要举一杯满杯给自己提神。饭后，他把表交给雷文，叫他照隐修院的大钟拨准。雷文把表送来，斯克罗普把它摆在桌上，雷文退了出去。斯克罗普又喊克罗；克罗睡着了，机械地答道：「我什么也没望见。」斯克罗普把手枪摆在表和酒瓶中间。时针走过了 VII——分针继续前移；——离约定的时刻只剩三分钟了。斯克罗普又喊克罗：克罗照旧回答。斯克罗普摇铃：雷文出现了。

===Original===
"Raven," said Scythrop, "the clock is too fast."

"No, indeed," said Raven, who knew nothing of Scythrop's intentions; "if anything, it is too slow."

"Villain!" said Scythrop, pointing the pistol at him; "it is too fast."

"Yes—yes—too fast, I meant," said Raven, in manifest fear.

"How much too fast?" said Scythrop.

"As much as you please," said Raven.

"How much, I say?" said Scythrop, pointing the pistol again.

"An hour, a full hour, sir," said the terrified butler.

"Put back my watch," said Scythrop.

===Chinese===
「雷文，」斯克罗普说，「大钟太快了。」

「没有的事，」雷文说——他对斯克罗普的意图一无所知——「要说有，也是太慢了。」

「恶棍！」斯克罗普说，拿手枪指着他；「它太快了。」

「是——是——太快，我是说太快，」雷文说，显然怕极了。

「快多少？」斯克罗普说。

「随您说多少，」雷文说。

「我问你，快多少？」斯克罗普说，又拿枪指了指。

「一个钟头，整整一个钟头，先生，」吓坏了的老管家说。

「把我的表拨回去，」斯克罗普说。

===Original===
Raven, with trembling hand, was putting back the watch, when the rattle of wheels was heard in the court; and Scythrop, springing down the stairs by three steps together, was at the door in sufficient time to have handed either of the young ladies from the carriage, if she had happened to be in it; but Mr. Glowry was alone.

"I rejoice to see you," said Mr. Glowry; "I was fearful of being too late, for I waited till the last moment in the hope of accomplishing my promise; but all my endeavours have been vain, as these letters will show."

Scythrop impatiently broke the seals. The contents were these:

===Chinese===
雷文哆哆嗦嗦正拨着表，院里传来车轮声；斯克罗普三级并作一步蹦下楼梯，赶到门口，时间绰绰有余，本可以搀两位小姐中随便哪一位下车——倘若车里碰巧有哪一位的话；可是格洛里先生独自一个。

「见到您真叫我欢喜，」格洛里先生说；「我一直怕来得太迟，因为我抱着兑现诺言的希望，直等到最后一刻；怎奈我的一切奔走全归无效，这几封信便可为证。」

斯克罗普迫不及待地拆开封漆。信的内容如下：

===Original===
> Almost a stranger in England, I fled from parental tyranny, and the dread of an arbitrary marriage, to the protection of a stranger and a philosopher, whom I expected to find something better than, or at least something different from, the rest of his worthless species. Could I, after what has occurred, have expected nothing more from you than the commonplace impertinence of sending your father to treat with me, and with mine, for me? I should be a little moved in your favour, if I could believe you capable of carrying into effect the resolutions which your father says you have taken, in the event of my proving inflexible; though I doubt not you will execute them, as far as relates to the pint of wine, twice over, at least. I wish you much happiness with Miss O'Carroll. I shall always cherish a grateful recollection of Nightmare Abbey, for having been the means of introducing me to a true transcendentalist; and, though he is a little older than myself, which is all one in Germany, I shall very soon have the pleasure of subscribing myself

> Celinda Flosky

===Chinese===
> 我在英国举目无亲，为了逃避父辈的专横、逃避一场任意摆布的婚姻，投奔到一位陌生人、一位哲学家的庇护之下，满以为他总该比他那一文不值的族类的其余成员强些，或者至少有所不同。发生了这一切之后，我指望您给我的，难道竟只是打发令尊来同我、同我的家人，替我议婚这等俗不可耐的唐突之举吗？倘若我能相信您真有能力实行令尊所说的、万一我执意不从您便要实行的那些决意，我倒该为您稍稍动一动心；不过我毫不怀疑您会执行它们的——至少就那一品脱酒而论，您会执行上两遍。祝您同奥卡罗尔小姐百年好合。我将永远怀着感激之情记着噩梦隐修院，因为它使我得以结识一位真正的先验论者；虽然他年纪比我大了些——这在德国根本不算一回事——我不久便将怀着愉快，署名为

> 塞琳达·弗洛斯基

===Original===
> I hope, my dear cousin, that you will not be angry with me, but that you will always think of me as a sincere friend, who will always feel interested in your welfare; I am sure you love Miss Toobad much better than me, and I wish you much happiness with her. Mr. Listless assures me that people do not kill themselves for love nowadays, though it is still the fashion to talk about it. I shall, in a very short time, change my name and situation, and shall always be happy to see you in Berkeley Square, when, to the unalterable designation of your affectionate cousin, I shall subjoin the signature of

> Marionetta Listless

===Chinese===
> 我希望，亲爱的表哥，您不要生我的气，而要永远把我当作一个真诚的朋友，一个永远关切您福祉的人；我确信您爱图巴德小姐远胜于爱我，祝您同她幸福美满。李斯特里斯先生向我担保，如今人们不再为爱情自寻短见了，尽管把这话挂在嘴上仍旧时兴。用不了多久，我就要改姓换位了；将来您到伯克利广场，我永远欢迎，届时在您亲爱的表妹这一永不变更的称谓之外，我将在下面添上签名

> 玛丽奥内塔·李斯特里斯

===Original===
Scythrop tore both the letters to atoms, and railed in good set terms against the fickleness of women.

"Calm yourself, my dear Scythrop," said Mr. Glowry; "there are yet maidens in England."

"Very true, sir," said Scythrop.

"And the next time," said Mr. Glowry, "have but one string to your bow."

"Very good advice, sir," said Scythrop.

===Chinese===
斯克罗普把两封信都撕成了齑粉，用一篇规规矩矩的成套文章，痛骂了一通女人的善变。

「平静些，我亲爱的斯克罗普，」格洛里先生说；「英格兰还有的是少女。」

「很对，先生，」斯克罗普说。

「下回，」格洛里先生说，「弓上只拴一根弦吧。」

「很好的忠告，先生，」斯克罗普说。

===Original===
"And, besides," said Mr. Glowry, "the fatal time is past, for it is now almost eight."

"Then that villain, Raven," said Scythrop, "deceived me when he said that the clock was too fast; but, as you observe very justly, the time has gone by, and I have just reflected that these repeated crosses in love qualify me to take a very advanced degree in misanthropy; and there is, therefore, good hope that I may make a figure in the world. But I shall ring for the rascal Raven, and admonish him."

Raven appeared. Scythrop looked at him very fiercely two or three minutes; and Raven, still remembering the pistol, stood quaking in mute apprehension, till Scythrop, pointing significantly towards the dining-room, said, "Bring some Madeira."

===Chinese===
「再说，」格洛里先生说，「致命的时辰已经过去了，现在已经快八点。」

「那么那个恶棍雷文，」斯克罗普说，「说大钟太快，是骗我了；不过，正如您非常公正地指出的，时辰已经过去；而且我刚想明白，这一而再、再而三的情场挫折，够资格让我在厌世学上拿一个很高的学位了；因此大有希望，我日后还能在世上崭露头角。不过我要摇铃把那个无赖雷文叫来，训诫他一番。」

雷文出现了。斯克罗普凶巴巴地盯了他两三分钟；雷文还惦记着手枪，站在那里发抖，一声不敢吭，直到斯克罗普意味深长地朝饭厅一指，说：「拿马德拉酒来。」

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. A corruption of Filosky, quasi *φιλοσχιος*, a lover, or sectator, of shadows. ↩︎

2. See Forsyth's *Principles of Moral Science*. ↩︎

3. We are not masters of the whole vocabulary. See any novel by any literary lady. ↩︎

===Chinese===
1. Flosky 一名系 Filosky 之讹变，仿佛希腊语 *φιλοσχιος*，意即「影之爱好者」，或曰「影之信徒」。 ↩︎

2. 参见福赛斯《道德科学原理》（*Principles of Moral Science*）。 ↩︎

3. 我们并非全套词汇的主人。请参阅任何一位文学女士的任何一部小说。 ↩︎

===Original===
4. Ahrimanes, in the Persian mythology, is the evil power, the prince of the kingdom of darkness. He is the rival of Oromazes, the prince of the kingdom of light. These two powers have divided and equal dominion. Sometimes one of the two has a temporary supremacy.—According to Mr. Toobad, the present period would be the reign of Ahrimanes. Lord Byron seems to be of the same opinion, by the use he has made of Ahrimanes in *Manfred*; where the great Alastor, or *Καχος Δαιμων*, of Persia, is hailed king of the world by the Nemesis of Greece, in concert with three of the Scandinavian Valkyrae, under the name of the Destinies; the astrological spirits of the alchemists of the middle ages; an elemental witch, transplanted from Denmark to the Alps; and a chorus of Dr. Faustus's devils, who come in the last act for a soul. It is difficult to conceive where this heterogeneous mythological company could have originally met, except at a table d'hôte, like the six kings in *Candide*. ↩︎

===Chinese===
4. 阿里曼（Ahrimanes），在波斯神话中，是邪恶的权能，是黑暗之国的君王。他是光明之国君王奥罗马泽斯（Oromazes）的敌手。这两种权能分治天下，权力相等。有时二者之一暂居上风。——照图巴德先生的说法，当今之世，便是阿里曼的统治期。拜伦勋爵似乎也持同一见解，证据是他把阿里曼用进了《曼弗雷德》（*Manfred*）：剧中，波斯那位伟大的阿拉斯托（Alastor），即 *Καχος Δαιμων*，被希腊的涅墨西斯会同三名斯堪的纳维亚的女武神（瓦尔基里）——以「命运诸神」之名——齐声拥立为世界之王；还有中世纪炼金术士们的星占之灵；一名从丹麦移植到阿尔卑斯山的元素女巫；以及浮士德博士的一队魔鬼——他们在最后一幕里前来收取一个灵魂。很难设想这群成分驳杂的神话角色当初能在何处聚齐，除非是在一张客饭桌（*table d'hôte*）上，像《老实人》（*Candide*）里的那六位国王。 ↩︎

===Original===
5. "**Pension**. Pay given to a slave of state for treason to his country." —Johnson's *Dictionary* ↩︎

===Chinese===
5. 「**年金**（**Pension**）：付给国家之奴、作为其叛国之酬的俸给。」——约翰逊《字典》（*Dictionary*） ↩︎

===Original===
6. See Denys Montfort: *Histoire Naturelle des Mollusques; Vues Générales*, pp. 37, 38. (P.) The second half of this speech by Mr. Asterias and the opening sentence of his previous speech are a paraphrase from Montfort, pp. 37–9. ↩︎

===Chinese===
6. 参见德尼·蒙福：《软体动物自然史：总论》（*Histoire Naturelle des Mollusques; Vues Générales*），页 37、38。（P.）阿斯特里亚斯先生本段演说的后半与其前一段演说的开头一句，是对蒙福页 37–9 文字的转述。 ↩︎

===Original===
7. There must be some mistake in this, for the whole honourable band of gentlemen-pensioners has resolved unanimously, that Mr. Burke was a very sublime person, particularly after he had prostituted his own soul, and betrayed his country and mankind, for £1,200 a year: yet he does not appear to have been a very terrible personage, and certainly went off with a very small portion of human respect, though he contrived to excite, in a great degree, the astonishment of all honest men. Our immaculate laureate (who gives us to understand that, if he had not been purified by holy matrimony into a mystical type, he would have died a virgin), is another sublime gentleman of the same genus: he very much astonished some persons when he sold his birthright for a pot of sack; but not even his *Sosia* has a grain of respect for him, though, doubtless, he thinks his name very terrible to the enemy, when he flourishes his criticopoeticopolitical tomahawk, and sets up his Indian yell for the blood of his old friends: but, at best, he is a mere political scarecrow, a man of straw, ridiculous to all who know of what materials he is made; and to none more so, than to those who have stuffed him, and set him up, as the Priapus of the garden of the golden apples of corruption. ↩︎

===Chinese===
7. 此事想必有些差池，因为全体可敬的「领年金绅士」队伍已经一致议决：伯克先生是一位非常崇高的人物，尤其是在他以每年 1,200 镑的价钱出卖自己的灵魂、背叛他的国家与人类之后：然而他似乎算不得一个怎样可怕的角色，而且带走的人间敬意无疑少得可怜，尽管他确实在很大程度上唤起了所有正人君子的惊诧。我们无可指摘的桂冠诗人（他让我们明白：若不是经神圣婚姻净化成一位神秘典型，他本会以童贞之身而死）是同属此类的另一位崇高绅士：他拿长子名分换一壶雪利酒的时候，着实叫某些人大吃一惊；可连他的*索西亚*（*Sosia*）也对他没有一粒敬意——尽管毫无疑问，每当他抡起那柄批评诗歌政治三合一的战斧、发出讨旧友鲜血的印第安战吼时，他自己必以为他的名字令敌人闻风丧胆：但说到底，他不过是一个政治稻草人、一个草扎的人，凡知道他是用什么材料扎成的，无不觉得可笑；而笑得最响的，莫过于那些把他填扎起来、把他竖起来、充作腐败金苹果园中普里阿普斯（Priapus）的人。 ↩︎
